### [1] [Analytic Bootstrap Solution for Quantum Mechanics on a Circle](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36031)
*David Berenstein,Upamanyu Moitra*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces a quantum mechanical bootstrap method to solve the Schrödinger equation for a particle on a circle, yielding analytical solutions via generalized continued fractions; it enables efficient computation of the density of states, wave functions, and quasimomentum, and demonstrates high-resolution spectral analysis—even for exponentially narrow bands—as illustrated with the Mathieu equation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a robust, analytical, and numerically stable method for solving periodic quantum systems—particularly where conventional perturbative or numerical approaches fail to resolve exponentially small spectral features.

Method: Quantum mechanical bootstrap method: imposing exponential decay of Fourier coefficients of the measure leads to recursion relations solved via generalized continued fractions; analytical continuation in energy and couplings is used to compute observables including density of states, wave functions, and quasimomentum.

Result: An exact analytical representation of eigenenergies and eigenfunctions as functions of couplings and energy; a single-variable inequality test for physicality of the density of states; explicit reconstruction of wave functions and quasimomentum dispersion; successful resolution of exponentially narrow bands in the Mathieu equation spectrum.

Conclusion: The bootstrap method provides a powerful, non-perturbative, and analytically tractable framework for integrable and near-integrable quantum systems on compact domains, with direct applicability to band structure analysis and spectral statistics.

Abstract: We solve the Schrödinger equation for a particle in the circle with a quantum mechanical bootstrap method. The main idea is that in physical situations one needs solutions of the recursion relations for the Fourier coefficients of the measure that vanish exponentially fast. We show that this results in a (generalized) continued fraction representation of the solution. The solution is an analytical function of the couplings and the energy. We also show how one this method directly computes the density of states and checking that the answer is physical is reduced to checking an inequality for a single variable. We also show how to reconstruct the wave function and how to extract the quasimomentum of the particle as a function of the energy. We illustrate our solution in the simplest case of the Mathieu equation, where we show that the method can easily resolve exponentially small bands in the band spectrum.

</details>


### [2] [The Art of Orbifolding by Huge Non-Abelian Groups](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36024)
*Jan Albert,Masaki Okada,Yuji Tachikawa*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes torus partition functions for orbifolds of 2D chiral fermions by large non-Abelian groups (including the Conway group), resolving previously unsolved conceptual and computational challenges; although motivated by identifying the periodicity element of topological modular forms (TMF), the studied orbifolds were found not to realize it.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify the periodicity element of topological modular forms (TMF) via orbifold constructions of chiral fermions, necessitating development of new techniques for computing torus partition functions under huge non-Abelian symmetries.

Method: Systematic computation of torus partition functions for orbifolds of two-dimensional chiral fermions by large non-Abelian groups—including the Conway group—addressing unresolved foundational and technical issues in conformal field theory and orbifold construction.

Result: Successful resolution of major theoretical and practical obstacles in computing such partition functions; explicit construction and analysis of these orbifolds; exclusion of the considered models as candidates for the TMF periodicity element.

Conclusion: The methods developed are broadly applicable to non-Abelian orbifolds in CFT, but the specific Conway-group-based chiral fermion orbifolds do not realize the TMF periodicity element.

Abstract: We compute torus partition functions of orbifolds of two-dimensional chiral fermions by huge non-Abelian groups involving the Conway group, one of the sporadic finite simple groups. This seemingly standard problem turns out to contain, somewhat unexpectedly, multiple major issues hitherto unresolved, both at the level of the principle and at the level of practice. We describe in detail how they can be overcome. Although the methods described in this paper are generally applicable, the choice of the particular orbifolds we consider in this paper was motivated by the search for the periodicity element of topological modular forms. We will provide a brief introduction to this question, to explain why all the techniques in this paper had to be developed. Unfortunately, the orbifolds we considered turned out not to be the periodicity element.

</details>


### [3] [Instanton Structure from 325-Loop 3D $ϕ^3$ Perturbation Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36025)
*Ian Moult,Bo Wang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Introduces an annealed importance sampling method on tropical curve moduli space to compute high-order perturbative coefficients in 3D massive φ³ theory up to 325 loops, enabling precise extraction of non-perturbative instanton data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the exponential weight-variance barrier of standard Feynman diagrammatic expansions and access high-order perturbative behavior for probing non-perturbative phenomena (e.g., instantons) in realistic QFTs.

Method: Leverages global sampling on the moduli space of tropical curves and introduces an annealed importance sampling framework tailored for tropical field theory.

Result: Computed two-point function perturbative series in 3D massive φ³ theory through 325 loops; extracted instanton action (0.03% precision), collective-coordinate exponent, one-loop determinant, and instanton fluctuation corrections up to four loops — all in remarkable agreement with semiclassical results.

Conclusion: The tropical/annealed sampling framework enables quantitatively reliable high-loop calculations in interacting QFTs, opening a new pathway for rigorous non-perturbative analysis beyond simplified models.

Abstract: The high order behavior of perturbation theory provides insight into non-perturbative phenomenon in quantum field theory. While extensively studied in simplified models, high order behavior is challenging to access in more realistic field theories due to the complexity of the standard Feynman diagrammatic expansion. Building on recent progress in global sampling on the moduli space of tropical curves, we introduce an annealed importance sampling framework for tropical field theory. Our approach overcomes the exponential weight-variance barrier, allowing precise numerical calculations to high orders. We apply our technique to compute the perturbative series for the two-point function in the massive three-dimensional $\phi^3$ theory through 325 loops. These coefficients determine the instanton action in $\phi^3$ theory to 0.03\% precision, confirm the collective-coordinate exponent, and extract the one-loop determinant and the instanton fluctuation corrections through four loops, in remarkable agreement with direct semiclassical calculations. Our approach is applicable to a variety of related field theories, facilitating the quantitative exploration of their high loop behavior.

</details>


### [4] [Cusp regularization with spin structures and gravitational anomalies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36028)
*Elisa Tabor,Cynthia Yan*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper addresses divergences in the sum over SL(2,Z) black hole geometries due to a cusp accumulation point, extends cusp regularization to spin-structure-dependent sums, shows cancellation of leading divergences via gravitational anomaly-induced phase cancellations, and computes the associated spin-structure-dependent anomaly phases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The sum over SL(2,Z) black hole geometries diverges due to an accumulation point at a cusped geometry; the paper aims to regularize this divergence while respecting boundary spin structure and incorporating gravitational anomalies.

Method: Cusp regularization is generalized to fixed boundary spin structures; modular invariance, spectral asymmetry, and gravitational anomaly contributions (via phase factors from chiral central charges c_L, c_R) are used to analyze cancellations across even spin structures.

Result: Including the cusp as a counterterm cancels the leading divergence for all three even spin structures when c_L = c_R; the required subtraction is universal and equals 2/3 of the bosonic counterterm; gravitational anomalies induce destructive interference among black hole contributions, eliminating the leading cusp divergence except at discrete values of c_L − c_R (0, ±24 for pure gravity; 0, ±12 for N=(1,1) supergravity); explicit anomaly phases depending on spin structure are derived.

Conclusion: Gravitational anomalies play a crucial role in regulating the black hole partition function by enforcing spin-structure-dependent cancellations; the cusp divergence is generically removed except at anomaly-saturated central charge differences, affirming consistency of quantum gravity with modular invariance and fermionic boundary conditions.

Abstract: The family of $SL(2,\mathbb Z)$ black holes has an accumulation point corresponding to a cusped geometry, which causes the sum over black holes to diverge. We extend cusp regularization to the sum over geometries compatible with a fixed boundary spin structure. For $c_L=c_R$, including the cusp as a counterterm cancels the leading divergence for each of the three even boundary spin structures. The required subtraction is the same in all three sectors and is two thirds of the bosonic one. We further show that gravitational anomalies cause cancellations between black hole contributions, removing the leading cusp divergence except at $c_L-c_R=0,\pm24$ in pure gravity and $c_L-c_R=0,\pm12$ in $\mathcal N=(1,1)$ supergravity. We also derive the spin structure dependent anomaly phases responsible for these cancellations.

</details>


### [5] [Cosmology of the Baby Universe in Coupled SYK](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36032)
*Andrew Sontag*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies the baby universe in the coupled SYK model realizing AS² geometry, using finite-N,p combinatorial methods to extend chord diagram rules; it identifies a persistent first-order thermal-to-AS² phase transition controlled by heavy particle mass and coupling ratio, and confirms a big bang/crunch cosmology via Lorentzian evolution yielding a cosine scale factor.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the quantum gravitational structure of the baby universe in the coupled SYK realization of AS² geometry, particularly beyond the large-N, large-p (small-λ) limit where standard chord diagram techniques break down.

Method: Develops exact finite-N,p combinatorial arguments for traces of Majorana operators to obtain the finite-λ completion of chord diagram rules; analyzes thermal partition functions and phase structure; performs Lorentzian time evolution in the dual bulk description to extract cosmological scale factor.

Result: A first-order phase transition between thermal AdS and AS² phases persists in the zero-temperature (β→∞) limit, governed by heavy particle mass and SYK/Maldacena-Qi coupling ratio; Lorentzian evolution yields a cosine scale factor, confirming a singular big bang / big crunch cosmology in the baby universe.

Conclusion: The coupled SYK model captures key features of 2D cosmology—including phase structure and singularity resolution—via exact finite-N,p techniques, validating its utility as a solvable quantum gravity toy model for baby universes.

Abstract: We analyze the structure of the baby universe which arises in the coupled SYK realization of the AS$^2$ geometry. To do this, we develop and leverage combinatorial arguments about traces of many Majoranas which remain valid at finite $N$ and $p$ and thus furnish the finite $\lambda$ completion of the chord rules found in [1]. We find that there is a first order phase transition which persists in the $\beta\rightarrow\infty$ limit between the thermal AdS and AS$^2$ phases parameterized by the mass of the heavy particle insertion and the ratio between the SYK and Maldacena-Qi coupling constants. We then explore Lorentzian time evolution in the baby universe and find a cosine scale factor confirming the big bang / big crunch nature of the bulk cosmology.

</details>


### [6] [BPS Chaos and Fortuity in Higgs Branches I: The Gepner Point](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36035)
*Micha Berkooz,Trivko Kukolj,Ohad Mamroud,Josef Seitz*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies BPS states in Calabi-Yau compactifications of type II string theory using quiver quantum mechanics, distinguishing monotone (multi-center supergravity) and pure-Higgs (fortuitous, R-charge localized) states via cohomological structure; it proposes Berry/Chern curvature as a probe for BPS chaos and computes it via 2D CFT localization in the Gepner model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the statistical and geometric structure of BPS state degeneracies—particularly their resemblance to black hole entropy—and to identify robust probes of quantum chaos within supersymmetric string vacua.

Method: Uses quiver quantum mechanics with Higgs branch geometry as a complete intersection; classifies BPS states via cohomological decomposition (monotone vs. pure-Higgs); employs 2D CFT localization techniques in the Gepner model to compute Berry/Chern curvature and analyze its Random Matrix Theory (RMT) statistics and deviations.

Result: Identifies a precise cohomological split between monotone and pure-Higgs states; establishes Berry/Chern curvature as a sensitive diagnostic of BPS chaos; provides explicit curvature computation and RMT analysis in the Gepner model, revealing universal and non-universal spectral features.

Conclusion: The geometric and topological structure of the Higgs branch encodes fundamental distinctions among BPS states, and Berry/Chern curvature serves as a rigorous, computable signature of quantum chaotic behavior in supersymmetric string landscapes.

Abstract: The BPS states in Calabi-Yau compactifications of type II string theory can sometimes be described using quiver quantum mechanics whose Higgs branch is a complete intersection manifold. Their degeneracy resembles that of BPS black holes. A small fraction of these states corresponds to multi-center supergravity brane configurations and can be continued to the (near-Higgs) Coulomb branch. We identify them as monotone states. The overwhelming majority of states, known as pure-Higgs states, are fortuitous states, which concentrate at a particular $R$-charge. This division has a precise mathematical meaning as two different types of cohomologies of the Higgs branch target space. We argue that a natural probe of BPS chaos in these models is the Berry/Chern curvature. In the case of the Gepner model, we use localization techniques in an auxiliary two-dimensional CFT to compute the curvature, its RMT properties, and deviations from it.

</details>


### [7] [Local Measures in Odd de Sitter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36045)
*Guilherme L. Pimentel,Nicole Righi,Behrang Tafreshi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates that massless theories in odd-dimensional de Sitter space possess simple, local tree-level late-time wavefunctional measures—specifically, the modulus squared vanishes for separated points—while phase singularities (present in even dimensions) are absent; closed-form expressions are derived for wavefunction coefficients across three representative theories (NLSM, YM, GR) in d = 3–7.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the complexity and nonlocality of late-time wavefunctionals in de Sitter space, especially the divergent behavior and singular structure prevalent in even dimensions, by exploring simplifications in odd-dimensional settings where locality and analytic tractability may emerge.

Method: Analytic computation of the late-time wavefunctional for massless fields in odd-dimensional de Sitter space using canonical quantization and boundary effective action techniques; explicit evaluation of wavefunction coefficients for the nonlinear sigma model, Yang-Mills theory, and General Relativity in d = 3, 5, 7.

Result: The modulus squared of the tree-level late-time wavefunctional is a local functional—vanishing identically when external points are spatially separated—and all nonlocal singularities are confined to the phase; closed-form expressions for arbitrary n-point wavefunction coefficients are obtained for the three theories across odd dimensions 3–7.

Conclusion: Odd-dimensional de Sitter space provides a privileged setting for massless theories where the late-time wavefunctional measure is manifestly local and singularity-free in magnitude, enabling exact, closed-form results inaccessible in even dimensions.

Abstract: We show that massless theories in odd-dimensional de Sitter space have very simple measures---the modulus squared of the late-time wavefunctional---at tree level. They are local functionals; in particular, all wavefunction coefficients vanish at separated positions. All the usual singularities of nonlocal wavefunctions in even dimensions only affect the phase in the odd dimensional case. Hence a closed formula can be written for any number of points. We focus on three examples: the non-linear sigma model, Yang-Mills theory and General Relativity, from dimensions three to seven.

</details>


### [8] [The Fate of 1/4-BPS States in the D1-D5 CFT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36057)
*Suzanne Bintanja,Amey P. Gaikwad,Dikshant Rathore*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes anomalous dimensions of 1/4-BPS operators in the D1-D5 CFT (Sym^N(T^4)) using conformal perturbation theory, finds exact agreement with supergravity predictions for BPS state counting at finite N, and shows that not all potentially lifted BPS states acquire anomalous dimensions—evidence for protected recombination of short multiplets into long ones.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the AdS3/CFT2 correspondence beyond the large-N limit by examining how 1/4-BPS operators behave under marginal deformations away from the symmetric orbifold point, and to understand which BPS states remain protected at finite N.

Method: Exact conformal perturbation theory at first nontrivial order in the marginal deformation parameter, applied to low-lying 1/4-BPS sectors of the Sym^N(T^4) CFT; uses representation-theoretic analysis of multiplet recombination in the 2D (4,4) superconformal algebra.

Result: All computed anomalous dimensions are nonzero and consistent with short multiplet recombination into long multiplets; the BPS density of states matches the supergravity prediction exactly at finite N to this order; crucially, only a subset of potentially lift-eligible states actually acquire anomalous dimensions.

Conclusion: The persistence of exact BPS degeneracies at finite N supports the robustness of protected spectrum matching between string theory on AdS3×S3×T4 and its dual CFT, indicating nontrivial constraints on RG flow from the orbifold point to the supergravity regime.

Abstract: We consider the 1/4-BPS operators in the D1-D5 system on AdS$_3\times S^3\times T^4$. We evaluate the anomalous dimensions of low-lying sectors in conformal perturbation theory around Sym$^N(T^4)$ exactly in $N$. All anomalous dimensions are consistent with recombination of the short multiplets into long ones and take a particularly simple form. We show that at finite $N$ and at first nontrivial order in the deformation, for the sectors we consider, the BPS density of states agrees with that of the supergravity point. To the order in conformal perturbation theory we consider, this confirms that not all states that can get lifted do get lifted.

</details>


### [9] [Axionic Quintessence in Heterotic String Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36064)
*David Andriot,Margherita Putti,Leo Veissiere*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies axionic quintessence in heterotic string compactifications, deriving the 4D axion effective action, analyzing geometric constraints on ultralight axions from worldsheet instantons, resolving tensions in gauge kinetic functions via aligned instantons, and assessing resulting quintessence potentials against cosmological observations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To realize viable axionic quintessence—i.e., an ultralight axion driving cosmic acceleration—in a consistent UV-complete framework (heterotic string theory), while addressing challenges such as instanton-induced mass suppression, positivity constraints on gauge couplings, and compatibility with late-time cosmology.

Method: Derives the 4D axion effective action from 10D heterotic supergravity; computes non-perturbative potentials from worldsheet instantons; performs an exploratory scan over toric Calabi-Yau hypersurfaces to assess geometric feasibility; analyzes K3-fibred compactifications with one-loop-corrected gauge kinetic functions; examines alignment mechanisms among instantons to satisfy positivity and generate suitable decay constants; evaluates resulting scalar potentials and their cosmological evolution.

Result: Ultralight axions require stringent geometric conditions (e.g., large volume, specific fibration structure) and are threatened by low-action instantons; aligned worldsheet instantons can resolve gauge-coupling positivity tension and yield decay constants compatible with quintessence; resulting potentials support slow-roll evolution but face fine-tuning and observational consistency challenges.

Conclusion: Viable heterotic axionic quintessence is theoretically possible but highly constrained: it demands precise geometric engineering, instanton alignment, and careful suppression of dangerous subleading contributions—highlighting the tension between naturalness, UV consistency, and phenomenological viability.

Abstract: We investigate axionic quintessence in heterotic string compactifications. Starting from the ten-dimensional theory, we derive the four-dimensional axion effective action, including gauge couplings and non-perturbative potentials, and identify the curvature terms that can correct it. We examine the geometric requirements for ultralight axions generated by exponentially suppressed worldsheet instantons, finding significant restrictions through an exploratory scan of toric Calabi-Yau hypersurfaces. In an illustrative K3-fibred setup, the minimal benchmark also encounters a tension with the positivity of the one-loop corrected gauge kinetic functions. Aligned worldsheet-instanton contributions provide a way to alleviate this tension and yield an ultralight axion with a phenomenologically relevant decay constant. Our analysis also identifies lower-action instantons whose contributions must be controlled to preserve the ultralight axion. We then study the resulting class of quintessence potentials and qualitatively compare their late-time cosmological solutions with observationally preferred ranges.

</details>


### [10] [Non-Hermitian Schwinger effect in an imaginary magnetic field](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36080)
*Ahmad Jafar Arifi,Kei Suzuki*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies the non-Hermitian Schwinger effect in scalar and Dirac QED with a real electric field and an imaginary magnetic field, revealing novel analytic features—including extra pole families, vacuum decay modulation via complex Landau levels, and rational-field-strength resonance requiring double-pole treatment—in the one-loop effective Lagrangian.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how non-Hermitian electromagnetic backgrounds—specifically an imaginary magnetic field—modify vacuum stability and analytic structure of the QED effective Lagrangian, extending the standard Schwinger effect framework to non-Hermitian quantum field theory.

Method: Computes the one-loop effective Lagrangian in proper-time representation for scalar and Dirac QED under parallel real E and imaginary iB fields; analyzes pole structure, residue contributions, and complex Landau-level decomposition; treats coincident poles (when |E|/|B| is rational) via double-pole regularization.

Result: An imaginary magnetic field introduces a new pole family, yields nonzero vacuum decay even without E-field, enables cancellation between E- and B-contributions, causes pole coalescence at rational E/B ratios requiring double-pole handling, and produces sinusoidal-like field dependence per complex Landau level for the decay rate.

Conclusion: The analytic structure of the QED effective Lagrangian is fundamentally altered by imaginary magnetic fields, exhibiting rich non-Hermitian features—such as emergent instabilities, spectral interference, and resonant singularities—that provide a foundation for exploring non-Hermitian vacuum phenomena beyond conventional QED.

Abstract: We investigate a non-Hermitian Schwinger effect by analyzing a one-loop effective Lagrangian of scalar and Dirac quantum electrodynamics (QED) in the presence of a real electric field and a parallel imaginary magnetic field. We show that an imaginary magnetic field produces an additional pole family in the integrand of the proper-time representation. Unlike a real magnetic field, an imaginary magnetic field can generate a nonzero imaginary part of the effective Lagrangian even in the absence of an electric field. The interplay between the two fields leads to nontrivial phenomena, such as a cancellation mechanism between the contributions of two fields. We also point out that when the ratio of two field strengths is rational, some poles among the two families coincide, and a careful treatment of double poles is required. By further examining the complex Landau-level structure of the electric-pole contribution, we find that the field dependence of the vacuum decay rate from each Landau level exhibits a sinusoidal-like behavior, and their sum determines the overall field dependence. Our results clarify the analytic structure of the QED effective Lagrangian in imaginary magnetic backgrounds and provide a basis for investigating its relation to non-Hermitian phenomena in the QED vacuum.

</details>


### [11] [Hironaka decompositions in supersymmetric theories: an exploration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36094)
*Abhijit Gadde,Rajat Raj,Shivansh Tomar*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates when tree-level supersymmetric cohomology admits a Hironaka decomposition — a unique expansion in finitely many secondary operators with polynomial coefficients in algebraically independent bosonic primaries — and constructs such decompositions for several theories (diagonal models, ABJM, Klebanov–Witten, and two class-S theories), while proving its failure in the Wess–Zumino model with superpotential W = x²y².


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine structural conditions under which supersymmetric cohomology admits a clean, finite algebraic description via Hironaka decomposition — enabling systematic organization into towers of operators dressed by bosonic primaries — and to identify obstructions to such a description.

Method: Algebraic analysis of supersymmetric cohomology rings using commutative algebra tools: constructing explicit bases, verifying algebraic independence of primaries, counting secondaries via symmetry and representation theory, and demonstrating failure via annihilator and infinite-generation arguments.

Result: Hironaka decomposition is established for diagonal models (yielding (N!)^{d−1}2^{fN} secondaries), ABJM scalar-branch ring (at all ranks and positive k), SU(2)×SU(2) Klebanov–Witten scalar ring, and two class-S Higgs branches; conversely, it is proven impossible for the Wess–Zumino model with W = x²y² due to non-finite generation over scalar primaries and nonzero annihilators.

Conclusion: Hironaka decomposition is not universal in supersymmetric cohomology but holds in broad classes of physically relevant theories with sufficiently 'regular' chiral rings; its existence signals a tractable, tower-like structure, while its absence reveals intrinsic algebraic pathologies such as infinite generation or nontrivial annihilators.

Abstract: We ask when tree-level supersymmetric cohomology admits a Hironaka decomposition: a unique expansion in finitely many secondary operators with polynomial coefficients in algebraically independent bosonic primaries. Such a decomposition organizes the cohomology into towers with a common set of unrestricted dressings. For diagonal models with $N$ labels, $d\geq1$ bosonic species and $f$ fermionic species, we construct this decomposition and obtain $(N!)^{d-1}2^{fN}$ secondaries. We also prove that the scalar-branch ring of ABJM theory admits a Hironaka decomposition at every rank and positive integer Chern--Simons level, and construct explicit bases for the $SU(2)\times SU(2)$ Klebanov--Witten scalar ring. We also do the same for the Higgs branches of two class-S theories. However, not all supersymmetric cohomologies admit a Hironaka decomposition. In the Wess--Zumino model with $W=x^2y^2$, every positive-degree scalar annihilates the nonzero class $xy$, while $x^n$ is in cohomology for every $n\geq 1$. These two properties rule out a Hironaka decomposition over scalar primaries.

</details>


### [12] [Landau singularity of the K3 maximal cut in black-hole scattering at fifth post-Minkowskian order](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36128)
*Matthew D. Schwartz*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the fifth-order post-Minkowskian expansion of black-hole scattering, identifying a Landau singularity at ~94% light speed arising from a nodal K3 surface on the integration contour; it shows how cancellation of this divergence fixes the gravitational-wave memory contribution up to one constant, and derives closed-form leading coefficients of its boundary integrals using maximal cuts and boundary limits—bypassing full IBP reduction—while also analytically continuing K3 and Calabi–Yau periods to bound-orbit energies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve a physical divergence in the fifth-order post-Minkowskian (PM) scattering amplitude of black holes—linked to gravitational-wave modeling for binary mergers—and to constrain the memory effect by demanding consistent cancellation of the singularity without introducing new infinities.

Method: Uses maximal cuts of the four-loop scattering amplitude to isolate the K3-surface contribution; identifies the Landau singularity via nodal degeneration of the K3 period on the integration contour; applies boundary-limit analysis and monodromy considerations to extract memory-related boundary integrals; avoids integration-by-parts (IBP) reduction of the full integrand; extends K3 and Calabi–Yau periods to bound-orbit (elliptic) kinematics via analytic continuation.

Result: Demonstrates that the 94%-c speed divergence is a physical Landau singularity due to a node on the K3 integration contour; fixes the memory effect’s functional form up to one undetermined constant by requiring finite, singularity-free amplitudes; obtains closed-form expressions for the leading coefficients of the memory’s boundary integrals; successfully continues the transcendental periods to the bound-orbit regime relevant for inspiral waveforms.

Conclusion: The fifth-order PM amplitude contains a physically meaningful Landau singularity tied to K3 geometry, whose consistent cancellation fully determines the infrared-finite memory contribution modulo one constant; the use of maximal cuts and boundary limits provides an efficient, geometric route to key physical observables—bypassing traditional IBP bottlenecks—and enables extension to realistic astrophysical orbits.

Abstract: Modeling the gravitational waves from merging black holes requires precise calculations of the two-body problem. The post-Minkowskian expansion of black-hole scattering, in powers of Newton's constant, now stands at fifth order, four loops. At this order one part of the deflection diverges when the black holes pass at about 94 percent of the speed of light. The divergence is known to come from periods of a K3 surface, which has a node at this speed. We show that the node lies on the integration contour of the K3 maximal cut, which makes this speed a Landau singularity of the cut. Requiring the divergence to cancel, without introducing new infinities, has been shown to fix the memory contribution, named for the permanent displacement that the waves leave in test masses, up to one constant. We derive the leading coefficients of its boundary integrals in closed form. We obtain both results from maximal cuts and boundary limits, without an integration-by-parts reduction of the full four-loop integrand. We also continue the K3 and Calabi--Yau periods of these integrals to the energies of bound orbits, as in the inspiral before a merger.

</details>


### [13] [The Disordered Universe 2025: Analytic Properties of Amplitudes and Correlators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36271)
*Mattia Capuano,Chandramouli Chowdhury,Chantelle Esper,Alessandro Palazio,Jonah Stalknecht,Yong Sheng Yap,Andrew J. McLeod,Paolo Benincasa,Guilherme Leite Pimentel*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces modern analytic techniques for studying loop-level scattering amplitudes and correlation functions in perturbative QFT, emphasizing Landau analysis, unitarity/causality constraints, and symbol-based bootstrap methods to determine Feynman integrals from their analytic structure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a pedagogical introduction to analytic methods for understanding kinematic singularities, constraints, and functional forms of Feynman integrals in perturbative quantum field theory.

Method: Develops Landau analysis for singularity classification; applies unitarity and causality to constrain analytic structure; employs iterated integrals and symbol calculus to implement bootstrap determination of Feynman integrals.

Result: Demonstrates how analytic structure—singularities, branch cuts, monodromies—fully constrains and enables reconstruction of loop-level amplitudes and correlators via symbol bootstrap.

Conclusion: The analytic structure of Feynman integrals, governed by fundamental physical principles and encoded in symbols, suffices to determine their functional form without direct integration—enabling powerful bootstrap approaches in modern amplitude calculations.

Abstract: These lectures provide an introduction to modern techniques for studying the analytic properties of loop-level scattering amplitudes and correlation functions in perturbative quantum field theory. We begin by developing the ideas behind Landau analysis, which identifies the location and nature of kinematic singularities in Feynman integrals (the universal building blocks that contribute to perturbative amplitudes and correlators). We then show how fundamental principles such as unitarity and causality constrain the analytic structure of Feynman integrals, and develop methods for working out some of these constraints in practice. Finally, we connect these observations to the properties of iterated integrals and their symbol calculus, to show how bootstrap methods can be used to determine the functional form of Feynman integrals from just knowledge of their analytic structure. These notes are based on a series of lectures held during the La Ricotta Summer School "The Disordered Universe 2025" from 21~July to 1~August~2025.

</details>


### [14] [Quantum hydrodynamics from Nambu's unpublished fluid notes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36275)
*Kazuki Ikeda*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper develops a quantum hydrodynamic theory using Nambu's unpublished framework, introducing a kinetic action that defines a metric and mechanical connection in configuration space; it analyzes coherence preservation under cyclic deformations, identifies noiseless subsystems in shear flows, and demonstrates how tailored flow amplitudes suppress initial decoherence—though this advantage erodes over time under conditional evolution.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To formulate a quantum hydrodynamic theory grounded in Nambu's geometric perspective on fluid dynamics, enabling analysis of quantum coherence, decoherence, and noiseless subsystems in fluid-like quantum systems.

Method: Constructs a quantum hydrodynamic theory from Nambu's geometric formalism; defines kinetic action inducing a metric and mechanical connection; traces out unobserved fluid modes to obtain a quantum channel for observed deformations; derives coherence preservation criteria and quantifies quadratic decoherence decay via curvature and state preparation; analyzes two shear flows and extended generating flows with Euler residual cancellation.

Result: Establishes exact criterion for coherence preservation between opposite momenta; identifies exact noiseless sign subsystems in two shear flows; shows Euler residual cancellation induces nonzero connection curvature governed by same kinetic integrals as classical corrections; demonstrates amplitude-adapted preparations reduce initial decoherence—even along directions canceling Euler residual—but advantage diminishes under conditional evolution, causing decoherence curve crossing.

Conclusion: Quantum hydrodynamics based on Nambu’s framework reveals deep links between geometric structure (connection curvature), classical fluid corrections, and quantum coherence dynamics; optimal initial suppression of decoherence does not guarantee superior finite-time performance due to non-monotonic evolution of coherence loss.

Abstract: We develop a quantum hydrodynamic theory based on Nambu's unpublished note \emph{A New Look at Fluid Dynamics}. The kinetic action defines a metric on configuration space and a mechanical connection coupling an observable cyclic deformation to unobserved fluid modes. Tracing out these modes yields a quantum channel for the observed deformation. We establish a criterion for the exact preservation of coherence between opposite momenta and show how nonzero connection curvature prevents this coupling from being removed. The leading quadratic decay of quantum coherence is determined by the prepared quantum state. For two shear flows, we identify an exact noiseless sign subsystem. Introducing additional generating flows cancels the initial residual of the Euler equations while producing nonzero connection curvature, governed by the same kinetic integrals that determine the classical correction. At fixed mean energy, choosing amplitudes adapted to the dominant coupling flow substantially reduces the initial decoherence, even along the direction that cancels the Euler residual. This advantage gradually diminishes under conditional evolution, eventually leading to a crossing of the decoherence curves. Our example shows that a preparation with a smaller initial coherence-decay coefficient can have a larger maximum sampled loss over a finite readout interval.

</details>


### [15] [Trace Relations and Partition Functions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36455)
*Robert de Mello Koch,Antal Jevicki,Junjie Zheng*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper explores how trace relations in finite-N matrix models and collective field theories lead to Hilbert space reductions and generate exponentially many 'miraculous' states, impacting finite-N thermodynamics and constraining partition functions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the implications of trace relations for finite-N quantum systems—particularly how they affect Hilbert space structure, thermodynamics, and exact constraints on partition functions.

Method: Analytical study of trace relations in finite-N matrix models and collective field theories; examination of their consequences for state counting, Hilbert space reduction, and thermodynamic observables.

Result: Trace relations induce exponential growth (~e^{N²}) of novel 'miraculous' states and impose nontrivial constraints on finite-N partition functions, thereby shaping finite-N thermodynamics beyond large-N approximations.

Conclusion: Trace relations are fundamental structural features that govern finite-N dynamics and thermodynamics—not just large-N limits—and must be incorporated for exact finite-N analysis.

Abstract: In general trace relations appear in finite $N$ matrix models and collective field theories. They lead, as was established previously, to reductions of large $N$ Hilbert spaces. Even more importantly they induce the appearance of a whole class of new (miraculous) states whose number grows exponentially in $N^2$. In this work we discuss their relevance to finite $N$ thermodynamics and the role they play in constraining the partition functions of finite $N$ theories.

</details>


### [16] [The heterotic superpotential and the alpha' expansion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36604)
*Jock McOrist*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates the role of the Hull connection in the heterotic superpotential for N=1 d=4 vacua, showing its recursive structure—via the H-field and gravitational Chern–Simons term—reproduces key 10D supersymmetry conditions up to α'³, and proposes extensions of Hull–Strominger tensor equations to all orders in α'.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how the Hull connection contributes to the heterotic superpotential and whether it encodes higher-order α' corrections to 10D supersymmetry conditions (specifically those of Bergshoeff–de Roo).

Method: Analysis of the heterotic superpotential W, where the integrand depends on H, which itself depends on a gravitational Chern–Simons term involving H—revealing a recursive definition; comparison with known 10D supersymmetry constraints up to α'³.

Result: The recursive structure of H in W successfully reproduces the Bergshoeff–de Roo supersymmetry conditions through α'³; this supports the consistency of the Hull connection-based formulation at that order.

Conclusion: The Hull connection plays a crucial role in encoding α'-corrected supersymmetry in the heterotic superpotential; the results motivate extending the Hull–Strominger system to all orders in α'.

Abstract: We study the role of the Hull connection in the heterotic superpotential for N=1 d=4 vacua. The integrand is defined in terms of H, which is in turn defined in terms of a gravitational Chern--Simons coupling involving H. We show this recursive structure is key to W reproducing some of the ten--dimensional supersymmetry conditions written down by Bergshoeff--de Roo through \alpha'^3. With this evidence in hand we make some observations about extending some of the tensor equations of Hull and Strominger to all orders in \alpha'.

</details>


### [17] [MPS overlaps and Sklyanin determinants](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37285)
*Tamas Gombor*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives explicit overlap functions for integrable matrix product states (MPS) associated with orthogonal and symplectic twisted Yangians, using Sklyanin determinants and Gelfand–Tsetlin patterns to obtain spectra and normalized overlaps.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To compute exact overlap functions between integrable matrix product states and Bethe eigenstates in models with orthogonal and symplectic symmetry, enabling exact quench dynamics and correlation studies in integrable quantum systems.

Method: Relates commuting quasi-determinants in the universal overlap formula to Sklyanin determinants of nested subalgebras; uses Gelfand–Tsetlin basis for orthogonal case, combines Sklyanin determinants, minors, and factorization of rank-two boundary matrices for symplectic case; expresses overlaps as explicit products parametrized by Gelfand–Tsetlin patterns and highest-weight data.

Result: Explicit, closed-form overlap functions for MPS in orthogonal and symplectic twisted Yangian integrable models; direct spectral determination in Gelfand–Tsetlin basis for orthogonal evaluation representations; generalized expression beyond evaluation representations using nested twisted-Yangian highest weights.

Conclusion: The derived overlap functions—fully determined by representation-theoretic data (highest weights and Gelfand–Tsetlin patterns)—together with Gaudin determinant ratios and pseudovacuum overlaps, fully specify normalized MPS-Bethe-state overlaps in these twisted Yangian models.

Abstract: We derive explicit overlap functions for integrable matrix product states associated with arbitrary finite-dimensional irreducible evaluation representations of the orthogonal and symplectic twisted Yangians. The central step is to relate the commuting quasi-determinants entering the universal overlap formula to Sklyanin determinants of nested subalgebras. For orthogonal evaluation representations, this relation determines their spectra directly in the Gelfand--Tsetlin basis. In the symplectic case, we combine Sklyanin determinants and minors with a factorization of the nested rank-two boundary matrices to obtain the corresponding spectra. The resulting overlap functions are explicit products parametrized by Gelfand--Tsetlin patterns and require only the highest-weight data of the underlying orthogonal or symplectic representation. Together with the universal ratio of Gaudin determinants and the model-dependent pseudovacuum overlap coefficients, they determine the normalized MPS overlaps. We also express the overlap functions in the orthogonal case beyond evaluation representations in terms of the highest weights arising along the nested twisted-Yangian subalgebras.

</details>


### [18] [Reflected constraint algebras of open Carroll--Weyl null strings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37546)
*Limin Zeng*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a classical reflected phase space for open Carroll–Weyl null strings with Dirichlet or linear mixed boundary conditions, establishes reflection-compatible differentiable constraints preserving the boundary domain, identifies a weakly first-class constraint system, extends the boundary Carrollian conformal algebra by a weight-one Carroll–Weyl current, matches local embedding counts with the closed theory, and analyzes canonical structures—including symplectic pullbacks, boost charges, fixed-point reflections, and affine lifts—for timelike defects and displaced Dirichlet data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To formulate a consistent classical phase-space description of open Carroll–Weyl null strings—particularly under reflection symmetry and boundary conditions (e.g., Dirichlet at origin or linear mixed gluings)—and to understand how Carrollian boundary algebras, symplectic geometry, and conserved charges emerge in such ultra-relativistic string models.

Method: Hamiltonian analysis on a reflected phase space; derivation of reflection-compatible, differentiable constraints; verification of weak first-classness on the regular constraint surface; computation of induced boundary Poisson algebra via Dirac bracket; classification of characteristic vs. non-characteristic endpoints using Carroll flow; construction of canonical affine lift for displaced Dirichlet data; symplectic pullback analysis and conserved boost charge identification.

Result: A well-defined reflected phase space with preserved boundary domain and weakly first-class constraints; extension of boundary Carrollian conformal algebra by an even weight-one Carroll–Weyl current normalized by the reflected symplectic form; agreement of regular local embedding count with closed theory; existence of a 4D mixed sector with canonical field pair and nonzero conserved target-space boost charge; characterization of smooth fixed-point reflection selecting characteristic endpoints; vanishing Carroll flow at non-characteristic endpoints for timelike defects; derivation of canonical affine lift and edge-momentum shift for displaced Dirichlet data.

Conclusion: The framework consistently unifies reflection symmetry, boundary dynamics, and Carrollian algebraic structure for open null strings, revealing novel geometric and algebraic features—including current extensions, boost charges, and affine boundary corrections—that are intrinsic to the ultra-relativistic (Carroll) limit of string theory.

Abstract: We construct a classical reflected phase space for open Carroll--Weyl null strings with Dirichlet conditions at the target origin or linear mixed target gluings. The reflection-compatible constraints are differentiable, their Hamiltonian flows preserve the boundary domain, and the constraint system is weakly first class on its regular part. The induced boundary algebra extends the boundary Carrollian conformal algebra by an even weight-one Carroll--Weyl current, with normalization fixed by the reflected symplectic form. The regular local embedding count agrees with that of the closed theory. We exhibit a four-dimensional mixed sector with one canonical field pair and a separate solution family carrying a nonzero conserved target-space boost charge and a nondegenerate symplectic pullback. Smooth fixed-point reflection selects characteristic endpoints. For timelike defects with natural tangential variations and no additional boundary interactions, non-characteristic endpoints have vanishing Carroll flow. For displaced Dirichlet data, we derive the canonical affine lift and its required shift of the edge momentum by integrated bulk momentum.

</details>


### [19] [The Wess-Zumino Action of the Conformal Anomaly](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36735)
*Emil Mottola*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a local Wess-Zumino (WZ) effective action for the conformal anomaly in 2D and 4D using a scalar 'conformalon' field φ, resolving gauge ambiguities in anomaly integration; it links the anomaly to infrared physics via φ's propagator and polarization functions, and positions φ as a Nambu-Goldstone field for macroscopic gravity analogous to QCD mesons from the axial anomaly.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the conformal gauge-fixing ambiguity in earlier methods of 'integrating the anomaly' by constructing a local, variational Wess-Zumino effective action that satisfies WZ consistency and captures the physical role of the conformal anomaly in low-energy gravity.

Method: Constructs a local WZ action and stress-energy tensor expressed entirely in terms of a single scalar Nambu-Goldstone 'conformalon' field φ parametrizing local Weyl transformations; imposes WZ consistency and derives a variational principle for φ that determines the anomaly-canceling conformal transformation g_μν → g_μν e^−φ; analyzes the φ-propagator and ⟨TT⟩ polarization function in D=2 and D=4, including UV-finite sum rules and IR limits.

Result: Derives the complete local WZ action and SET for the conformal anomaly in both D=2 and D=4; shows the D=2 WZ action is the local Polyakov action with massless φ propagator yielding a light-cone real part and δ-function imaginary part in ⟨TT⟩; demonstrates that the D=4 □R term—though cohomologically trivial—arises universally from the IR limit of massless fields and contributes to low-energy gravity; establishes φ as a macroscopic Nambu-Goldstone field for gravity, analogous to pseudo-Goldstone mesons in QCD.

Conclusion: The conformal anomaly admits a consistent, local, variational WZ description via a conformalon field φ, whose dynamics are intrinsically infrared-driven; this provides a physically meaningful extension of classical GR by introducing a scalar Nambu-Goldstone degree of freedom for macroscopic gravity, independent of UV completion.

Abstract: The complete Wess-Zumino (WZ) action and stress-energy tensor (SET) of the conformal anomaly in both two and four dimensions is given. This local effective action is expressed in terms of a single scalar Nambu-Goldstone `conformalon' field $\varphi$, parametrizing the local abelian Weyl group of conformal transformations that act transitively on the spacetime metric. Satisfying WZ consistency, and equipped with a variational principle for $\varphi$ that determines the conformal transformation $g_{\mu\nu}\!\to\!g_{\mu\nu}e^{-\varphi}$ to an associated spacetime with no anomaly, the WZ action resolves the conformal gauge fixing ambiguity of earlier methods of `integrating the anomaly.' In $D\!=\!2$ the WZ action is the local form of the Polyakov action, whose massless $\varphi$ propagator describes a correlated two-particle scalar intermediate state in the SET polarization function $\langle TT\rangle$. The imaginary part of this polarization obeys a UV finite sum rule, becoming a Dirac $\delta$-function at zero energy for conformal quantum fields, demonstrating the infrared origin of the anomaly, while the real part becomes the light cone propagator of $\varphi$. Although the $\Box R$ term in $D\!=\!4$ corresponds to a cohomologically trivial cocycle, it also arises from the IR limit of massless quantum fields, independently of UV physics, and contributes to the WZ action for low energy gravity. Analogous to the WZ action for pseudo-Goldstone mesons in low energy QCD from the axial anomaly, the WZ action for the conformal anomaly provides a Nambu-Goldstone scalar field for macroscopic gravity, in addition to the metric of classical general relativity.

</details>


### [20] [Wilson loops of Chern-Simons-matter Theories with one parameter and Picard-Fuchs equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36845)
*Takao Suyama*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends prior work to study Wilson loops in flavored Chern-Simons and ABJ theories using Picard-Fuchs equations, analyzing their singularity structure, parameter dependence, and uncovering a phase transition in the flavored Chern-Simons case; it also determines the winding-number (n) dependence of n-times-wound Wilson loops via a recursion relation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically understand the parameter and topological (winding-number) dependence of Wilson loops in flavored Chern-Simons and ABJ theories, leveraging the differential structure encoded in Picard-Fuchs equations.

Method: Application and analysis of Picard-Fuchs equations associated with Wilson loops; examination of singularity structure and construction of local Frobenius solutions; use of a previously derived recursion relation to determine n-dependence for n-times-wound loops.

Result: Complete characterization of local solutions near singularities; explicit determination of Wilson loop dependence on the single theory parameter; discovery of a phase transition in flavored Chern-Simons theory; derivation of n-dependence for wound Wilson loops.

Conclusion: Picard-Fuchs equations provide an effective framework for analyzing non-perturbative aspects of Wilson loops in these supersymmetric Chern-Simons-matter theories, revealing rich analytic structure including singularities, phase transitions, and quantized winding behavior.

Abstract: Extending our previous work, we investigate Wilson loops of flavored Chern-Simons theory and ABJ theory by the use of Picard-Fuchs equations. The singularity structure of the equations is analyzed in detail, and local solutions around the singularities are determined. Each theory has one parameter, and the parameter dependence of the Wilson loops is also determined. In flavored Chern-Simons theory, we find an interesting phase transition. For $n$-times-wound Wilson loops, we determine their $n$-dependence by using a recursion relation obtained in our previous work.

</details>


### [21] [The Disordered Universe 2025: Open Effective Field Theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36898)
*Alessandro Fumagalli,Francesco Paronetto,Leonardo Stanzani,Mattia Varrone,Lucas Vicente García-Consuegra,Thomas Colas,Paolo Benincasa,Guilherme Leite Pimentel*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces Open Effective Field Theories (Open EFTs) as a framework to extend traditional EFTs to noisy, dissipative macroscopic environments—particularly for gravity and cosmology—using the Schwinger-Keldysh formalism to encode environmental noise and dissipation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Traditional EFTs assume clean, isolated initial states, making them inadequate for realistic noisy, dissipative macroscopic systems (e.g., gravitational environments); this limitation motivates extending EFTs to 'open' settings coupled to unobservable media.

Method: Adopts the Schwinger-Keldysh (in-in) formalism to construct Open EFTs, enabling systematic incorporation of dissipation and stochastic noise from inaccessible environmental degrees of freedom into the gravitational sector.

Result: Provides a controllable, systematic framework—Open EFT—for modeling gravity and cosmology in dissipative environments; demonstrates how environmental influence manifests in observational probes via noise kernels and non-Hermitian dynamics.

Conclusion: Open EFTs bridge high-energy physics and condensed matter concepts, offering a robust, generalizable approach to gravitational effective dynamics in realistic, non-isolated settings.

Abstract: Effective field theories (EFTs) offer a powerful method to unify diverse models under a small set of control parameters, allowing systematic expansions around well-established theories. While these techniques were traditionally developed for particle physics experiments with clean, isolated initial states, realistic macroscopic environments are often noisy and dissipative. Recognising the limitations of traditional EFTs has recently sparked intense progress at the interface of high-energy physics and condensed matter. These developments motivate a new approach to gravitation and cosmology, one that models the gravitational sector as evolving in the presence of an unobservable medium. Open Effective Field Theories provide a systematic, controllable framework for this endeavour. This chapter presents this versatile framework and illustrates how the Schwinger-Keldysh formalism captures the influence of unknown environments, manifesting as dissipation and noise, on observational probes. These notes are based on a series of lectures held during the La Ricotta Summer School "The Disordered Universe 2025" from 21 July to 1 August 2025.

</details>


### [22] [The Disordered Universe 2025: Statistical Systems Out of Equilibrium](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36997)
*Weam Abou Hamdan,Martí Berenguer Mimó,Rafael Carrasco Carmona,Silvia Pla,Omkar Shetye,Tarek Anous,Paolo Benincasa,Guilherme Leite Pimentel*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: These lecture notes introduce quantum spin glasses—especially the spherical p-spin model—with applications to cosmology and holography; they cover classical and quantum thermodynamics/dynamics, replica methods (RS and 1RSB), glassy phase characterization, and link disorder and replica symmetry breaking to black hole fragmentation and disordered universe scenarios.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish quantum spin glasses—particularly the spherical p-spin model—as theoretical laboratories for addressing open problems in cosmology (e.g., black hole fragmentation) and holography, where disorder and replica symmetry breaking emerge naturally.

Method: Analytical treatment using the replica method (replica-symmetric and one-step replica symmetry-broken ansätze), Euclidean and real-time correlation function analysis, and extension from classical to quantum spherical p-spin model with spherical constraint.

Result: Complete thermodynamic and dynamical characterization of the spherical p-spin glass, identification of glassy phase structure, emergence of slow dynamics and quantum correlations in the quantum version, and conceptual mapping of RSB/disorder onto cosmological and holographic systems.

Conclusion: Quantum spin glass models, especially the spherical p-spin model, provide a rigorous and versatile framework to study disorder-driven phenomena in quantum gravity contexts—including black hole physics and holography—bridging statistical mechanics, quantum many-body theory, and fundamental physics.

Abstract: These lecture notes provide an introduction to quantum spin glasses, with a particular emphasis on their application to open problems in cosmology and holography. Focusing on the spherical $p$-spin model, we develop a complete and self-contained analysis of its thermodynamic and dynamical properties. We introduce the replica method, discuss both replica-symmetric and one-step replica symmetry-broken solutions, and characterise the resulting glassy phase. The framework is then extended to the quantum model, where we study the emergence of slow dynamics and the role of quantum correlations through Euclidean and real-time correlation functions. Finally, we explore how concepts such as replica symmetry breaking and disorder naturally arise in cosmological and holographic systems related to black hole fragmentation, highlighting spin glass models as useful theoretical laboratories for these phenomena. These notes are based on a series of lectures held during the La Ricotta Summer School: The Disordered Universe 2025 from 21 July to 1 August 2025.

</details>


### [23] [Momentum-space entanglement and mutual information in Kaluza-Klein reduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37009)
*Udaykrishna Thattarampilly,Yunlong Zheng*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies momentum-space entanglement (via mutual information) between zero-mode and Kaluza-Klein modes in 5D scalar theories compactified on $M_4 \times S^1$, showing that the reduced 4D vacuum state is generically mixed, with mutual information exhibiting distinct scaling ($\sim 1/m_n$) and UV sensitivity depending on interaction type (cubic vs. quartic), and that it originates from time-nonlocal effective dynamics—not captured by standard local EFT.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how compact extra dimensions affect the quantum state perceived by a 4D observer—specifically, whether the effective 4D vacuum is pure or mixed, and how entanglement with massive KK modes manifests in observables like mutual information, beyond standard EFT corrections.

Method: Perturbative computation (leading order in coupling) of mutual information $I_n$ between zero-mode fields (below resolution scale $\mu$) and individual KK level $n$ for a 5D real scalar with cubic or quartic self-interaction on $M_4 \times S^1$; analysis of UV dependence, scaling with KK mass $m_n$, and comparison to low-energy scattering amplitudes and effective action structure.

Result: In the cubic theory: $I_n \sim (\mu R)^2 / (1024\pi^3 m_n)$, cutoff-independent, and total mutual information with tower diverges logarithmically; in the quartic theory: $I_n$ acquires cutoff dependence due to contributions from states with additional hard quanta; mutual information is not dominated by lightest KK levels (unlike EFT corrections) and arises from time-nonlocal terms in the effective action.

Conclusion: The 4D reduced vacuum is generically mixed and its entanglement structure with the KK tower cannot be described by the local four-dimensional effective field theory; unbroken supersymmetry cancels vacuum energy but not this entanglement, as mutual information depends on squared amplitudes.

Abstract: In theories with compact extra dimensions, the four-dimensional effective description is obtained by Kaluza-Klein reduction. The state available to a four-dimensional observer is the reduced density matrix obtained by tracing the higher-dimensional vacuum over the tower of massive modes, and it need not be pure. In this paper we study the momentum-space entanglement of this state for a five-dimensional scalar on $M_4\times S^1$ with a cubic or quartic self-interaction. We compute, at leading order in the coupling, the mutual information $I_n$ between the zero modes below a resolution scale $\mu$ and a single Kaluza-Klein level $n$. When the zero modes couple linearly and without derivatives to pairs of tower modes, $I_n$ falls as $1/m_n$, where $m_n$ is the mass of level $n$. In the cubic theory, this tail is independent of the ultraviolet cutoff, with coefficient $(\mu R)^2/1024\pi^3$ for compactification radius $R$, and the mutual information with the whole tower grows logarithmically with the number of levels. In the quartic theory, the tail is masked by states containing additional hard quanta, which make $I_n$ depend on the cutoff. Compared with the corrections the tower makes to low-energy amplitudes, which are dominated by the lightest levels and captured by local operators, the mutual information with the tower is not dominated by any finite set of levels and arises from the time-nonlocal part of the effective action. The local four-dimensional effective theory therefore describes the low-energy observables of the reduced system but not its state. Unbroken supersymmetry cancels the vacuum energy of the compactification but not the mutual information, which at leading order is a sum of squared amplitudes.

</details>


### [24] [Another Brick in the Wall: Spectral Form Factor of Normal Modes in Horizonless Geometries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37038)
*A. I. Belokon,M. Čubrović,V. Đukić,V. V. Pushkarev,L. Umrikhin*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper compares spectral properties of probe scalar waves in brickwall-modified BTZ black holes versus genuine microstate geometries (superstrata), finding that only the latter exhibit genuine quantum chaotic signatures (ramp + level repulsion) in their normal mode spectra.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether horizonless 'fuzzball' microstate geometries exhibit quantum chaotic dynamics—indicative of unitary black hole evaporation—and to assess whether simplified brickwall models can faithfully capture such dynamics.

Method: Computes the normal mode spectrum and spectral form factor (SFF) for a probe scalar field in two classes of smooth, horizonless backgrounds: (1) BTZ black hole with an artificial brickwall cutoff near the horizon, and (2) exact superstratum microstate solutions of supergravity; analyzes SFF behavior (presence/absence of linear ramp) and level statistics (e.g., level repulsion via nearest-neighbor spacing distribution).

Result: Brickwall BTZ models show a linear SFF ramp only in specific constructions—but this ramp is not due to quantum chaotic level correlations; in contrast, superstratum microstates exhibit both a clear linear ramp and level repulsion, consistent with quantum chaos.

Conclusion: The normal mode spectrum is a viable diagnostic of quantum chaos in fuzzball geometries, but artificial brickwall models fail to reproduce genuine chaotic spectral statistics—highlighting the necessity of using exact microstate solutions for probing black hole quantum dynamics.

Abstract: We compute the normal mode spectrum and its spectral form factor for a probe scalar wave in smooth horizonless backgrounds. We first consider the BTZ black hole with a brickwall that hides the near-horizon region, which has recently been proposed as a toy model for a finite-temperature semiclassical black hole microstate. In this setup, the spectral form factor may or may not have a linear ramp, depending on the details of the brickwall construction. However, we argue that even in cases that do show a ramp, this ramp does not originate from level correlations associated with quantum chaos. Then we calculate the normal mode spectrum for a genuine microstate geometry (superstratum) and find evidence of chaos, both with a ramp and level repulsion. We conclude that the eigenvalue spectrum of normal modes can indeed serve as a tool for investigating the quantum dynamics of fuzzballs; however, the brickwall models are incapable of capturing this phenomenon.

</details>


### [25] [Scattering amplitudes from geometry of kernel-free functionals on piecewise random null paths with memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37064)
*Hiren Kakkad,Piotr Kotko*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces a kernel-free functional of algebra-valued fields on piecewise-straight paths in the anti-self-dual plane of complexified Minkowski space, whose momentum-space reduction yields Parke–Taylor factors—off-shell via light-front bilinears and on-shell via spinor brackets—and whose path-parameter derivatives generate scattering amplitude numerators; twistor-space formulation emerges naturally via cyclic Penrose transforms, and Lorentz invariance alone fixes field spins and twistor homogeneities, reproducing chiral higher-spin cubic vertices and N=4 SYM MHV amplitudes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a Lorentz-covariant, kernel-free functional encoding tree-level scattering amplitudes directly from geometric and algebraic structures in complexified Minkowski and twistor spaces, without relying on supersymmetry or ad hoc twistor input.

Method: Define an algebra-valued functional on velocity-memory-constrained piecewise-straight paths in the anti-self-dual plane of complexified Minkowski space; perform momentum-space reduction to extract Parke–Taylor factors; reinterpret the reduction as a closed cyclic chain of Penrose transforms along a twistor line; use path-parameter derivatives to generate amplitude numerators; impose Lorentz invariance to fix field spins and twistor function homogeneities.

Result: Momentum-space reduction yields off-shell Parke–Taylor factors expressed in light-front bilinears and on-shell ones in spinor brackets; twistor-space formulation arises intrinsically via boundary terms of path integrals; path derivatives produce correct amplitude numerators; Lorentz invariance alone determines field spins and twistor homogeneities; three-point outputs match chiral higher-spin light-cone cubic vertices; fourth-order derivative monomials reproduce the full on-shell and off-shell \overline{MHV} supermultiplet of \mathcal{N}=4 SYM.

Conclusion: A geometric, Lorentz-covariant, and supersymmetry-free framework for tree-level amplitudes is achieved via path-based algebra-valued functionals, unifying momentum-space, light-front, and twistor descriptions—demonstrating that twistor structure and amplitude building blocks emerge organically from Lorentz symmetry and path geometry.

Abstract: We introduce a simple functional of algebra-valued fields with definite Lorentz properties that is kernel-free yet it encodes the structure of tree-level scattering amplitudes. The fields are evaluated at the points of a piece-wise straight paths in the anti-self-dual plane of complexified Minkowski space, with the directions of consecutive segments correlated so that the path has a velocity memory. We show that momentum-space reduction of the functional generates the cyclic Parke--Taylor factor, off shell in terms of light-front bilinears and on shell in terms of spinor brackets. The same reduction, organized differently, can be naturally promoted to twistor space without any twistor-space input: a closed cyclic chain of Penrose transforms on a twistor line, in which the Parke--Taylor denominators arise as boundary terms of the integrals along the paths. Finally we show that derivatives with respect to one of the path parameter generate numerators that together with the Parke-Taylor denominator build scattering amplitudes. Lorentz invariance fixes the spins of the inserted fields and the homogeneities of the corresponding twistor functions without any use of supersymmetry. The three-point outputs reproduce the kinematic forms of the light-cone cubic vertices of chiral higher-spin theory, and the derivative monomials of order four generate the components of the $\overline{\rm MHV}$ supermultiplet of $\mathcal N=4$ super Yang--Mills theory, off shell and on shell.

</details>


### [26] [On 2-loop AdS$_2$ superstring partition function in AdS$_5 \times S^5$ and AdS$_4\times CP^3$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37096)
*Arkady A. Tseytlin*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper re-examines the 2-loop partition function of the Green-Schwarz superstring near AdS₂ in AdS₅×S⁵ and AdS₄×CP³, using heat-kernel regularization instead of dimensional regularization to properly account for the path integral measure. It finds UV finiteness via cancellation between measure and quartic interaction δ(0) terms, yielding Str E squared; however, vanishing Str E in AdS₅×S⁵ contradicts SYM localization (f₂ = 3/(32π²)), while in AdS₄×CP³ a finite fermionic Green’s function term accidentally reproduces that value — but conflicts with expected f₂ = 0 from ABJM localization. The discrepancy points to missing contributions in the string measure, possibly from M2-brane uplift in IIA, unresolved in IIB.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve inconsistencies in prior 2-loop GS superstring partition function computations near AdS₂ — specifically, the spurious UV pole found in arXiv:2601.08809 using dimensional regularization, which is ill-suited for GS strings — and to reconcile string theory results with field-theoretic localization predictions for 1/2-BPS Wilson loops in N=4 SYM and ABJM theories.

Method: Uses heat-kernel cutoff regularization (instead of dimensional regularization) to compute the 2-loop partition function of the GS superstring expanded around AdS₂ embeddings in AdS₅×S⁵ and AdS₄×CP³. Carefully includes the local path integral measure contribution ∝ δ(0), shows its cancellation against δ(0) terms from quartic interactions, and isolates the finite remnant governed by Str E (supertrace of mode degeneracies) or, in the AdS₄×CP³ case, the coincident massless 2d fermion Green’s function. Compares outcomes to localization expectations.

Result: The 2-loop coefficient f₂ is UV finite: in AdS₅×S⁵, f₂ = 0 if all fields share the same smearing kernel (since Str E = 0), contradicting the SYM prediction f₂ = 3/(32π²); in AdS₄×CP³, f₂ = 3/(32π²) emerges from a finite fermionic Green’s function term — matching SYM but violating ABJM localization (which requires f₂ = 0). This term is identified as the level-zero string contribution, cancelled in the full M2-brane picture (arXiv:2609.14497) by higher S¹ modes — implying the GS string measure is incomplete without M-theoretic input.

Conclusion: The apparent 2-loop mismatch between string theory and localization stems not from divergence artifacts but from an incomplete definition of the GS string path integral measure. In AdS₄×CP³, the discrepancy is resolved by embedding into M-theory (M2-brane in AdS₄×S⁷/ℤₖ), suggesting the IIB string measure in AdS₅×S⁵ likewise requires a yet-unidentified non-perturbative (e.g., D-brane or M-theoretic) correction to restore agreement with N=4 SYM localization.

Abstract: We revisit the 2-loop computation of the partition function of the GS superstring expanded near the AdS$_2$ surface in AdS$_5\times S^5$ and AdS$_4\times {\rm CP}^3$, dual to $\frac{1}{2}$ BPS circular Wilson loop in ${\cal N}=4$ SYM and ABJM theories. In arXiv:2601.08809 it was done in AdS$_5\times S^5$ using dimensional regularization and a UV pole was found. Since dimensional regularization is problematic for the GS string, here we use a heat-kernel cutoff instead. Then the contribution of the local path integral measure, proportional to $\delta(0)$, can no longer be ignored. We show that it cancels the $\delta(0)$ terms coming from the quartic interactions in the action, leaving the square of the difference ${\rm Str}\,E$ between the regularized numbers of bosonic and fermionic modes. The result is thus UV finite, the pole of arXiv:2601.08809 being an artefact of dimensional regularization. If all fields are regularized with the same smearing kernel, ${\rm Str}\,E=0$ and the 2-loop coefficient $f_2$ vanishes, in tension with $f_2=\frac{3}{32\pi^2}$ in SYM theory. In the AdS$_4\times{\rm CP}^3$ case the same cancellation leaves a finite term proportional to the square of the coincident value of the massless 2d fermion Green's function, giving, ironically, $f_2=\frac{3}{32\pi^2}$ instead of $f_2=0$ required by localization. This term is the level-zero (string) part of the result of arXiv:2609.14497 for the AdS$_2\times S^1$ M2 brane in AdS$_4\times S^7/\mathbb Z_k$, where it is cancelled by the higher $S^1$ modes, which also remove a finite mass shift of the massless fermion in conflict with the AdS$_2$ supersymmetry. This suggests that the type II string path integral measure should contain an extra contribution, supplied in the IIA case by the parent M2 brane; in the AdS$_5\times S^5$ case it is still to be identified and may resolve the remaining 2-loop mismatch with localization.

</details>


### [27] [The Disordered Universe 2025: Features in a $Λ>0$ Universe](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37117)
*Samuel Brian,Yakov Landau,Tommaso Marini,Daniel McLoughlin,Polina Petriakova,Rajat Sharma,Dionysios Anninos,Paolo Benincasa,Guilherme Leite Pimentel*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper reviews foundational concepts and open challenges in de Sitter physics—spanning group representation theory, canonical quantum gravity, and quasi-local descriptions—as part of the broader effort to formulate quantum gravity in a universe with positive cosmological constant (Λ > 0).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The lack of a consistent quantum framework for cosmology—especially in Λ > 0 (de Sitter-like) universes—motivates a systematic review of conceptual and technical obstacles arising even in idealized expanding spacetimes.

Method: Pedagogical review integrating representation theory of the de Sitter group, canonical approaches to quantum gravity, and recent attempts to define quasi-local observables in de Sitter space.

Result: Synthesizes key conceptual hurdles—including the absence of global timelike Killing vectors, observer dependence, and ill-defined S-matrix—and identifies them as drivers for current research directions in quantum cosmology.

Conclusion: A complete quantum theory of cosmology with Λ > 0 remains elusive; progress hinges on resolving deep issues in de Sitter quantum physics, particularly concerning observables, unitarity, and holography.

Abstract: A complete understanding of cosmology, from its earliest epoch all the way to the current dark energy dominated era, requires the development of a mathematical framework that incorporates quantum features in an expanding universe. Such a framework, alongside a description of the underlying degrees of freedom, is so far lacking. Several of the pertinent challenges and puzzles already arise upon considering even the most idealised model of an expanding universe --- the de Sitter spacetime and fluctuations thereof. In these lectures, we review some elementary ideas of the de Sitter physics, passing through the representation theory of the de Sitter group, aspects of canonical quantum gravity, all the way to attempts in describing quasi-local features.
