Throughout, we highlight open problems that motivate ongoing efforts to understand quantum gravity and cosmology in universes with $\Lambda>0$. These notes are based on a series of lectures held during the La Ricotta Summer School: The Disordered Universe 2025 from 21 July to 1 August 2025.

</details>


### [28] [The Disordered Universe 2025: An Intro and a Vision](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37160)
*Paolo Benincasa,Guilherme Leite Pimentel*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces 'The Disordered Universe'—a unifying interdisciplinary framework bridging cosmology, scattering amplitudes, open quantum systems, and non-equilibrium statistical physics (e.g., spin glasses)—to develop a microscopic, scale-spanning theory of the universe.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address open cosmological questions across all scales—from late-time IR to early-universe UV—by integrating conceptual and technical tools from seemingly disparate fields, overcoming the fragmentation of current approaches.

Method: Conceptual synthesis and cross-disciplinary framing: identifying shared structures (e.g., analyticity, stochasticity, memory effects, replica symmetry breaking) across de Sitter quantum gravity, scattering amplitudes, open quantum dynamics, and disordered statistical systems.

Result: A coherent narrative linking four domains—open quantum systems, scattering amplitudes, de Sitter foundations, and non-equilibrium statistical physics—under a single cosmological paradigm, positioning cosmology as a unifying physical arena.

Conclusion: A microscopic, unified theory of the universe demands interdisciplinary convergence; 'The Disordered Universe' provides both a vision and methodological roadmap for future research, with implications for quantum gravity, inflation, dark energy, and cosmic initial conditions.

Abstract: The Disordered Universe is a novel research direction that exploits ideas and techniques coming from apparently unrelated research areas, ranging from cosmology to scattering amplitudes in particle physics and disordered complex systems, with the aim of addressing open questions in cosmology at all scales, from the very infra-red (or very late time) to the early universe. This paper acts as a general introduction and vision for a set of lectures delivered at the first La Ricotta Summer School "The Disordered Universe 2025", held from 21 July to 10 August 2025. These lectures involved open quantum systems, analytic properties of scattering amplitudes and correlation functions, foundations and quantum aspects of the de Sitter universe, as well as statistical physics out of equilibrium and spin glasses. While the lecture notes related to each course taught at the school will be published separately and are inevitably devoted to both conceptual and technical aspects of each of these topics, the school was not meant to be a collage of independent topics: the present paper has the purpose of outlining the global vision, how cosmology ends up being a unifier and benefits from both their viewpoint and techniques, how all the concepts and techniques originally coming from these four different areas are part of the same cosmological narrative, why this point of view deserves to be explored, and drawing perspectives for the future. We consider it crucial to build a microscopic theory for the early universe and maybe one that embraces all scales.

</details>


### [29] [Two types of hairy black holes in the (3+1)-dimensional $O(3)$ sigma-model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37223)
*Jutta Kunz,Aliaksei Mikhaliuk,Yakov Shnir*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs stationary, axially symmetric black holes with scalar hair in a gravitating nonlinear O(3) sigma model featuring a partly negative symmetry-breaking potential; it identifies two distinct families—type I (vacuum-connected) and type II (scalaron-supported)—differing in solitonic origin, scalar field structure, horizon behavior, and parameter dependence, with type II exhibiting larger horizon radii and nontrivial near-horizon scalar deformation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the existence and properties of hairy black holes beyond general relativity, specifically within a well-motivated field-theoretic framework—the gravitating nonlinear O(3) sigma model with symmetry-breaking potential—that allows for both solitonic and black hole solutions.

Method: Numerical construction of stationary, axially symmetric, asymptotically flat black hole solutions in Einstein-sigma model theory; systematic analysis of scalar field configurations, global charges (mass, angular momentum), horizon geometry, and bifurcation structure across model parameters (e.g., gravitational coupling strength).

Result: Two qualitatively distinct families of hairy black holes are found: type I (connected to vacuum solitons, scalar field near vacuum everywhere) and type II (connected to spinning scalaron solitons, strongly deformed scalar field—vacuum on symmetry axis, anti-vacuum near equatorial horizon); type II extends to significantly larger horizon radii while retaining nontrivial exterior scalar hair; at weak gravity, fundamental and second branches reconnect into two continuous families.

Conclusion: The O(3) sigma model supports rich hairy black hole phenomenology, with type II solutions representing genuinely new black hole configurations distinct from Kerr and type I, characterized by topologically nontrivial scalar hair and robust existence even for large horizons—challenging no-hair expectations and offering testable alternatives in strong-field gravity.

Abstract: We construct stationary axially symmetric black holes with scalar hair in the gravitating nonlinear $O(3)$ sigma model with a partly negative symmetry-breaking potential. Our results show that it supports two qualitatively different types of asymptotically flat hairy black holes, distinguished by their connection to the corresponding solitonic branches and by the structure of their scalar hair; the type I black holes are connected to the vacuum branch and type II black holes are connected to regular scalaron-supported spinning solitons. The type II black holes have a strongly deformed scalar field structure, with the scalar field taking its vacuum value on the symmetry axis and approaching the anti-vacuum in the near-horizon equatorial region. We analyze their global charges, horizon properties, and dependence on the model parameters, comparing the solutions with Kerr black holes. For sufficiently weak gravitational coupling, the branch connectivity changes: the fundamental branches of types I and II reconnect to form one continuous family, while their corresponding second branches form another. We find that, in contrast to the vacuum-connected type I family, the scalaron-supported type II black holes extend to substantially larger horizon radii while preserving a nontrivial scalar configuration outside the horizon.

</details>


### [30] [On the geometric origin of central charges in gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37302)
*Lorenzo Di Giacomo,Daniele Pranzetti*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a covariant, Lagrangian-based method to compute central extensions of asymptotic symmetry algebras—bypassing traditional charge algebra computations—via two general theorems on charge representation under diffeomorphisms with field-dependent vector fields and Lagrangian anomalies; it is concretely verified for AdS₃ gravity with Brown–Henneaux boundary conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguities in conventional methods for computing central extensions of asymptotic symmetry algebras—specifically, the reliance on explicit conserved charge algebra computations—and to provide an intrinsic, geometric, and unambiguous derivation rooted solely in the Lagrangian and covariant phase space structure.

Method: Uses an ambiguity-free covariant phase space formalism grounded in the Noetherian split; proves two Charge Representation Theorems covering cases of vanishing and integrable Noetherian flux (i.e., field-dependent vector fields and Lagrangian anomalies); derives explicit formulas for central extensions and verifies cocycle identities; applies the framework to asymptotically locally AdS₃ spacetimes with Penrose–Brown–Henneaux boundary conditions.

Result: Derives general, explicit expressions for central extensions directly from the Lagrangian; establishes cocycle identities confirming their consistency; recovers the centrally extended Virasoro algebra for AdS₃ gravity, matching the Brown–Henneaux result as a non-trivial validation.

Conclusion: Central extensions of asymptotic symmetry algebras are intrinsic geometric data of the covariant phase space—not artifacts of charge algebra computations—and can be systematically constructed from the Lagrangian alone via the Noetherian split; the Charge Representation Theorems provide a universal, constructive algorithm applicable beyond AdS₃.

Abstract: Central extensions of asymptotic symmetry algebras are traditionally determined through an explicit computation of the algebra of conserved charges. We show that, within an ambiguity-free covariant phase space formalism based on the Noetherian split, they instead arise as intrinsic quantities completely determined by the Lagrangian through the geometry of the associated covariant phase space. We prove two general Charge Representation Theorems describing the canonical realization of diffeomorphism symmetries in the presence of field-dependent vector fields and Lagrangian anomalies, corresponding respectively to vanishing and integrable Noetherian flux. In both cases, we derive explicit expressions for the associated central extensions and establish the corresponding cocycle identities. This provides a constructive algorithm for determining central charges directly from the Lagrangian formulation of the theory, without requiring an explicit computation of the charge algebra. As a non-trivial application, we consider asymptotically locally AdS$_3$ spacetimes with Penrose--Brown--Henneaux boundary conditions. We recover the corresponding centrally extended asymptotic symmetry algebra, providing an explicit realization of the Charge Representation Theorems.

</details>


### [31] [Entanglement Bounds on Cardy Growth in Higher Dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37449)
*Yuya Kusuki,Yin Tang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes universal bounds linking thermal entropy and entanglement in unitary CFTs in 3D and 4D, expressed via anomaly coefficients (a, F) and the thermal cosmological constant coefficient f; connects them to Rényi entropy, conformal collider bounds, and stress-tensor three-point functions; verifies the bounds across diverse CFT regimes (free, weakly coupled, large-N, holographic, finite-N numerics); and shows implications for Cardy density-of-states growth.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover universal constraints on thermal entropy and entanglement structure in unitary conformal field theories, motivated by the interplay between anomalies, entanglement measures, and high-energy spectral behavior—particularly how thermodynamic quantities encode quantum information theoretic data.

Method: Analytic derivation of inequalities using spherical Rényi entropy, conformal collider bounds, and decomposition of the stress-tensor three-point function; verification across controlled CFT families: free theories (saturation), weakly coupled gauge theories, large-N vector models, and holographic (AdS/CFT) duals; supplemented by finite-N numerical checks.

Result: Proved bounds: f ≤ 4π²a (4D) and f ≤ 2.99830949…F (3D); identified a stronger conjectural inequality involving the stress-tensor OPE coefficient that is saturated by all free CFTs and verified in multiple limits; showed these bounds restrict the Cardy exponent governing high-energy density-of-states growth.

Conclusion: Thermal entropy (via f) is universally bounded by conformal anomalies (a, F) and governed by stress-tensor correlation structure; this reveals a deep, quantifiable link between thermodynamics, entanglement, and conformal symmetry in unitary CFTs, with implications for quantum gravity via holography and spectral theory.

Abstract: We propose bounds relating thermal entropy to entanglement in unitary conformal field theories: $f\le4\pi^2a$ in four dimensions and $f\le2.99830949\ldots\,F$ in three dimensions. Here $f$ is the dimensionless coefficient of the cosmological constant term in the thermal effective action, $a$ is the Euler anomaly coefficient, and $F$ is the sphere free energy. Spherical Rényi entropy relates the four-dimensional bound to the conformal collider bounds. We formulate a stronger conjectural inequality involving the stress-tensor three-point function, saturated by every free theory, and establish it in controlled regimes of weakly coupled gauge theories, large-$N$ vector models, and holographic CFTs. Finite-$N$ numerical results provide additional tests. The bounds constrain the Cardy growth of the density of states at high energy.

</details>


### [32] [Exact $T\bar{T}$-Like flows in Integrable Sigma Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37458)
*H. Babaei-Aghbolagh*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper develops a universal exact autonomy framework for TT̄-like irrelevant deformations of 2D integrable sigma models and 4D self-dual nonlinear electrodynamics, governed by the Courant–Hilbert equation and expressible via Russo–Townsend auxiliary fields; it derives a universal flow equation ∂_λ ℒ = X ℱ(√(Y/X)) with X = T_μν T^μν, Y = (T^μ_μ)², proves integrability preservation, commutativity with marginal root-TT̄ flow, and extends the construction to arbitrary dimension d.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically construct and classify exact TT̄-like irrelevant deformations beyond the standard Born–Infeld case, unifying 2D integrable sigma models and 4D self-dual nonlinear electrodynamics under a common autonomous flow structure rooted in the Courant–Hilbert equation.

Method: Uses the Courant–Hilbert equation characterization and Russo–Townsend auxiliary-field formulation; derives an exact one-parameter irrelevant flow equation in universal form involving scalar invariants X and Y of the stress tensor; obtains parametric solutions, perturbative expansions, regularity conditions, and proves commutativity and integrability preservation; extends construction to arbitrary spacetime dimension d.

Result: Derives universal irrelevant flow ∂_λ ℒ = X ℱ(√(Y/X)) where ℱ is determined by the Courant–Hilbert solution; provides parametric representation valid for all one-scale models; establishes perturbative expansion and domain of regularity; proves commutativity with marginal root-TT̄ flow and preservation of classical integrability; gives explicit results for generalized Born–Infeld, q-deformed, no-τ-maximum, and logarithmic models (the latter admitting closed form); extends framework to arbitrary d with unified one-variable structure.

Conclusion: The work establishes a systematic, exact, and integrability-preserving classification framework for TT̄-like irrelevant deformations across dimensions, linking 2D sigma models and 4D self-dual nonlinear electrodynamics through a shared Courant–Hilbert–Russo–Townsend structure—enabling broader exploration of solvable irrelevant deformations beyond conventional settings.

Abstract: Recently, a general exact autonomy framework for $T\bar{T}$-like deformations of 2D integrable sigma models and 4D nonlinear electrodynamics has been developed. The theories are characterized by the Courant-Hilbert equation and admit an equivalent description in the Russo-Townsend auxiliary-field formulation. For general one-scale families, we derive an exact irrelevant flow equation of the universal form $\partial_\lambda \mathcal{L}=X\mathcal{F}(\sqrt{Y/X}), \,\,\,X=T_{\mu\nu}T^{\mu\nu}, \,\,\, Y=(T^\mu_\mu)^2 $ where the function $\mathcal{F}$ is determined by the underlying Courant-Hilbert solution. We obtain a parametric representation valid for arbitrary one-scale models, establish its perturbative expansion in terms of the defining function or auxiliary potential, and identify the domain in which the flow remains regular. We also show that the irrelevant flow commutes with the marginal root-$T\bar{T}$ deformation and that both flows preserve classical integrability. Explicit results are derived for generalized Born-Infeld, q-deformed, no-$\tau$-maximum, and logarithmic models, with the latter admitting a closed representation. Finally, we extend the flow construction to arbitrary spacetime dimension $(d)$, revealing a common one-variable structure that connects two-dimensional integrable sigma models to four-dimensional self-dual nonlinear electrodynamics. These results provide a systematic framework for constructing and classifying exact $T\bar{T}$-like flows beyond the standard Born-Infeld setting.

</details>


### [33] [Recovering the leading quantum correction to Newton Potential beyond the Trace Anomaly](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37506)
*Riccardo Fecchio*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates how semiclassical gravity, incorporating vacuum polarization and trace anomaly effects, reproduces the quantum-corrected Newton potential at order 1/r³. It shows that the trace anomaly alone is insufficient, but adding the vacuum-polarization stress tensor—using Visser’s Schwarzschild ansatz in the Boulware vacuum—yields the correct 1/r³ correction linear in mass M; its coefficient is adjustable via a single parameter to match the one-loop quantum result, while trace-anomaly coefficients enter only at 1/r⁴.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the discrepancy between the known one-loop quantum correction to the Newton potential (∝ 1/r³) and the inability of the trace-anomaly-derived effective action to reproduce it; and to consistently incorporate quantum vacuum effects into semiclassical gravity.

Method: Combines the semiclassical Einstein equations with both anomalous and vacuum-polarization stress tensors; employs Visser’s polynomial ansatz for the stress tensor in the Boulware vacuum for Schwarzschild spacetime; expands the full stress tensor asymptotically (large r); solves the field equations perturbatively in ℏ.

Result: Visser’s ansatz generates a 1/r³ correction to the Newton potential linear in M, structurally matching the one-loop result; its coefficient is determined by a single free parameter in the ansatz, tunable to the one-loop value; trace-anomaly coefficients contribute only at subleading order (1/r⁴).

Conclusion: The 1/r³ quantum correction to Newtonian gravity arises from vacuum polarization—not the trace anomaly—and can be consistently captured within semiclassical gravity using Visser’s approximation, provided the vacuum-polarization stress tensor is included alongside the anomalous one.

Abstract: The leading quantum correction to the Newton potential induced by the one-loop vacuum effective action of gravity is of order $1/r^3$. One-loop corrections are also responsible for the trace anomaly. We first show, following our previous work, that the effective action obtained by integrating the trace anomaly cannot reproduce this correction. We then complete the semiclassical Einstein equations with the vacuum-polarization stress tensor in addition to the anomalous stress tensor. The full stress tensor follows from the approximation proposed by Visser for the Schwarzschild spacetime. The stress tensor is taken in the Boulware vacuum and expanded at large distance, where it is conserved and reproduces the trace anomaly. Solving the equations perturbatively in $\hbar$, we find that Visser's approximation generates the $1/r^3$ correction to the Newton potential, linear in the source mass $M$, with the same structure as the one-loop result. Its coefficient is controlled by a single free parameter of Visser's polynomial ansatz, which can be tuned to match the one-loop value. The dependence on the trace-anomaly coefficient appears only at order $1/r^4$.

</details>


### [34] [Resurgence of Black Holes in Thermal OPE](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37554)
*Alba Grassi,Hewei Frederic Jia*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the resurgent structure of the momentum-space thermal OPE expansion for two-point functions in holographic CFTs, linking perturbative (stress-tensor-driven, near-boundary) and non-perturbative (bulk-geometry-sensitive, WKB-determined) contributions; it identifies Borel singularities with complex WKB actions, finds a timelike complex-frequency chamber where Borel resummation reconstructs the exact correlator, and derives Stokes jumps governing non-perturbative transitions across chamber boundaries.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how non-perturbative physics—encoded in bulk geometry beyond the boundary—is captured in the asymptotic high-energy expansion of thermal correlators via resurgence theory, bridging holographic CFTs and semiclassical gravity.

Method: Exact WKB analysis of bulk scalar wave equations in planar and spherical AdS black hole backgrounds; Borel resummation of the momentum-space thermal OPE; identification of Borel singularities with complex WKB actions connecting the AdS boundary to distinguished points (e.g., black hole singularity); analysis of Stokes phenomena in the complex frequency plane.

Result: Perturbative thermal OPE is fully determined by stress-tensor sector and near-boundary bulk dynamics; Borel singularities are located at values equal to WKB actions from boundary to critical points in complex radial coordinate; a well-defined complex-frequency chamber exists in the timelike regime where Borel resummation yields the exact thermal two-point function; Stokes jumps across chamber boundaries quantify required non-perturbative corrections.

Conclusion: The thermal OPE’s asymptotic expansion in holographic CFTs exhibits a controlled resurgent structure: perturbative data fixes the series, while non-perturbative effects—geometrically tied to global bulk features—are precisely encoded in Borel singularities and Stokes phenomena, enabling exact reconstruction of thermal correlators via resummation in appropriate analytic domains.

Abstract: The momentum-space thermal OPE generically gives an asymptotic high-energy expansion of thermal two-point functions. We study the resurgent structure of this expansion in holographic CFTs at infinite and finite volume, whose dual bulk geometries are planar and spherical AdS black holes, respectively. The perturbative expansion is fixed to all orders by the stress-tensor sector of the thermal OPE and can be computed from a near-boundary analysis of the bulk wave equation. Non-perturbative effects, on the other hand, encode information about the bulk geometry away from the boundary. Using exact WKB analysis, we identify the locations of the associated Borel singularities as WKB actions between the AdS boundary and distinguished points in the complex radial plane, which include the black hole singularity in certain regimes. Furthermore, we identify a complex-frequency chamber in the timelike momentum regime where Borel resummation of the high-energy expansion reconstructs the full thermal two-point function. We further derive Stokes jumps that determine the non-perturbative contributions required upon crossing the boundary of this chamber.

</details>


### [35] [A Spin on Radiation Reaction with Cuts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37603)
*Asaad Elkhidir,Matteo Sergola,Pablo Vives Matasan*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives radiation-reaction equations of motion for a spinning charged particle ('√Kerr' particle) up to quadratic order in spin, generalizing the Abraham–Lorentz–Dirac equation; validates them by computing electromagnetic waveforms in scattering off a spectator charge using both classical self-field and modern amplitude-based (unitarity-cut + Compton) methods, finding agreement through O(s²); also recovers known linear-in-spin and spinless six-dimensional self-force results.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically incorporate spin-dependent radiation reaction into the equations of motion for a classical spinning charged particle beyond linear order, bridging classical electrodynamics and modern scattering-amplitude techniques, and verifying consistency between classical self-field and quantum-inspired amplitude-based approaches.

Method: 1) Classical self-field approach: compute the particle's own electromagnetic field on its worldline using dimensional regularization to handle divergences; 2) Amplitude-based approach: extend the unitarity-cut formalism with spinning-particle Compton amplitudes to extract radiation-reaction contributions to the waveform; 3) Both methods applied to electromagnetic scattering off a static spectator charge, at tree level and one-loop (leading radiation-reaction).

Result: Radiation-reaction equations of motion for a spinning charged particle valid through quadratic order in spin; full agreement between classical and amplitude-derived electromagnetic waveforms at one loop up to O(s²); recovery of known linear-in-spin self-force and six-dimensional spinless self-force as consistency checks.

Conclusion: The derived quadratic-in-spin radiation-reaction equations are consistent across complementary frameworks — classical point-particle electrodynamics and modern on-shell amplitude methods — confirming their validity and establishing a robust foundation for higher-spin, higher-order radiation-reaction studies in classical and quantum-gravitational analogues.

Abstract: We derive the radiation-reaction equations of motion for a spinning charged body, the $\sqrt{\text{Kerr}}$ particle, through quadratic order in its spin, generalising the Abraham--Lorentz--Dirac equation. Extending an argument of Coleman, we obtain radiation reaction equations by evaluating the self-field of the spinning body on its own worldline, and removing its divergences with dimensional regularisation. We use the resulting equations of motion to compute the electromagnetic waveform emitted in the scattering of the spinning body off a spectator charge, both at tree level and including the leading radiation-reaction correction, which enters at one loop. Then, we apply the construction introduced in a previous work by some of the authors -- where radiation reaction is encoded in unitarity cuts involving Compton amplitudes -- to spinning particles, and recompute the radiation-reaction contribution to the one-loop waveform. We find that the waveform calculated using classical and amplitude methods agree through quadratic order in spin, providing a check on our radiation-reaction equations. Along the way, we also reproduce known results for linear-in-spin self-force effects and for the spinless self-force in six dimensions.

</details>


### [36] [Stability of Axion--SU(2) Chromomagnetic Condensates with Stochastic Spin-2 Dressing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37610)
*Sha Azyzy,Azadeh Maleknejad,Vahid Kamali*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper explores how a stochastic spin-2 gauge-field background stabilizes the chromomagnetic condensate in axion–SU(2) Yang–Mills theory at fixed axion charge; using coarse-graining, it identifies new statistically homogeneous/isotropic spin-2-dressed condensates, analyzes their fluctuation spectrum at Hartree level, and shows that gap survival depends on non-Gaussian correlations—constrained by statistical positivity—yielding model-independent consistency bounds on stability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether stochastic spin-2 gauge-field backgrounds can stabilize non-Abelian chromomagnetic condensates in flat spacetime, relevant for early-universe cosmology (inflation, dark energy, primordial gravitational waves) and high-temperature QCD-like sectors where thermal effects are subdominant.

Method: Coarse-graining of the stochastic spin-2 gauge field to construct statistically homogeneous and isotropic dressed chromomagnetic condensates; mean-field (Hartree) analysis of fluctuation spectra; inclusion of leading scattering corrections from fluctuations propagating in the stochastic background; application of statistical positivity to constrain higher-point correlation functions via model-independent consistency conditions.

Result: A new class of spin-2-dressed chromomagnetic condensates satisfying classical background equations is identified; the Hartree-level fluctuation spectrum reveals a positively gapped region; non-Gaussian correlations—bounded by statistical positivity—partition this region into one where a positive Born-level gap is ruled out and another where stability remains viable.

Conclusion: Stochastic spin-2 dressing can stabilize chromomagnetic condensates, but only under stringent, model-independent constraints from non-Gaussianity and statistical positivity; this provides a robust framework for assessing stability of non-Abelian gauge condensates in cosmological and high-energy contexts beyond thermal equilibrium.

Abstract: We investigate whether a stochastic spin-2 gauge-field background can stabilize the chromomagnetic condensate of axion--SU(2) Yang--Mills theory at fixed axion Noether charge in flat spacetime. By coarse-graining the gauge field, we uncover a new class of statistically homogeneous and isotropic spin-2-dressed chromomagnetic condensates satisfying the classical background equations. We then study their fluctuations in two steps. First, at the mean-field (Hartree) level, we determine the fluctuation spectrum and the region where all modes remain positively gapped. Second, we include the leading scattering corrections from fluctuations propagating through the stochastic background. We find that gap survival is controlled by non-Gaussian correlations of the dressing. Statistical positivity constrains the otherwise undetermined higher-point contributions through model-independent consistency conditions, without fixing their detailed momentum dependence. These bounds separate the Hartree-gapped region into a part where a positive Born-level gap is ruled out and a part where stability remains possible. The results are relevant to non-Abelian gauge condensates in early-universe cosmology, including inflation, dark energy, and primordial gravitational waves, and to QCD-like gauge sectors above confinement where thermal effects are subleading.

</details>


### [37] [The 't Hooft model on (A)dS$_2$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37667)
*Panos Betzios*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper solves large-N 2D QCD ('t Hooft model) on rigid AdS₂ and dS₂ spacetimes, deriving an exact meson equation that modifies 't Hooft's equation with a curvature-dependent Legendre operator; it establishes chiral symmetry breaking, Gell-Mann–Oakes–Renner relation, Regge-to-logarithmic dimension crossover, boundary-bound quark-antiquark scattering, and finite-width resonances in dS₂ due to thermal dissociation at the de Sitter temperature.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the exactly solvable 't Hooft model to curved two-dimensional backgrounds—AdS₂ and dS₂—to probe how spacetime curvature affects confinement, chiral dynamics, integrability, and meson stability in a controlled large-N setting, bridging light-front holography, string theory, and quantum field theory in curved space.

Method: Uses light-front quantization in null gauge A₋ = 0 on AdS₂ and dS₂; exploits SL(2,ℝ) isometry to identify the curvature’s role via the special-conformal generator; derives an exact meson Bethe–Salpeter equation by incorporating the SL(2,ℝ) Casimir, yielding a modified 't Hooft equation with a Legendre differential operator; solves analytically for meson spectrum, chiral condensate, scattering phases, and resonance widths.

Result: On AdS₂: (i) exact meson equation with curvature-induced Legendre term; (ii) massless pion and Gell-Mann–Oakes–Renner relation fixing chiral condensate; (iii) Regge trajectory crossover to logarithmic scaling when flux tube length exceeds AdS radius; (iv) persistence of flat-space integrability; (v) boundary-bound quark-antiquark scattering with critical coupling and phase shift. On dS₂: mesons are unstable resonances with widths ∼ exp(−m/T_dS), where T_dS is the de Sitter temperature.

Conclusion: Curvature fundamentally reshapes the meson spectrum and dynamics in the 't Hooft model: AdS₂ preserves key nonperturbative features (confinement, chiral symmetry breaking, integrability) while introducing geometric scaling crossovers, whereas dS₂ intrinsically destabilizes bound states via thermal effects, demonstrating that infinite-N does not guarantee stability in expanding spacetimes.

Abstract: We solve large-$N$ two-dimensional QCD with fundamental quarks---the 't Hooft model---on rigid AdS$_2$ and dS$_2$. Their isometries preserve the null gauge $A_-=0$ and in affine coordinates the curvature enters the light-front Hamiltonian only through the special-conformal generator of the null line. The $SL(2,\mathbb{R)}$ Casimir then results in an exact meson equation: 't Hooft's equation plus a Legendre operator whose sign is determined by the curvature. On AdS$_2$ it is related to equations proposed in the context of folded/segmented strings and light-front holography. The pion is massless in the chiral limit and obeys a Gell-Mann--Oakes--Renner relation that fixes the chiral condensate, the dimensions cross over from Regge to logarithmic growth once the flux tube exceeds the curvature radius, and the flat-space integrable structure survives. We also solve the problem of a quark interacting with an antiquark placed at the boundary: beyond a critical coupling the string pulls it back to the boundary, resulting in a scattering phase. On dS$_2$ and due to the expansion of space, the mesons are unstable resonances even at $N=\infty$ and eventually dissociate. Their widths are given by Boltzmann factors at the de Sitter temperature.

</details>

### [38] [SuperJEPA: Pretraining with a Supervision-Augmented Joint-Embedding Predictive Architecture for High Energy Physics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36121)
*Dylan Sheldon Rankin,Ho Fung Tsoi*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper investigates whether self-supervised learning improves jet-tagging performance in high-energy physics when large labeled simulation data is already available, proposing SuperJEPA—a joint-embedding predictive architecture with added supervised loss—and demonstrating consistent gains over pure supervised pretraining.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine if self-supervised objectives provide benefits beyond supervised pretraining in high-energy physics, where high-fidelity labeled simulations are abundant—contrasting the typical foundation-model regime reliant on massive unlabeled data.

Method: Introduces SuperJEPA: a joint-embedding predictive architecture (JEPA) extended with an auxiliary supervised loss; evaluates it on jet-tagging tasks across varying model sizes, pretraining dataset scales, and encoder usage modes (frozen vs. fine-tuned).

Result: SuperJEPA achieves improved downstream jet-tagging performance consistently across multiple tasks, model sizes, and pretraining dataset sizes; gains hold for both frozen and fine-tuned encoders.

Conclusion: Predictive self-supervision, even alongside strong supervised signals, enhances representation learning in particle physics; SuperJEPA validates its utility and motivates integrating such hybrid objectives in future large-scale physics foundation models.

Abstract: Foundation models often obtain powerful results through very large unlabeled training datasets that dwarf available labeled datasets in size, but high energy physics already provides large samples of high-fidelity labeled simulation. We therefore ask whether self-supervised objectives improve over supervised pretraining alone when large labeled samples already exist. We introduce SuperJEPA, a joint-embedding predictive architec- ture augmented with a supervised objective, and find improved downstream jet-tagging performance across multiple tasks, model sizes, and pretraining dataset sizes. The gains persist for both frozen and fine-tuned encoders, motivating predictive self-supervision in future large-scale particle-physics foundation models.

</details>


### [39] [Update on transverse-spin azimuthal asymmetries in single-hadron SIDIS on transversely polarised deuterons at COMPASS](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36224)
*Artur Hoghmrtsyan*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports the simultaneous extraction of all eight transverse-spin-dependent azimuthal asymmetries in single-hadron SIDIS off a transversely polarised deuteron target, using the full 2022 COMPASS dataset; it updates Collins and Sivers asymmetries and presents six previously unmeasured asymmetries for the first time.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To achieve a comprehensive understanding of nucleon structure via transverse-spin-dependent observables in SIDIS, particularly to constrain transverse-momentum-dependent parton distribution functions (TMDs) and subleading-twist correlations.

Method: Analysis of the full 2022 COMPASS dataset from SIDIS on a transversely polarised deuteron target using a 160 GeV/c muon beam; simultaneous extraction of all eight azimuthal asymmetries via fitting of azimuthal modulations in hadron production.

Result: Updated Collins and Sivers asymmetries consistent with prior partial-statistics results; first measurements of the remaining six transverse-spin asymmetries with full 2022 statistics; completion of COMPASS's transverse-spin SIDIS programme across proton and deuteron targets.

Conclusion: These results provide essential, high-precision constraints for global TMD analyses and advance the quantitative understanding of transverse-spin structure in the nucleon.

Abstract: Over the years, the COMPASS experiment at CERN has pursued an extensive SIDIS programme aimed at a comprehensive understanding of the structure of the nucleon, using a high-energy muon beam and polarised targets. Within this programme, measurements with a transversely polarised deuteron target were performed in 2022 using the 160 GeV/c muon beam. In single-hadron SIDIS on such a target, eight transverse-spin-dependent azimuthal asymmetries arise, providing sensitivity to several transverse-momentum-dependent parton distribution functions and to subleading-twist partonic correlations. We report the simultaneous extraction of all eight asymmetries from the complete 2022 deuteron sample. The Collins and Sivers asymmetries, previously presented using about 70% of the 2022 statistics, are updated with the full sample and found consistent with the earlier result, while the six remaining asymmetries are presented for the first time with the full 2022 statistics. Combined with the existing proton results, these measurements complete a comprehensive COMPASS programme on transverse-spin effects in SIDIS, providing essential input for global analyses.

</details>

*Ying-Hao Wang,Yi-Feng Wu,Pei-Rong Li,Yu Zhang,Xiao-Rui Lyu*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper evaluates the potential of the Super Tau-Charm Facility to precisely measure charm mixing, indirect CP violation, and key hadronic parameters (e.g., strong-phase differences, coherence factors) using quantum-correlated D-Dbar pairs produced at √s = 3.773 and 4.030 GeV, with time-integrated double-tag analysis and 1 ab⁻¹ luminosity per energy point.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide high-precision, complementary constraints on charm mixing, indirect CP violation, and hadronic parameters—especially for the CKM angle γ determination—using quantum-correlated D-Dbar events, filling gaps left by time-dependent measurements at Belle II and LHCb.

Method: Time-integrated double-tag analysis of coherent D-Dbar pairs from e⁺e⁻ → D(*)Dbar(*) at two center-of-mass energies; one D is flavor-tagged via quasi-flavor-specific decays (K⁻π⁺π⁺π⁻, K⁻π⁺π⁰, K⁻π⁺), while the other Dbar is reconstructed in flavor-specific, CP eigenstate, or self-conjugate final states.

Result: Projected sensitivities show capability to precisely determine x, y (mixing parameters), |q/p|, ϕ (indirect CPV), strong-phase differences δ_Kπππ, δ_Kππ⁰, δ_Kπ, and coherence factors R_Kπππ, R_Kππ⁰, R_Kπ; these yield unique inputs to the CKM angle γ global fit.

Conclusion: The Super Tau-Charm Facility, with its quantum-correlated D-Dbar sample and high statistics, offers a uniquely powerful platform for precision charm physics—particularly for hadronic parameter extraction and indirect CPV tests—complementing current and future experiments.

Abstract: We investigate the prospects for determining charm mixing, indirect $C\!P$ violation and hadronic parameters in charm decays with quantum-correlated neutral charm mesons at the proposed Super Tau-Charm Facility. The study is based on time-integrated double tag rates of coherent $D\overline{D}$ pairs produced at $\sqrt{s}=3.773$ and $4.030$ GeV, from $e^{+}e^{-}\to D^{(*)}\overline{D}^{(*)}$ processes. Here, one $D$ is tagged with the quasi flavour-specific decays $D\to K^-\pi^+\pi^+\pi^-$, $K^-\pi^+\pi^0$, and $K^-\pi^+$, while the other $\overline{D}$ is detected with the final states in flavour-specific mode, $C\!P$ eigenstate mode, or self-conjugate mode. The expected sensitivities are evaluated assuming an integrated luminosity of $1~{\rm ab}^{-1}$ at each energy point, demonstrating the capability on precise determination of the charm mixing parameters, the indirect $C\!P$ violation parameters, the strong-phase differences, and the coherence factors. The constraints of the hadronic-parameters provide unique inputs to the CKM angle $\gamma$ fit, while the measurements of charm mixing and indirect $C\!P$ violation parameters are complementary to the time-dependent measurements in the Belle II and LHCb experiments.

</details>


### [41] [Antenna-coupled microstrip summing network for wave-like dark matter searches](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36684)
*Chelsea Bartram,Taj A. Dyson,Jacob Laurel,Sephora Ruppert,Matthew O. Withers,Osmond Wen,Andrew K. Yi,Chao-Lin Kuo*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper introduces a novel microwave slot-antenna array with a microstrip summing network to improve signal coupling and power extraction from large-volume, high-frequency (>19 GHz) axion-search cavities—especially for multi-wavelength resonators where conventional wire antennas fail—enabling potential DFSZ sensitivity via scalable cavity arrays.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Conventional wire antennas struggle with efficient power extraction from large-volume, multi-wavelength resonators (V ≫ λ³) used in high-frequency axion searches (e.g., ADMX-VERA), limiting sensitivity.

Method: Design and implementation of a capacitively coupled microwave slot-antenna array interfaced with a microstrip 'summing network'—adapted from CMB detector technology—to coherently combine signals from multiple transmission lines into a single output; validated in a 19–21 GHz high-frequency resonator.

Result: Successful demonstration and characterization of the new readout scheme in the 19–21 GHz band; shows improved coupling scalability for large-volume cavities.

Conclusion: The slot-antenna + summing network architecture enables coherent, scalable readout for high-frequency, large-volume axion cavities, paving the way toward DFSZ sensitivity using multiple such cavities in a shared cryogenic volume.

Abstract: A commonly used technology for axion and other wave-like dark matter searches is a microwave resonator. In such experiments, the detected signal strength depends on the coupling between the resonator and the microwave transmission line that delivers power to the amplifier. In Axion Dark Matter eXperiment (ADMX) and other cavity searches, this coupling is typically achieved using a compact wire antenna inserted into the cavity. While this approach is adequate for right-cylindrical cavities, it can become difficult to efficiently extract power from large-volume, multi-wavelength ($V \gg \lambda^3$) resonators targeted for higher frequencies, such as those designed by ADMX-VERA (Volume Enhanced Resonant Axion). Here, we present a solution based on a microwave slot-antenna array capacitively coupled to a microstrip "summing network," which coherently combines signals from multiple transmission lines into a single output. This summing architecture is adapted from designs originally developed for cosmic microwave background (CMB) detectors. We demonstrate the design and characterization of a new high-frequency resonator that employs this readout scheme in the $19-21$ GHz range. Finally we discuss the potential to reach Dine-Fischler-Srednicki-Zhitnitsky (DFSZ) sensitivity using multiple summing network cavities fitted into a large volume.

</details>

Additionally, we search for $CP$ asymmetry in the $J/\psi\to\mu^+\mu^-\mu^+\mu^-$ decay and determine the T-odd observable to be $A_T = [0.05\pm 0.04~{\rm (stat.)}\pm0.01~{\rm (syst.)}]$, which is consistent with $CP$ conservation. In the search for $J/\psi\to Z^{\prime}\mu^+\mu^-$ via $Z^{\prime}\to\mu^+\mu^-$, no significant $Z^{\prime}$ signal is observed in the $\mu^+\mu^-$ invariant mass region of $(0.212,~2.6)$~GeV$/c^2$, and we set upper limits on the ${\rm U}(1)_{L_{\mu}-L_{\tau}}$ model coupling constant $g^{\prime}$ ranging from 0.001 to 0.294 at the 90\% confidence level, which is comparable to the upper limits set by Belle II for $Z^{\prime}$ masses below $0.7$~GeV$/c^2$.

Conclusion: This measurement provides the strongest confirmation of CEνNS to date, validates SM predictions at low momentum transfer, and demonstrates HPGe detectors’ sensitivity for precision electroweak physics and beyond-SM searches in the sub-keV nuclear recoil regime.

Abstract: We report the most precise measurement to date of coherent elastic neutrino-nucleus scattering (CE$\nu$NS) from reactor antineutrinos using the CONUS+ high-purity germanium detectors at the Leibstadt nuclear power plant. With an exposure of 1296 kg$\cdot$days reactor-on, a blind likelihood analysis yields $1555 \pm 152$ CE$\nu$NS events, with a significance of 10.2 $\sigma$, in agreement with the Standard Model expectation of $1701 \pm 198$ events within 0.6 standard deviations. The result also determines the weak mixing angle at a momentum transfer of about 10 MeV, $\sin^2\theta_W = 0.225 \pm 0.022$, consistent with the Standard Model prediction.

</details>

Abstract: Coherent elastic neutrino-nucleus scattering (CE$\nu$NS) provides a uniquely sensitive probe of neutrino properties, but its detection requires pushing energy thresholds to the sub-keV scale while controlling backgrounds. The CONUS+ experiment, located at the Leibstadt nuclear power plant, reported the first observation of CE$\nu$NS using reactor antineutrinos in 2025. Following a detector upgrade that replaced three of the original 1 kg high-purity germanium detectors with larger 2.42 kg diodes, this paper presents the analysis of the CONUS+ run 2 dataset, including the detector characterization, the background model, the signal prediction and expected sensitivity, and the extraction of the CE$\nu$NS signal. The new detectors reach a background level per unit mass reduced by approximately a factor of two relative to the original detectors, while reaching energy thresholds between 140 and 170 eV$_{ee}$. With an exposure of 1296 kg$\cdot$days reactor-on and 86 kg$\cdot$days reactor-off, a blind likelihood analysis fitting directly the number of signal counts yields $N_s = 1555 \pm 152$ CE$\nu$NS events, corresponding to a significance of 10.2$\,\sigma$. The result is in agreement with the Standard Model expectation of $1701 \pm 198$ events, with $N_s/N_{\mathrm{SM}} = 0.91 \pm 0.14$.

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe new physics via long-lived particles (LLPs) produced in neutrino-nucleus upscattering—complementing existing searches by accessing meter-scale displaced decays inaccessible to typical collider or short-baseline experiments.

Method: Analysis of 2.95×10²⁰ POT CRT Beam Telescope data (2017–2018), searching for displaced e⁺e⁻ pairs from LLP decays in the CRT system; background estimation via data-driven and simulation-based methods; limit setting using profile likelihood with 90% CL.

Abstract: The Short-Baseline Near Detector (SBND) is a liquid argon time projection chamber in Fermilab's Short-Baseline Neutrino Program, positioned 110 meters from the neutrino target along the Booster Neutrino Beam (BNB). In addition to its neutrino physics goals, SBND enables searches for new physics, including long-lived particles (LLPs) produced via neutrino-nucleus upscattering that decay into visible final states. Although neutrino data taking with the full SBND experiment began in December 2024, a subset of the Cosmic Ray Tagger (CRT) system operated from 2017--2018 as a standalone detector, referred to as the CRT Beam Telescope, collecting data corresponding to $2.95\times 10^{20}$ protons on target from the BNB. Originally intended for commissioning and performance studies, the substantial dataset collected also enables searches for displaced LLP decays. This paper presents the first search for physics beyond the Standard Model using data from the SBND CRT Beam Telescope, targeting LLP decays into electron-positron pairs. We observe no significant excess above the expected background and set 90% confidence-level upper limits on an effective production cross section at the level of $10^{-38}$ cm$^2$ for laboratory decay lengths of 0.3-100 m. These results complement existing searches by probing displaced decay signatures at meter-scale decay lengths.

</details>

### [49] [Closure of Traceful Radiation-Gauge Reconstruction with Matter: General Kerr Conditions, a Thin-Shell Benchmark, and a Conserved Torus](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35938)
*G. G. L. Nashed*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper formulates a metric reconstruction framework for radiation-gauge perturbations on a Kerr background with matter, decomposing it into Weyl-curvature, Ricci-trace, and mass–angular-momentum completion sectors; proves local existence/uniqueness of the associated triangular transport system under compatible data; and validates the framework using two concrete matter sources: a static thin shell around Schwarzschild (yielding analytic ADM-mass shift and frame-dragging predictions) and a stationary fluid torus on Kerr (demonstrating coupling across Ricci sectors).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the long-standing closure problem in first-order, traceful, radiation-gauge metric reconstruction on Kerr spacetimes with physical matter sources—enabling consistent, gauge-invariant, and physically interpretable gravitational-wave modeling and self-force applications.

Method: Decomposes the linearized Einstein equations into three coupled sectors (Weyl curvature, Ricci trace, and mass–angular-momentum completion); constructs a triangular first-order hyperbolic transport system on a regular Kerr domain; imposes compatibility conditions among initial data, radiation-gauge constraints, and completion prescriptions; and validates via exact solutions: Israel junction conditions for a thin spherical shell and relativistic hydrodynamics for a constant-angular-momentum torus.

Result: Local existence and uniqueness of solutions to the triangular transport system are established under compatible data; global closure requires additional asymptotic and horizon-regularity conditions; for the thin shell, the ADM-mass shift is analytically derived as √(1−2M/R) times rest mass, axial sector fully determines angular momentum, moment of inertia, and interior frame dragging; for the Kerr torus, Newman–Penrose Ricci projections reveal nontrivial coupling between Ricci sectors due to nonsphericity.

Conclusion: The radiation-gauge reconstruction problem admits a well-posed local formulation when properly sector-decomposed and constrained; the thin shell serves as a closed analytic benchmark, while the Kerr torus demonstrates that generic matter sources necessitate full coupling across Ricci sectors—highlighting the essential role of completion data in global consistency.

Abstract: We formulate the closure problem for first-order, traceful radiation-gauge metric reconstruction on a Kerr background with matter. The reconstruction is decomposed into Weyl-curvature, Ricci-trace, and mass--angular-momentum completion sectors. For compatible initial, gauge, and completion data, we establish local existence and uniqueness of the resulting triangular transport system on a regular Kerr domain, and identify the additional conditions needed for a closed global formulation. This is not a global existence theorem. We test the framework using two conserved matter sources. For a thin shell around a Schwarzschild black hole, Israel matching, regularity, asymptotic flatness, a barotropic equation of state, and fixed rest mass determine the static, axial, and leading rotational sectors. The ADM-mass shift is $\sqrt{1-2M/R}$ times the shell rest mass, while the axial sector determines the angular momentum, moment of inertia, and interior frame dragging. We also construct a stationary constant-angular-momentum fluid torus on Kerr and compute its Newman--Penrose Ricci projections, showing that nonspherical sources generally excite coupled Ricci sectors. The shell provides a closed benchmark, while the torus illustrates the additional structure required for general Kerr sources.

</details>


### [50] [Can a Wormhole Induce a Monopole?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35969)
*Sergei Slobodov*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: The paper refutes the idea that a star orbiting one mouth of a wormhole induces periodic mass variations detectable at the other mouth; such variations do not occur at first order in gravity, and even the Bondi mass at the observer’s end cannot increase—only decrease via gravitational radiation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To critically assess the theoretical basis for proposed observational signatures—specifically periodic mass modulations—around Sgr A* that were hypothesized to arise from stellar orbits around one mouth of a traversable wormhole.

Method: Analytical general relativistic reasoning, drawing analogies with electromagnetism (Maxwell), examining conservation laws for mass definitions (Schwarzschild mass, Bondi mass), and analyzing asymptotic behavior of vacuum spacetimes with two wormhole mouths.

Result: No first-order change in observed Schwarzschild mass occurs due to source motion through a wormhole mouth; Bondi mass at the observer end cannot increase—even beyond first order—and is only subject to decrease via gravitational radiation; identical external dynamics can be mimicked without invoking wormholes when mouths are widely separated.

Conclusion: The proposed periodic mass signal for wormhole detection is unphysical; it stems from misinterpreting static solutions as dynamical states. Wormhole-induced mass modulation is not a viable observational signature.

Abstract: Could a star orbiting one mouth of a wormhole make the mass seen near the other rise and fall? We show that the periodic mass change proposed for searches around Sgr A* does not occur. The predicted signal comes from treating static solutions with different masses as successive states of the same orbiting system. In the Maxwell analogue, charge measured around a mouth changes only when current crosses the measuring surface. Similarly, in gravity, source motion cannot change the observed Schwarzschild mass at first order. Furthermore, the Bondi mass of a vacuum observer end cannot increase even beyond first order, though gravitational radiation can reduce it. The same observer-side dynamics can be reproduced with both mouths in one exterior at sufficiently large separation.

</details>


### [51] [First-order corrections to equatorial periodic orbits around rapidly rotating black holes in dynamical Chern--Simons gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35983)
*Hao-Jie Lin,Tao Zhu,Jing-Fei Zhang,Xin Zhang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes first-order corrections to orbital energy, radial period, and circumferential turning radii for equatorial periodic orbits around rapidly rotating black holes in dynamical Chern–Simons (dCS) gravity, using analytic metric corrections matched to Kerr references; it identifies sign changes and null boundaries (C_T=0) for period corrections, reveals discrepancies between circumferential and coordinate shifts, and links corrections to phase and frequency shifts in kludge waveforms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe deviations from general relativity via gravitational-wave observables by quantifying how dynamical Chern–Simons gravity modifies equatorial periodic orbits around fast-spinning black holes—specifically targeting measurable effects on orbital periods, turning points, and waveform phasing.

Method: Analytic perturbative calculation of first-order dCS metric corrections; matching of dCS orbits to Kerr reference orbits at fixed dimensionless spin, specific angular momentum, and rational periodicity; decomposition of period correction into direct and energy-compensation terms; two-parameter numerical scan over prograde/retrograde orbits; identification of C_T = 0 contours; mapping to leading-quadrupole numerical kludge waveforms and spectral analysis.

Result: Period corrections exhibit sign changes across orbital families due to competition between direct and energy-compensation contributions; negative corrections are dominated by energy compensation (prograde) or direct contribution (retrograde); C_T = 0 boundaries are located; circumferential shifts can oppose coordinate shifts; phase accumulation in kludge waveforms is first-order equivalent to a Kerr mass rescaling; for C_T = 0 orbits, this phase contribution vanishes at equal black hole masses, while amplitude corrections persist.

Conclusion: dCS gravity induces rich, orbit-dependent signatures in orbital dynamics and gravitational wave phasing; the existence of C_T = 0 orbits enables null tests of dCS coupling; waveform phase effects can be degenerate with Kerr mass rescaling, but amplitude and spectral corrections break the degeneracy, supporting model discrimination with future high-precision observations.

Abstract: We calculate first-order corrections to the energy, radial period, and circumferential turning radii of equatorial periodic orbits around rapidly rotating black holes in dynamical Chern--Simons (dCS) gravity. Using an analytic fit to the metric correction, we match each orbit to a Kerr reference at fixed dimensionless spin, dimensionless specific angular momentum, and rational periodicity. The period correction combines a direct contribution at fixed energy with compensation from the energy adjustment that preserves periodicity. Their competition produces sign changes across orbital families and reference paths. In a two-parameter scan, negative period corrections are dominated by energy compensation for sampled prograde orbits and by the direct contribution for sampled retrograde orbits. We locate $C_T=0$ boundaries, where the coefficient of the first-order fractional period correction vanishes. Circumferential shifts can differ in sign from coordinate shifts. Numerical kludge waveforms at leading quadrupole order and their discrete spectra connect these corrections to phase accumulation and harmonic frequency shifts. At fixed dimensionless orbital parameters and physical mass of the small body, this phase contribution can be reproduced to first order by rescaling the Kerr mass. For the $C_T=0$ orbit examined here, this contribution is suppressed at equal black hole masses, while corrections to the waveform and spectral amplitudes remain nonzero.

</details>


### [52] [Attractors and initial-condition sensitivity in Horndeski cosmologies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36036)
*Yulia A. Ageeva,Andrey K. Sharofeev*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates the dynamical stability and attractor behavior of homogeneous cosmological solutions—specifically Genesis and contracting models—focusing on background convergence, sensitivity to initial scalar velocity, and parametrization dependence; it shows that background attraction does not guarantee physical viability, especially when perturbative instability coexists with trajectory convergence.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether Genesis and contracting cosmological solutions are physically viable by examining their dynamical robustness—particularly whether nearby homogeneous trajectories converge (attractor behavior) and how uncertainties in late-time scalar field velocity propagate to inferred initial conditions.

Method: Analytical and dynamical systems analysis of homogeneous cosmological backgrounds; examination of phase-space flow near Genesis and power-law contracting solutions; study of sensitivity of initial scalar velocity to late-time observational uncertainty; inclusion of full gravitational dynamics in stability analysis.

Result: 1) Convergence of homogeneous trajectories can occur even for backgrounds with unstable perturbations—hence attractor behavior at the background level is insufficient for physical viability. 2) In the contracting model, one power-law solution acts as an attractor, another as a repeller; their relative dominance depends on parametrization of the cosmological state. 3) Including gravitational dynamics significantly alters the mapping between late-time scalar velocity uncertainty and inferred initial velocity in Genesis models.

Conclusion: Background-level attractor analysis alone cannot establish physical viability of early-universe cosmologies; perturbative stability, parametrization dependence, and coupling to gravity must be jointly considered—especially for singularity-avoiding scenarios like Genesis and smooth contraction.

Abstract: We study whether nearby homogeneous cosmological histories approach or move away from the Genesis and contracting solutions. We also determine how an uncertainty in the scalar velocity at a later stage affects the inferred initial velocity. In the Genesis models, including the gravitational dynamics can substantially change this relation. We find that convergence of homogeneous solutions can occur even around backgrounds with unstable perturbations, and therefore attraction of the background is not sufficient to establish that a cosmological solution is physically viable. In the contracting model, one power-law solution attracts nearby trajectories, whereas another repels them. The strength of these effects depends on how the cosmological state is parametrized. Their evolution through the subsequent bounce is not considered here.

</details>


### [53] [Semi-agnostic inspiral modeling: the massless scalar case at leading post-Newtonian order](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36037)
*Dario Rossi,Leonardo Gualtieri,Thomas P. Sotiriou*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: Proposes a semi-agnostic, post-Newtonian framework for black hole binary inspirals beyond GR, using a massless scalar field and scaling in l/L; yields a compact, universal parameterization for multi-source tests and targeted field searches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a theory-flexible yet physically grounded framework for testing gravity beyond General Relativity in black hole binary inspirals—without committing to a specific modified gravity theory, but retaining predictive power via controlled expansions.

Method: Develops a semi-agnostic effective model based on fixed field content (metric + massless scalar), expands dynamics in post-Newtonian order and in the small ratio l/L (where l sets the scale of GR deviations and L is the system size), and performs dimensional and scaling analysis of interaction terms and source properties.

Result: Identifies a minimal set of universal, dimensionless parameters that fully characterize the leading-order scalar-mediated modifications to the inspiral waveform; these parameters govern deviations in energy flux and orbital phasing.

Conclusion: The resulting parametrization is robust, theory-agnostic at the level of Lagrangian structure, and directly applicable to gravitational-wave data analysis—enabling joint constraints across events and targeted searches for light scalar fields.

Abstract: We introduce a semi-agnostic modeling framework for black hole binary inspirals beyond general relativity that fixes the field content without selecting a particular theory. We expand in the post-Newtonian order and in powers of $l/L$, where $l$ is the characteristic length-scale that controls deviations from General Relativity and $L$ is the characteristic length-scale of the system. We focus on the case of a massless scalar field coupled with the metric, at leading post-Newtonian order. Taking into account the scaling with $l/L$ of both the interaction terms and the quantities that characterise the sources, we find that the inspiral can be described in terms of a small number of universal parameters. Such a parametrisation is suitable for obtaining combined bounds from multiple sources and performing searches for specific fields.

</details>


### [54] [Fresh Look at Shift Symmetric Modified Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36063)
*Rodrigo Calderon,Eric V. Linder*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates that shift-symmetric Horndeski gravity—previously thought incapable of crossing the phantom divide (w = −1)—can in fact do so, both analytically and numerically; it provides a dynamical systems analysis identifying stable, ghost-free regions, fixed points, attractors, and two distinct mechanisms for phantom divide crossing while preserving shift symmetry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To challenge the conventional belief that shift-symmetric Horndeski gravity cannot cross the phantom divide (w = −1), and to explore its viability as a quantum-protected, well-posed dark energy theory.

Method: Analytic derivation and numerical verification combined with comprehensive dynamical systems analysis: classification of ghost-free and stable parameter subspaces, identification of fixed points and dynamical attractors, and geometric analysis of the w = −1 curve’s intersection with phase space structures.

Result: Proof that shift-symmetric Horndeski gravity can cross the phantom divide; characterization of cosmological evolution paths within stable/ghost-free regions; identification of two distinct dynamical mechanisms enabling w = −1 crossing while preserving shift symmetry.

Conclusion: Shift-symmetric Horndeski gravity is a viable, theoretically robust framework for phantom-divide-crossing cosmologies, reconciling quantum protection, classical stability, and observational flexibility.

Abstract: Shift symmetric Horndeski gravity can be a simple, well posed theory with protection against quantum corrections. Overturning conventional thought that such a theory cannot cross the phantom divide ($w=-1$) we present both analytic and numerical proof that it can. We carry out a detailed dynamical systems analysis showing the evolution of ghost free and stable regions within the model family, identify the fixed point and dynamical attractor structure, and derive the interaction of the $w=-1$ curve with these phase space structures. This reveals two mechanisms for cosmologies crossing the phantom divide while preserving shift symmetry.

</details>


### [55] [Scalarized Einstein-Maxwell black holes in external scalar fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36085)
*Lang Cheng,Guangzhou Guo,Yuhan Li,Peng Wang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies static black holes in Einstein-Maxwell-scalar theory under external scalar fields, mapping existence domains and scalar charge behavior; it reveals how external fields modify RN black hole scalarization thresholds, induce scalar charges below critical values, create distinct solutions under identical external conditions, and produce mutual scalar induction in binaries—including non-monotonic charge evolution and multi-equilibrium configurations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how external scalar fields influence black hole scalarization, existence domains, and scalar charge acquisition—particularly beyond the zero-source threshold—and to explore emergent phenomena in binary systems due to mutual scalar field coupling.

Method: Analytical and numerical construction of static black hole solutions in Einstein-Maxwell-scalar theory with prescribed asymptotic scalar fields; systematic mapping of existence domains in parameter space (charge-to-mass ratio, external field amplitude); analysis of scalar charge dependence on external field sign/magnitude; extension to binary systems via leading-order companion-generated scalar field coupling.

Result: A positive external scalar field enables scalar charge acquisition in sub-threshold RN black holes and broadens the allowed charge-to-mass range; a negative external field introduces a finite lower existence boundary and supports multiple distinct black hole solutions under identical external conditions; in binaries, mutual scalar induction leads to non-monotonic scalar charge evolution with separation for same-sign charges, and multiple equilibrium branches—some terminating before merger—for opposite-sign charges.

Conclusion: External scalar fields fundamentally reshape black hole scalarization phenomenology: they act as catalysts below intrinsic thresholds, break uniqueness in solution space, and induce rich binary dynamics—including bifurcations and equilibrium limitations—unattainable in isolated or zero-source scenarios.

Abstract: We construct static black holes in Einstein-Maxwell-scalar theory under prescribed external scalar fields, mapping their existence domain and determining how their scalar charges vary with the external field. Focusing on solutions with positive scalar charge, we find that a positive external field induces scalar charge in Reissner-Nordström (RN) black holes below the zero-source scalarization threshold and extends the allowed charge-to-mass range. A negative external field instead introduces a finite lower existence boundary and permits distinct black holes under identical external conditions. Coupling these isolated responses through the leading companion-generated scalar fields, we find mutual scalar induction in binaries whose components are RN black holes in isolation. For binaries with scalar charges of the same sign, their scalar charges need not increase as the separation decreases, while some binaries with opposite scalar charges admit multiple equilibria and cannot continue along their original equilibrium branches to smaller separations.

</details>


### [56] [A gaussian-process reconstruction of gravitational-wave friction from dark standard sirens](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36087)
*Michele Mancarella,Viola De Renzis,Alessandro Agapito*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a non-parametric Gaussian process method to reconstruct anomalous gravitational-wave friction (α_M(z)) and its integrated effect on the GW luminosity distance ratio Ξ(z), using dark standard sirens from GWTC-5.0; it finds no deviation from general relativity, with α_M = 0 and Ξ = 1 fully consistent with data, and introduces interpretable function-space diagnostics for robust model comparison.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test whether gravitational waves propagate as predicted by general relativity on cosmological scales, particularly in light of modified gravity theories that introduce anomalous friction (encoded in α_M(z)) altering GW amplitude evolution and yielding a GW luminosity distance distinct from the electromagnetic one.

Method: Non-parametric reconstruction of the friction function α_M(z) using Gaussian processes (GP), with explicit integration to obtain the GW-to-EM luminosity-distance ratio Ξ(z); enforces monotonicity and invertibility of the GW luminosity distance; jointly infers source population, modified propagation, and cosmological parameters using GWTC-5.0; includes function-space diagnostics—prior calibration, prior-to-posterior contraction, degeneracy summaries, and principal-component analysis—to assess data-informed vs. prior-driven features.

Result: No evidence for deviations from GR: posterior distributions of α_M(z) and Ξ(z) are consistent with zero and unity, respectively, across the redshift range covered by GWTC-5.0; results are robust under variations in GP kernel, smoothness/amplitude priors, resolution, and likelihood treatment.

Conclusion: General relativity remains consistent with current dark standard siren data; the proposed non-parametric GP framework—augmented with interpretable function-space diagnostics—provides a rigorous, prior-aware, and flexible methodology for testing GW propagation beyond parametric assumptions.

Abstract: Standard sirens provide a direct probe of the cosmic distance-redshift relation and therefore a powerful test of whether gravitational waves (GWs) propagate as predicted by general relativity on cosmological scales. In many extensions of general relativity, GW propagation is modified by an anomalous friction term, which alters the amplitude evolution and leads to a GW luminosity distance that differs from its electromagnetic counterpart. In this work, we develop a non-parametric reconstruction of this anomalous friction from dark standard sirens. We reconstruct its redshift dependence, encoded in the friction function $\alpha_M(z)$, with a Gaussian process (GP) and derive the corresponding GW-to-EM luminosity-distance ratio $\Xi(z)$ by integration, explicitly retaining the relation between the local friction modification and its accumulated effect on the observed distance, while enforcing a monotonic and invertible GW luminosity distance. We apply the method to the GWTC-5.0 catalog, jointly inferring the source population, modified propagation, and background cosmological parameters. We find no evidence for departures from GR, with $\alpha_M=0$ and $\Xi=1$ consistent with the posterior over the redshift range probed by the catalog. The result is stable under variations of the GP kernel, smoothness and amplitude priors, resolution prescription, and likelihood treatment. We further introduce function-space diagnostics for comparing flexible and parametric reconstructions, including prior calibration, prior-to-posterior contraction, common summaries of propagation-population degeneracies, and principal-component analysis. These tools make explicit which features are informed by the data and which are imposed by the function-space prior, providing a framework for interpretable non-parametric tests of gravitational-wave propagation.

</details>


### [57] [Improved Gauge Conditions for Large Expansion Cosmological Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36123)
*Fan Zhang,Lee Lindblom*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: Introduces a novel gauge condition enabling stable, long-duration numerical evolutions of Einstein's equations on cosmological spacetimes with non-trivial spatial topologies—achieving spatial volume expansions exceeding 10⁶×.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome numerical instabilities that prevent reliable long-term evolution of Einstein's equations on compact cosmological manifolds with non-trivial topology, which are physically relevant for global structure and cosmic microwave background analyses.

Method: Design and implementation of a new gauge condition for the 3+1 ADM formalism; numerical testing via long-duration evolutions of homogeneous and isotropic initial data on three compact manifolds: Sixth-Turn space, lens space L(5,2), and Seifert fiber space SFS[S²:(2,1)(2,1)(2,−1)].

Result: Demonstrated stable evolutions with spatial volumes increasing by over six orders of magnitude; confirmed robustness across all three topologically distinct compact spatial manifolds.

Conclusion: The proposed gauge condition significantly extends the feasibility and reliability of numerical relativity simulations on cosmologically realistic, topologically nontrivial spacetimes.

Abstract: This study develops a new gauge condition for performing long duration numerical evolutions of Einstein's equation on cosmological spacetimes with non-trivial spatial topologies. This new gauge makes it possible to perform reliable numerical evolutions on these manifolds in which the spatial volumes become more than one million times larger. This new condition was tested for this study by evolving homogeneous and isotropic initial data on exemplar manifolds with three spatial topologies: Sixth-Turn space, the lens space L(5,2), and the Seifert fiber space SFS[S2:(2,1)(2,1)(2,-1)].

</details>


### [58] [Unified Dynamics of Black-Hole Interiors: From Geometric to Scalar-Kinetic Dominance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36129)
*Fu-Guo Yang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper establishes a unified dynamical framework linking small-amplitude scalar field inputs at black hole event horizons to scalar–Kasner-type interior geometries, identifying area contraction as the key physical driver of the transition from geometric to scalar kinetic dominance—differently manifested in Schwarzschild-like (smooth suppression of curvature) versus Kerr–Newman-like (strong gradient amplification near the Cauchy-horizon zero) branches; numerical solutions across multiple black hole families corroborate the mechanism.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve how scalar fields sourced near the event horizon dynamically shape black hole interiors—particularly the onset of scalar–Kasner behavior and inner-horizon instability—and to unify disparate behaviors across black hole families under a common geometric mechanism.

Method: Analytical dynamical systems analysis within boundary-supported stationary scalar–gravity families, combined with fully coupled numerical relativity simulations of four-dimensional axisymmetric black hole interiors (Schwarzschild-like, Kerr–Newman, Reissner–Nordström, Kerr). Key diagnostics include metric behavior, scalar gradient evolution, and curvature–kinetic term competition near inner horizons.

Result: Area contraction is identified as the universal driver of the geometric-to-scalar-kinetic dominance transition; distinct branching behavior emerges: Schwarzschild-like interiors exhibit smooth kinetic dominance with small gradients, while Kerr–Newman-like interiors show strong gradient amplification toward a Cauchy-horizon zero followed by a narrow kinetic-dominant transition; cancellation between kinetic and geometric terms yields a positive minimum in a metric diagnostic, dynamically explaining inner-horizon loss.

Conclusion: A unified dynamical picture—centered on area contraction and modulated by the presence and location of a would-be Cauchy-horizon zero—explains scalar–Kasner interior formation and inner-horizon destabilization across stationary black hole families, reconciling analytical structure with numerical evidence.
