Abstract: We identify a unified dynamical picture connecting small-amplitude scalar input at the event horizon to scalar--Kasner interiors. Within boundary-supported stationary families, area contraction drives the transition from geometric to scalar kinetic dominance. For the Schwarzschild-like branch, contraction suppresses the relative curvature contribution, so the scalar kinetic term becomes dominant while the gradient remains small. For the Kerr--Newman-like branch, the scalar gradient is strongly amplified as area contraction carries the solution toward the would-be Cauchy-horizon zero, followed by a narrow transition to scalar kinetic dominance. Cancellation between the kinetic and geometric terms produces a positive minimum of a metric diagnostic, explaining inner-horizon loss dynamically. Fully coupled numerical solutions for the Schwarzschild-like branch and four-dimensional stationary axisymmetric Kerr--Newman interiors, including the Reissner--Nordström and Kerr limits, support this picture. Area contraction provides the common physical mechanism, while the would-be Cauchy-horizon zero determines the strength of gradient amplification and the localization of the transition.

</details>


### [59] [Non-Perturbative Approach to Gravitational Particle Production in the Inflation-Kination Transition: Surveying a Wide Range of the Parameter Space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36137)
*Amirsalar Nikandish,Shiva Rostam Zadeh,Nematollah Riazi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies gravitational particle production (GPP) as a reheating mechanism in an inflation-kination transition background, using both numerical non-perturbative (adiabatic vacuum) and analytical methods; it quantifies how the scalar field’s bare mass, non-conformal gravitational coupling constant, and transition time jointly determine the reheating temperature—finding numerically that the maximal reheating temperature is sufficient for Hot Big-Bang onset.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically assess the impact of the non-conformal Ricci-matter coupling (beyond just field mass) on GPP-driven reheating in inflation-kination transitions, filling a gap in prior perturbative and adiabatic-vacuum treatments.

Method: Numerical implementation of a modified non-perturbative adiabatic vacuum formalism applied to a continuous scale-factor model for inflation-to-kination transition; supplemented by analytical treatment in a simplified solvable model for cross-validation and mathematical insight.

Result: The reheating temperature depends sensitively and non-trivially on three parameters: bare scalar mass, non-conformal coupling ξ, and transition time; full 3D parameter-space scan yields a maximal reheating temperature adequate for thermalization and Hot Big-Bang initiation.

Conclusion: Gravitational particle production can robustly reheat the universe after inflation-kination transition even for superheavy scalars, provided realistic values of ξ and transition dynamics are considered; the non-conformal coupling plays a decisive, previously underexplored role in enhancing GPP efficiency.

Abstract: In this study, we investigate the gravitational particle production (GPP) as a natural reheating channel in time-dependent backgrounds. We focus on reheating in an inflation-kination background. To maintain generality across inflationary models, we use a continuous scale-factor function that produces this transition and has been proposed in the literature. In this scenario, the gravitational reheating channel arises from the gravitational excitation of a superheavy spectator scalar field with Ricci-matter gravitational coupling. Besides all the perturbative approaches, a recently proposed non-perturbative one that uses the notion of the adiabatic vacuum has also been applied to the case of superheavy particles. Although the effect of the mass of the field on the reheating temperature has been investigated, the impact of the non-conformal values of the gravitational coupling constant on the GPP rate is not considered. Hence, we modify the non-perturbative approach in such a way that both effects can be traced. Three parameters affect the reheating temperature in this case: the bare mass of the field, the gravitational coupling constant, and the transition time. In this paper, the effect of each of these parameters alone on the resultant reheating temperature, as well as the combined effects of these parameters, is studied. Taking into account all involved parameters and surveying a wide range of the 3D parameter space, the highest reheating temperature possible is obtained. All of the aforementioned investigations are done based on numerical calculations, and we obtain that the highest possible reheating temperature is quite sufficient for the universe to enter the Hot Big-Bang era. For the sake of completeness, we study the GPP in such a transition using an analytical method in an independent, parallel, solvable model. This analysis is presented only as a mathematical tool.

</details>


### [60] [On the Interpretation of Tests of the Horizon Area Law](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36162)
*Selma Vangstein,Christopher J. Moore*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates that gravitational-wave tests of the horizon area law—often interpreted as consistency checks of general relativity—are model-dependent and can be passed by exotic compact objects (e.g., boson stars) without event horizons, undermining their reliability as horizon diagnostics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To challenge the interpretation of gravitational-wave area-law tests as robust confirmations of black hole event horizons and general relativity, by exposing their model dependence on GR-based waveform templates.

Method: The authors simulate gravitational-wave signals from merging boson stars (exotic compact objects lacking event horizons), inject them into synthetic data, and apply standard GR-based area-law tests (comparing initial and final horizon areas inferred from ringdown and inspiral parameters).

Result: Boson star mergers mimic black hole signals closely enough to pass the area-law test with ~92% confidence—despite having no event horizons—revealing a critical degeneracy between horizon-bearing and horizonless models under GR-based inference.

Conclusion: Area-law tests are not horizon-specific diagnostics; they are sensitive to the assumed GR template family, not intrinsic horizon physics. Their success does not confirm the existence of event horizons or validate GR in strong-field regimes beyond the template class.

Abstract: This Letter investigates gravitational-wave tests of the horizon area law. Although they provide a striking consistency check of general relativity (GR), these tests rely on models that assume GR, making their interpretation inherently model dependent. We show with a specific example---simulations of merging boson stars---that exotic compact objects can mimic merging black holes and pass an area-law test at a confidence as high as $\sim 92\%$ despite not even having event horizons.

</details>


### [61] [The Symbiosis of Vainshtein and Chameleon Screening](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36166)
*Sergi Sirera,Tessa Baker,Clare Burrage,James Hallam,Krishna Naidoo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates the coexistence of Vainshtein and Chameleon screening mechanisms in cosmic structures, revealing mutual suppression effects that reshape observable gravitational signatures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Realistic dark-energy models may activate multiple screening mechanisms simultaneously, but their interplay has been overlooked despite its importance for modified gravity viability and cosmological tests.

Method: Theoretical analysis of how Vainshtein and Chameleon screening mechanisms interact in cosmic structures, focusing on their mutual influence on screening radii and structural pathologies.

Result: Chameleon screening resolves the Galileon void pathology, suppresses or eliminates the Vainshtein radius around massive sources, and the Vainshtein term shrinks the Chameleon's screened core.

Conclusion: Coexisting Vainshtein and Chameleon screening significantly alter key observables used in cosmological gravity tests, necessitating joint treatment in model building and data analysis.

Abstract: We present the first study of coexisting Vainshtein and Chameleon screening in cosmic structures. Screening hides fifth forces locally, keeping modified gravity viable. Realistic dark-energy models can activate several mechanisms at once, yet this interplay has been overlooked. We find that (i) the Chameleon resolves the long-standing Galileon void pathology; (ii) it suppresses or eliminates the Vainshtein radius around massive sources; and (iii) the Vainshtein term shrinks the Chameleon's screened core. These effects reshape the observable signatures central to cosmological tests of gravity.

</details>


### [62] [Testing Conformal Weyl Gravity with Plasma Modified Black Hole Shadows and EHT Constraints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36562)
*Zhichen Guan,Connor McMillin,Owen Gartlan,William Horner,Leo Rodriguez,Shanshan Rodriguez*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes black hole shadows in fourth-order conformal Weyl gravity (CWG) with charge, rotation, and a de Sitter background immersed in plasma, distinguishing plasma-induced effects from intrinsic gravitational theory effects—finding that plasma shrinks the shadow in both CWG and GR, whereas charge enlarges it in CWG but shrinks it in GR, enabling observational discrimination.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To disentangle observational signatures arising from dispersive plasma environments from those intrinsic to the underlying gravitational theory—specifically, fourth-order conformal Weyl gravity—using black hole shadow morphology and photon deflection.

Method: Uses a separable Hamilton-Jacobi formalism for null geodesics in a charged, rotating, de Sitter CWG spacetime immersed in cold, unmagnetized plasma; derives radial/angular photon potentials, finite-distance equatorial deflection angle, and observer-frame shadow boundary accounting for asymptotic non-flatness; compares predictions with GR and EHT shadow size constraints for Sgr A* and M87*.

Result: Plasma strength universally reduces shadow radius, signed deflection angle, and energy emission peak in both CWG and GR; electric charge q has opposite effects: increases CWG shadow radius but decreases GR shadow radius for representative parameters; lensing behavior corroborates geometry-dependent photon region structure; finite-observer dimensionless shadow radius is compatible with EHT size intervals when plasma and charge are jointly tuned.

Conclusion: Plasma effects can partially mimic gravitational theory-induced shadow shifts, but the qualitatively distinct (opposite) response of the shadow radius to charge in CWG versus GR provides a robust observational discriminant between conformal gravity and general relativity, especially in plasma-rich astrophysical environments.

Abstract: We study the shadows of a charged rotating de Sitter solution in fourth order conformal Weyl gravity (CWG) immersed in plasma and analyze the joint charge-plasma constraints. Our main goal is to distinguish observational effects caused by the dispersive plasma environment from those caused by the underlying gravitational theory. Within a separable Hamilton-Jacobi plasma model, we derive the radial and angular photon potentials, the equatorial finite-distance deflection angle, and the finite-observer shadow boundary due to the asymptotically non-flat nature of the CWG spacetime. Our results separate two effects. Increasing the plasma strength generally reduces the shadow radius, the signed deflection angle, and the energy emission peak in both CWG and GR. Charge ($q$) produces a stronger theory dependence: for the representative parameters studied, increasing $q$ enlarges the CWG shadow but reduces the GR shadow. The lensing behavior independently supports the geometry-dependent response found from the photon region and shadow observables. We further compare the finite observer dimensionless shadow radius with EHT-inferred size intervals for Sgr~A$^*$ and M87$^*$. Our results indicate that plasma effects can mimic part of the shadow size shift, but the distinct charge response of CWG offers a potential observational route for separating conformal gravity signatures from environmental propagation effects.

</details>


### [63] [Reconstruction of the Riemann tensor from causal order and element counts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36695)
*Nathan Madsen*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops methods to recover continuum geometric quantities—normal coordinates, parallel transport, and the full Riemann tensor—from causal sets (discrete partially ordered sets), proving that curvature estimation error vanishes with increasing sampling density under uniform counting assumptions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge causal set theory—a discrete quantum gravity approach—with continuum spacetime physics by recovering general relativistic geometric structures from purely combinatorial and counting data.

Method: Constructs approximate normal coordinates using causal order and element counts; estimates parallel transport via geodesic approximations in the causal set; recovers the Riemann tensor through local volume and counting ratios; proves error bounds using uniform sampling assumptions in a fixed smooth spacetime.

Result: A provably convergent algorithm for reconstructing the full Riemann curvature tensor from causal set data; establishes a uniform bound on curvature estimation error that vanishes as sampling density increases.

Conclusion: Causal sets encode sufficient information to reconstruct local spacetime geometry—including full curvature—with quantifiable, controllable error, supporting their viability as a fundamental discrete model of spacetime.

Abstract: A causal set is a locally finite partially ordered set. Causal set theory proposes such discrete structures as a basis for spacetime physics. Connecting this description with continuum physics requires recovering the geometric quantities used in general relativity and controlling the errors introduced by discreteness. Here, with dimension and sampling density specified, we construct approximate normal coordinates, estimate parallel transport, and recover the full Riemann tensor from causal order and element counts. For samples embedded in a fixed smooth spacetime and satisfying stated uniform counting assumptions, we prove a uniform curvature error bound that tends to zero as the sampling density increases.

</details>


### [64] [Improved constraints on higher-derivative effective field theory extensions of general relativity with GW230627_015337](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36728)
*Tao Zhu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper tests general relativity in the strong-field regime using gravitational-wave observations of binary black holes, incorporating higher-curvature effective field theory corrections into a state-of-the-art waveform model (SEOBNRv5HM_ROM), and places tight Bayesian constraints on three EFT couplings—consistent with GR—with one-sided 90% upper limits on characteristic length scales at ~10–20 km.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe general relativity in the strong-field, dynamical regime via gravitational-wave observations of binary black holes, specifically testing effective field theory extensions involving parity-even cubic- and quartic-curvature operators that modify inspiral dynamics.

Method: Incorporated leading-order phase corrections from two 5PN/6PN cubic-curvature and one 8PN quartic-curvature EFT couplings into the SEOBNRv5HM_ROM waveform using a flexible, theory-independent framework; performed Fisher-matrix forecasts on 92 BBH events from LVK catalogs, selected GW230627_015337 for full Bayesian inference with higher-order modes and IMR signal.

Result: All three EFT couplings are consistent with zero at 90% credibility; one-sided 90% upper limits on characteristic length scales are 17.1 km, 11.1 km, and 18.3 km; log Bayes factors vs. GR range from −5.49 to −2.51, disfavoring the EFT models.

Conclusion: The results provide the numerically tightest current constraints on these higher-curvature EFT operators, reinforcing GR’s validity in the strong-field regime; bounds reach ~10–20 km length scales, improving upon representative prior inspiral and ringdown analyses, though model and prior differences must be considered.

Abstract: Gravitational-wave (GW) observations of binary black holes provide a powerful probe of general relativity (GR) in the strong-field regime. In effective field theory (EFT) extensions of GR, higher-curvature operators modify the binary dynamics and introduce corrections to the inspiral phase. In this work, we consider two parity-even cubic-curvature couplings and one quartic-curvature coupling, whose leading phase corrections enter at 5PN, 6PN, and 8PN order, respectively. We incorporate these corrections into the individual multipoles of the \texttt{SEOBNRv5HM\_ROM} waveform using the flexible theory-independent framework, allowing higher-order modes and the full inspiral-merger-ringdown signal to be used in the analysis. We first perform a Fisher-matrix analysis of 92 binary-black-hole events selected from a parent sample of 273 events in the public LIGO-Virgo-KAGRA catalogs. GW230627\_015337 provides the tightest forecast constraints on all three EFT couplings and is therefore selected for a full Bayesian analysis. The posterior distributions are consistent with GR, with all three couplings consistent with zero at 90\% credibility. The corresponding one-sided 90\% upper limits on the characteristic length scales are \(17.1\), \(11.1\), and \(18.3\,\mathrm{km}\). The log Bayes factors of the three EFT models relative to GR range from \(-5.49\) to \(-2.51\), favoring GR under the adopted priors. These bounds probe higher-curvature length scales at the level of \(10\)--\(20\,\mathrm{km}\) and are numerically tighter than representative previous inspiral and ringdown constraints, although differences in the models and analysis assumptions should be taken into account.

</details>


### [65] [Impact of deformation parameters on the greybody factor and Joule-Thomson expansions of Schwarzschild-AdS black hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36730)
*Faisal Javed,A. Eid,Arfa Waseem,Jiafeng Guo,Abdelmalek Bouzenada,Sulaman Shaukat,Farruh Atamurotov,N. Mustapha*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies greybody factors and Joule-Thomson expansion for deformed Schwarzschild-AdS black holes, deriving the effective potential via tortoise-coordinate-transformed Klein-Gordon equation and analyzing its dependence on deformation, mass, and angular momentum; it finds deformation enhances the potential and alters wave penetration behavior near the horizon, and shows deformation and mass increase both the cooling/heating zones in isenthalpic curves.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how spacetime deformation affects quantum field propagation (via greybody factors) and thermodynamic response (via Joule-Thomson expansion) in Schwarzschild-AdS black holes.

Method: Constructed Schrödinger-like wave equation from Klein-Gordon equation using tortoise coordinates; derived and analyzed exact effective potential as function of deformation parameter, mass, and angular momentum; computed greybody transmission/reflection behavior; performed thermodynamic analysis of Joule-Thomson expansion, including isenthalpic curve construction and parameter dependence.

Result: Deformation parameter increases effective potential, enhancing scattering and reducing absorption of massless scalar fields; higher-frequency waves penetrate near-horizon potential barriers more readily than lower-frequency ones; deformation and mass enlarge the cooling and heating regions in Joule-Thomson isenthalpic curves.

Conclusion: Spacetime deformation significantly modifies both semi-classical wave dynamics and black hole thermodynamics in Schwarzschild-AdS geometry, with measurable impacts on greybody factors and Joule-Thomson behavior.

Abstract: This paper investigates the greybody factor and Joule-Thomson expansion of deformed Schwarzschild-AdS black hole models. We constructed the Klein-Gordon equation into a Schrödinger-like wave equation using tortoise coordinates and derived the exact effective potential. Also, we analyzed its behavior under varying physical parameters, including deformation parameters, mass and angular momentum. In this case, our results illustrate that increasing the deformation parameter enhances the effective potential, significantly impacting the absorption and scattering of massless scalar fields. It is noted that the Schwarzschild black hole effective potential is greatly increased when the deformation parameter is present. Near the event horizon, we found that waves behave differently, with higher-frequency waves more easily penetrating potential obstacles and lower-frequency waves bouncing off them. Furthermore, we investigated the thermodynamic characteristics of black holes using the Joule-Thomson expansion, detecting isenthalpic curves that indicate heating and cooling zones controlled by pressure and temperature changes. In this context, we tested the influence of deformation black hole parameters; the isenthalpic curves are enhanced by increasing the deformation parameters and the black hole mass.

</details>


### [66] [Novel topological subclasses of a magnetic black hole in causal nonlinear electrodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36796)
*Qi-Qi Liang,De-jiang Yin,Yu-Die Wan,Zheng-Wen Long*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper applies topological defect theory to magnetic black holes in Einstein gravity with causal nonlinear electrodynamics, identifying novel thermodynamic topological subclasses (including a new $\overline{W}^{0+\leftrightarrow1-}$) and demonstrating a boundary-induced thermodynamic topological phase transition signaled by a change in global winding number $W$ from 0 to −1.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To classify thermodynamic phase structure of black holes using topological defect theory in parameter space, especially for static asymptotically flat magnetic black holes where conventional methods may obscure global topological features.

Method: Uses generalized off-shell free energy and Duan's $\phi$-mapping topological current theory to analyze the canonical ensemble; constructs defect curves in thermodynamic parameter space and tracks evolution of topological charge (winding number $W$) as auxiliary inverse temperature varies, with attention to physical horizon boundaries.

Result: Identifies four defect-curve profiles, including a new thermodynamic topological subclass $\overline{W}^{0+\leftrightarrow1-}$ with paired generation/annihilation points and unstable innermost/outermost branches; observes a boundary-induced transition where an unstable small-black-hole defect enters through the lower physical boundary, changing $W$ from 0 to −1 — signaling a thermodynamic topological phase transition.

Conclusion: The physical horizon boundary plays a decisive role in thermodynamic topological classification; identical endpoint behavior and winding-number sequences can arise from distinct defect evolutions, underscoring the necessity of boundary-aware topological analysis; this black-hole family serves as a rich arena for discovering further defect structures and thermodynamic topological phase transitions.

Abstract: By treating black-hole states as topological defects in thermodynamic parameter space, we investigate a static, asymptotically flat magnetic black holes in Einstein gravity coupled to causal nonlinear electrodynamics. Using the generalized off-shell free energy and Duan's $\phi$-mapping topological current theory, we analyze the physical single-horizon sector in the canonical ensemble. We identify four representative defect-curve profiles, including realizations of the conjectured $W^{0+\leftrightarrow1-}$ class and $\overline{W}^{1-}$ subclass, as well as a new thermodynamic topological subclass, $\overline{W}^{0+\leftrightarrow1-}$. This new subclass features paired generation and annihilation points, with unstable innermost and outermost branches. As the auxiliary inverse temperature increases, the system evolves from two equilibrium states to three and then back to one, with an unstable small-black-hole defect entering through the physical lower boundary and changing the global topological number from $W=0$ to $W=-1$. This boundary-induced change signals a thermodynamic topological phase transition. These results show that the same inverse-temperature endpoint behavior and winding-number sequence from small to large black holes can accompany different defect evolution, highlighting the role of the physical horizon boundary in thermodynamic topological classification. More broadly, this causal nonlinear-electrodynamics black-hole family provides a setting for discovering further defect structures and thermodynamic topological phase transitions.

</details>


### [67] [Realistic neutron stars in the constant-Q branch of f(Q) gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36814)
*Debadri Bhattacharjee,Pradip Kumar Chattopadhyay,P. H. R. S. Moraes*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper explores neutron star structure in symmetric teleparallel gravity (f(Q) theory), focusing on constant-non-metricity (Q = Q_*) models. It introduces three new f(Q) extensions—Born-Infeld–, Hu–Sawicki–, and Tsujikawa-inspired—and solves modified TOV equations with realistic EoSs (DD2, SLy4). Results show trade-offs: first two models fit low-mass radii but cap max mass below ~2.43 M⊙; the third achieves ~3.36 M⊙, potentially explaining GW190814’s heavy secondary, but overpredicts radii. The work highlights phenomenological complementarity between radius and maximum-mass constraints for testing non-metricity gravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test viable alternatives to general relativity using neutron stars as extreme gravity laboratories, specifically within symmetric teleparallel gravity where gravity is sourced by non-metricity Q—not curvature or torsion—and to assess whether nonlinear f(Q) models can simultaneously satisfy multimessenger constraints (e.g., GW170817 radii, GW190814 massive component).

Method: Adopts the metric-affine formulation of f(Q) gravity; restricts to the regular, constant-Q branch (Q = Q_*) to ensure central regularity and retain functional dependence via f(Q_*) and derivatives. Proposes three analytic f(Q) models: Born-Infeld–like, Hu–Sawicki–like, and Tsujikawa–like. Solves the modified Tolman-Oppenheimer-Volkoff equations numerically using unified realistic EoSs (GPPVA(DD2), SLy4); evaluates stability via the Harrison-Zel'dovich-Novikov criterion (dM/dρ_c > 0).

Result: Born-Infeld and Hu–Sawicki analogues recover GR in high-control-parameter limit and reproduce observed low-mass NS radii (<2.0 M⊙), but yield maximum masses ≤2.43 M⊙ (DD2) and ≤2.06 M⊙ (SLy4). Tsujikawa-inspired model strongly stiffens effective gravity, yielding up to ~3.36 M⊙ and accommodating GW190814’s 2.59 M⊙ secondary, yet overpredicts radii for typical NSs. Clear radius–maximum-mass complementarity emerges across models.

Conclusion: The constant-Q f(Q) framework exhibits robust phenomenological trade-offs: models fitting radius constraints tend to underpredict maximum mass, and vice versa. Multimessenger observations (X-ray binaries, gravitational waves) are essential to break degeneracies and constrain non-linear non-metricity gravity. The Tsujikawa-inspired model demonstrates that f(Q) gravity can naturally support ∼3 M⊙ neutron stars without invoking exotic matter, challenging standard interpretations of massive compact objects.

Abstract: We investigate the hydrostatic equilibrium and global structure of neutron stars within symmetric teleparallel gravity, where gravitational interactions are mediated by the non-metricity scalar $Q$. Working within the metric-affine formulation, we adopt the regular, constant-$Q$ branch ($Q = Q_*$) of $f(Q)$ gravity, which preserves regularity at the stellar centre while maintaining explicit dependence on the non-linear functional form through $f(Q_*)$ and its derivatives. We propose three novel non-linear $f(Q)$ extensions: (i) a Born-Infeld-inspired model, (ii) a Hu--Sawicki analogue, and (iii) a Tsujikawa-inspired model. Incorporating state-of-the-art realistic unified equations of state, namely GPPVA(DD2) and SLy4, we numerically solve the modified Tolman-Oppenheimer-Volkoff equations and evaluate stellar stability via the Harrison-Zel'dovich-Novikov criterion. The Born-Infeld and Hu-Sawicki analogues smoothly recover the general relativistic limit as their control parameters increase, predicting stellar radii for low-mass objects ($M < 2.0\,M_\odot$) that match observational constraints from X-ray binaries and GW170817; however, their maximum masses remain capped below $2.43\,M_\odot$ (DD2) and $2.06\,M_\odot$ (SLy4). Conversely, the Tsujikawa-inspired model significantly stiffens the effective gravitational coupling, accommodating massive compact objects up to $\sim 3.36\,M_\odot$ and offering a viable physical origin for the $2.59_{-0.09}^{+0.08}\,M_\odot$ secondary object in GW190814, albeit at the expense of overpredicting radii for typical low-mass neutron stars. These contrasting behaviours demonstrate a key phenomenological complementarity between maximum mass and radius bounds within the constant-$Q$ framework, highlighting the utility of multimessenger observations in constraining non-linear non-metricity gravity.

</details>


### [68] [Primordial black holes from a type-IIB/F theory inflaton potential](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36863)
*Chitrak Sarkar,Amitava Choudhuri,Buddhadeb Ghosh*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates primordial black hole (PBH) formation via an ultra-slow-roll inflationary mechanism induced by a localized Gaussian bump in a Kähler modulus within the large-volume scenario of type-IIB string theory; it predicts enhanced curvature power at k ~ 1.12×10⁹ Mpc⁻¹, yielding PBHs of mass ~10⁻⁶ M⊙ consistent with microlensing bounds, and forecasts detectability of associated scalar-induced gravitational waves by SKA.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To realize viable PBH formation within a well-motivated top-down string-theoretic framework—specifically, the large-volume scenario of type-IIB/F-theory—with full Kähler moduli stabilization, while generating sufficient curvature perturbation enhancement on small scales without violating CMB constraints on large scales.

Method: Constructs an inflaton potential from stabilized Kähler moduli including both perturbative (α′) and non-perturbative (instanton) corrections; introduces a localized Gaussian bump in one modulus to induce ultra-slow-roll dynamics; solves the Mukhanov-Sasaki equation numerically for curvature perturbations; computes PBH abundance via Press-Schechter formalism with varying δ_c; evaluates Hawking temperature and evaporation time; compares f_PBH against current observational bounds (microlensing, SKA sensitivity).

Result: A sustained enhancement of the curvature power spectrum at k ≈ 1.12×10⁹ Mpc⁻¹ yields PBHs with mass ~10⁻⁶ M⊙, f_PBH ≈ 10⁻³–10⁻² (depending on δ_c), satisfying microlensing constraints for δ_c = 0.439; predicted Hawking temperature ~2.78×10⁻¹⁵ GeV and evaporation time ~8.5×10³⁸ Gyr; scalar-induced GW signal falls within SKA’s sensitivity band.

Conclusion: PBH formation is feasible in a fully stabilized large-volume string inflation model via controlled moduli-space perturbations; the resulting phenomenology is observationally viable and testable—particularly through upcoming SKA observations of secondary gravitational waves.

Abstract: We study the possibility of the formation of primordial black holes (PBHs) using an inflaton potential constructed by stabilizing all Kähler moduli employing both the perturbative and non-perturbative corrections in the large volume scenario in type-IIB/F string theory. We further introduce a small perturbation in one of the Kähler moduli that modifies the existing potential in the field space. The effective correction in the moduli space is made by introducing a localised Gaussian bump, which induces an ultra-slow-roll feature in the inflaton potential during the early cosmological inflation. The evolution of the amplitude of the curvature power spectrum is solved numerically by using the Mukhanov-Sasaki equation, with Bunch-Davies initial conditions. The inclusion of the Gaussian bump produces a sustainable enhancement in the amplitude of curvature power spectra around $k\sim 1.12 \times 10^{9} Mpc^{-1}$ for the formation of PBHs. The abundance of PBHs ($f_{PBH}$) is calculated using the Press-Schechter formalism for different values of critical density contrast and presented in the background of contemporary $f_{PBH}$ bounds and checked for viability with existing microlensing bounds, corresponding to a mass value $10^{-6}M_\odot$ for $\delta$ threshold value $\delta_c=0.439$. The Hawking temperature and evaporation time are also determined for these black holes and found to be of the order of $2.78\times 10^{-15}$ GeV and $8.5\times10^{38}$ Gyr, respectively. The enhancement of curvature power spectra lies in the sensitivity region of the SKA observations, suggesting an indication that scalar-induced gravity waves may be probed in future observations.

</details>


### [69] [Self-gravitating Schrödinger--Poisson Solitons in Curved Spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36923)
*Kei-ichi Maeda*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a framework to model self-gravitating Schrödinger–Poisson solitons in curved spacetime—specifically around a Kerr black hole—by incorporating tidal effects from spacetime curvature into the SP equations via an effective tidal potential; it computes the soliton’s quadrupolar deformation, Love number, tidal deformability, and associated gravitational-wave strain, assessing detectability by future space-based GW observatories.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically study how self-gravitating bosonic solitons (e.g., fuzzy dark matter candidates) respond tidally in strong-field black-hole spacetimes and assess their potentially observable gravitational-wave signatures.

Method: Uses a local inertial frame centered on the soliton in weak-field, non-relativistic limit; incorporates background curvature via an effective tidal potential in the Schrödinger–Poisson system; applies this to a soliton on a circular equatorial geodesic around a Kerr black hole; computes induced mass quadrupole, defines tidal deformability and dimensionless Love number; estimates gravitational radiation and characteristic strain using quadrupole formula.

Result: Derives analytic and numerical estimates of soliton deformation, Love number, and GW strain; identifies orbital radii and soliton sizes for which the characteristic strain falls within sensitivity bands of planned space-based detectors (e.g., LISA, DECIGO).

Conclusion: The framework enables quantitative analysis of tidal interactions between bosonic solitons and black holes, revealing that certain astrophysically plausible configurations may produce detectable continuous gravitational-wave signals with next-generation observatories.

Abstract: We formulate a description of self-gravitating Schrödinger--Poisson (SP) solitons in curved spacetime using a local inertial frame centered on the soliton. In the weak-field and non-relativistic regime, the effect of the background spacetime is incorporated into the SP equations through an effective tidal potential determined by the spacetime curvature. We apply this framework to a soliton moving along a circular equatorial geodesic around a Kerr black hole. The tidal field contains stationary and time-dependent quadrupolar components, which induce a deformation of the soliton and an associated mass quadrupole moment. We formulate the quadrupolar response in terms of the tidal deformability and the dimensionless Love number. We then estimate the gravitational radiation associated with the rotating deformed soliton and evaluate the corresponding characteristic strain for representative soliton sizes and orbital radii. A comparison with representative sensitivity curves of planned space-based gravitational-wave detectors identifies parameter regimes in which the estimated characteristic strain may lie within their sensitivity ranges. Our framework provides a systematic basis for investigating the tidal response of self-gravitating bosonic solitons in black-hole spacetimes and their potential gravitational-wave signatures.

</details>


### [70] [Can One Hear the Absence of a Singularity?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37028)
*Matteo Della Rocca,Paolo Pani,Andrea P. Sanna*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper shows that regular black hole geometries—designed to resolve GR curvature singularities—exhibit a discontinuous, nonperturbative change in their high-overtone quasinormal mode (QNM) spectrum compared to Schwarzschild, even for arbitrarily small regularization. Specifically, the real part of QNMs vanishes asymptotically, unlike singular black holes where it approaches a nonzero constant; this serves as a robust spectral signature of singularity resolution.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify observable imprints of quantum gravity-induced singularity resolution in black-hole physics, particularly via quasinormal modes — which encode dynamical and geometric properties of spacetime near horizons.

Method: Analytical monodromy method applied systematically to a broad class of regular black hole metrics, complemented by numerical verification of the asymptotic QNM spectrum in the large-overtone (n → ∞) limit.

Result: For generic regular black holes, Re(ω_n) → 0 as n → ∞, whereas for Schwarzschild and other singular black holes, Re(ω_n) → constant ≠ 0; this difference persists arbitrarily close to Schwarzschild geometry, indicating spectral instability under singularity resolution.

Conclusion: The large-overtone QNM spectrum is a sensitive probe of spacetime singularity structure: its asymptotic behavior provides a sharp, nonperturbative diagnostic for curvature singularity resolution in quantum gravity models.

Abstract: One of the central expectations of quantum gravity is the resolution of the curvature singularities predicted by General Relativity, potentially giving rise to effective black-hole geometries that are regular inside the horizon. We show that, for a very broad class of regular black holes including several known solutions, the quasinormal-mode spectrum in the large-overtone limit differs discontinuously from that of a Schwarzschild black hole, even when the regularization parameter is arbitrarily small. The real part of the quasinormal frequencies vanishes asymptotically, rather than approaching a nonzero constant, as generically occurs in General Relativity and other theories predicting black holes with curvature singularities. The asymptotic black-hole spectrum thus retains a distinctive imprint of singularity resolution. We establish these results analytically through a systematic formulation of the monodromy method and confirm them numerically. We interpret this phenomenon as a manifestation of black-hole spectral instability, whereby arbitrarily small deviations from vacuum General Relativity produce nonperturbative changes in the large-overtone spectrum.

</details>


### [71] [Genesis in Minimally Modified Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37063)
*Alexander Ganz,Paul Martens,Shinji Mukohyama,Pavel Petrov*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes a 'Genesis' cosmological scenario within type-II minimally modified gravity, featuring a past geodesically complete, Minkowski-approaching background that avoids initial singularity; it yields a nearly scale-invariant scalar spectrum and blue tensor spectrum with highly suppressed tensor-to-scalar ratio, while ensuring ghost-free scalar perturbations and Planck-suppressed interactions in the asymptotic past.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To realize a nonsingular early-universe cosmology (a 'Genesis' scenario) that avoids the initial singularity and violates the null-energy condition only transiently—without introducing ghosts or gradient instabilities—within a theoretically controlled, minimally modified gravity framework.

Method: Constructs an explicit background solution in type-II minimally modified gravity; analyzes linear scalar and tensor perturbations (ghost-freedom, gradient stability, sound speed, power spectra); performs numerical evolution of superhorizon modes; examines cubic interaction vertices (matter–graviton and graviton self-interactions) in the asymptotic past using regularized matter fluctuation δχ.

Result: A past geodesically complete, Minkowski-approaching background; ghost-free scalar perturbations with transient infrared negative c_R² but stable UV behavior; numerically confirmed nearly scale-invariant (slightly red-tilted) scalar power spectrum; blue tensor spectrum with r ≪ 1; vanishing background-dependent cubic couplings in the far past, leaving only Planck-suppressed graviton interactions.

Conclusion: The type-II minimally modified gravity framework supports a viable, nonsingular Genesis scenario compatible with observational constraints on primordial fluctuations, offering a consistent alternative to inflation with distinct spectral signatures and robust UV behavior of perturbations.

Abstract: We construct a Genesis scenario within the framework of a type-II minimally modified gravity. The background solution is past geodesically complete and approaches Minkowski spacetime in the asymptotic past. While the Hubble expansion rate grows, the matter sector satisfies the null-energy condition. Scalar perturbations are ghost-free and have no ultraviolet gradient instability, although the squared sound speed $c_\mathcal{R}^2$ becomes negative within a finite infrared region in the Fourier space for a finite time interval. Numerical evolution, including superhorizon growth, yields a nearly scale-invariant, slightly red-tilted scalar spectrum over a broad region of parameter space. The tensor spectrum is blue, with a strongly suppressed tensor-to-scalar ratio. Finally, using the matter fluctuation $\delta\chi$, whose asymptotic normalization is regular, we find that the background-dependent cubic couplings vanish in the far past at fixed nonzero momenta. The surviving matter--graviton and graviton self-interactions are Planck suppressed.

</details>


### [72] [Thermodynamics and phase structure of rotating Hayward-AdS black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37194)
*Qi-Quan Li,Yu Zhang,Hoernisa Iminniyaz*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs a rotating Hayward-AdS black hole via the modified Newman–Janis algorithm, derives its full thermodynamics in the $(S,J,q,\alpha,P)$ phase space, and shows that imposing the regularity condition $M_1 = q^3/\alpha$ yields physically consistent thermodynamics—including correct Hawking temperature and response functions. It reveals a novel 8-shaped or C-shaped Gibbs free energy vs. temperature ($G_2$--$T_2$) phase diagram—distinct from standard swallowtail behavior—driven by the regularity condition, not spherical symmetry; the transition between shapes occurs at characteristic pressure $P_z$, with $P < P_z$ giving first-order small/large black hole transitions and $P_z < P < P_c$ yielding zeroth-order transitions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether the distinctive 8-shaped/C-shaped thermodynamic phase structure observed in spherically symmetric regular AdS black holes (e.g., Hayward-AdS) persists under rotation—and thus to assess whether that structure arises from spacetime regularity per se or from spherical symmetry.

Method: Applies the modified Newman–Janis algorithm to generate the rotating 'mother' black hole solution; computes fundamental thermodynamic quantities (mass, temperature, entropy, angular momentum, charge, pressure, volume) in the unconstrained 6D phase space $(S,J,q,\alpha,P)$; imposes the regularity condition $M_1 = q^3/\alpha$ to obtain the physical rotating Hayward-AdS regular black hole; analyzes phase structure via $P$--$V$ isotherms and $G_2$--$T_2$ diagrams; identifies critical pressures $P_c$ and $P_z$ and classifies stability and transition orders.

Result: The rotating Hayward-AdS black hole exhibits: (i) a local maximum and minimum in Hawking temperature $T_2$ for $P<P_c$, leading to three black hole branches (small/intermediate/large); (ii) oscillatory $P$--$V$ isotherms signaling liquid/gas-like phase coexistence; (iii) local stability of small and large branches (positive heat capacity), instability of intermediate branch (negative heat capacity); (iv) an 8-shaped $G_2$--$T_2$ diagram for $P<P_z$ (first-order transition) and a C-shaped diagram for $P_z<P<P_c$ (zeroth-order transition); (v) reduction of the unstable intermediate region with increasing $a$, $q$, or $P$.

Conclusion: The 8-shaped/C-shaped thermodynamic phase structure is preserved under rotation, proving it originates from the regularity condition restructuring the thermodynamic phase space—not from spherical symmetry. This establishes regularity as the fundamental origin of nonstandard black hole thermodynamic topology in AdS space.

Abstract: We use the modified Newman--Janis algorithm to obtain the rotating ``mother'' black hole solution of the rotating Hayward-AdS black hole. We further derive the fundamental thermodynamic quantities of the ``mother'' black hole in the full unconstrained phase space $(S,J,q,\alpha,P)$, and impose the regularity condition $M_1=q^3/\alpha$ to obtain the thermodynamic quantities of the rotating Hayward-AdS regular black hole. This order of operations gives a Hawking temperature identical to the surface gravity temperature and produces the correct response functions of the regular black hole. For $P<P_c$, the Hawking temperature $T_2$ possesses a local maximum and a local minimum, which divides the black hole into small, intermediate, and large black hole branches, and the $P$--$V$ isotherms display oscillatory behavior characteristic of a liquid/gas phase transition. The small and large black hole branches are locally stable, whereas the intermediate black hole branch has negative heat capacity and is locally unstable. Increasing the rotation parameter $a$, magnetic charge $q$, or pressure $P$ reduces the region of the intermediate branch. The $G_2$--$T_2$ diagram shows that the phase structure, in contrast to the standard swallowtail of the Reissner--Nordström-AdS and Kerr--Newman-AdS black holes, is 8-shaped or C-shaped. These two shapes are divided by the characteristic pressure $P_z$. For $P<P_z$, the curve is 8-shaped, corresponding to a first-order small/large black hole transition. For $P_z<P<P_c$, the curve is C-shaped, corresponding to a zeroth-order transition. These results show that the 8-shaped/C-shaped phase structure characteristic of regular AdS black holes persists under rotation, indicating that it stems from the restructuring of the thermodynamic phase space by the regularity condition rather than from spherical symmetry.

</details>


### [73] [Noether Symmetries in Scalar--Vector--Two-Form Cosmology with a Topological BF Interaction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37278)
*Amin Rezaei Akbarieh,Yusuf Kucukakca*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates a scalar field coupled to Abelian one-forms and two-forms via scalar-dependent kinetic functions and a topological BF term; it identifies domains of healthy cosmological dynamics (positive kinetic terms, luminal propagation), finds accelerating solutions (e.g., a ∝ t² and a ∝ t⁴/³), classifies solution branches via Noether symmetry analysis, and clarifies the distinct roles of scalar potential vs. BF coupling in driving expansion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a healthy, consistent cosmological model with multiple form fields coupled to a scalar, avoiding ghost and gradient instabilities, while distinguishing genuine acceleration mechanisms—especially whether topological BF interactions contribute negative pressure or merely redistribute energy between form sectors.

Method: Homogeneous cosmological reduction with explicit lapse preservation to recover Friedmann constraint; stability analysis of kinetic matrix and sound speeds; construction of exponential and hyperbolic-squared scalar potentials via Noether symmetry in flat minisuperspace; classification of dynamical branches by causal character of potential coordinate; comparison with uncoupled and reduced-field limits to isolate form-sector contributions.

Result: A domain is identified where all 12 physical modes are healthy (positive kinetic energy, luminal propagation); accelerating mixed solutions found (a ∝ t² with nonzero asymptotic form fractions; exact a ∝ t⁴/³ in Branch III); Noether analysis yields non-power-law potentials (hyperbolic-squared/exponential); BF term mediates finite-time energy exchange between one- and two-form sectors but provides no direct negative pressure.

Conclusion: Scalar-driven acceleration is necessary for sustained cosmic acceleration; the BF interaction alone cannot act as a dark energy source but modifies form-sector dynamics; viable models require specific scalar potential forms and careful kinetic coupling design to ensure full perturbative health and observational consistency.

Abstract: We study a canonical scalar field coupled to isotropic triplets of Abelian one-forms and two-forms through scalar-dependent kinetic functions and a constant topological $BF$ interaction. By retaining the lapse in the homogeneous reduction, we recover the Friedmann constraint and identify a sufficient domain in which all twelve physical modes have positive kinetic terms and luminal high-frequency propagation. A separate exponential model admits an accelerating mixed solution with $a\propto t^2$, nonzero asymptotic form fractions, and bounded normalized linear perturbations. For the Noether analysis, we choose a quadratic effective potential in flat minisuperspace coordinates and solve the zero-boundary determining equations. The canonical scalar potential is hyperbolic-squared or exponential, rather than a power law in the scalar field. The causal character of the potential coordinate separates three branches: Branch I admits a de Sitter limit, Branch II cannot sustain acceleration on regular future-complete expanding trajectories, and Branch III gives an exact solution with both form sectors active and late expansion $a\propto t^{4/3}$. Comparisons with consistent uncoupled and reduced-field limits separate the form contributions from the scalar-driven acceleration. The $BF$ term changes the finite-time exchange between the form sectors but supplies no direct negative-pressure source.

</details>


### [74] [Geodesic dynamics and tidal forces in a quantum-regularized black hole from string T-duality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37331)
*G. Alencar,Micael S. Oliveira,Diego Sáez-Chillón Gómez,Marcos V. de S. Silva*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies geodesic motion and tidal forces in T-duality-inspired regular (neutral and charged) black hole spacetimes, showing how the T-duality scale ℓ₀ and charge Q govern horizon structure, orbital stability, finite central tidal forces, and geodesic deviation—recovering classical limits appropriately.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how quantum-inspired T-duality regularization and electric charge affect strong-field gravitational physics—specifically geodesic dynamics and tidal forces—in regular black hole and horizonless geometries.

Method: Analytical and qualitative analysis of geometric structure, effective potentials for timelike/null geodesics, tidal tensor components along radial geodesics, and numerical/analytical evolution of geodesic deviation vectors; comparison across parameter space (ℓ₀, Q) and against classical limits.

Result: 1) ℓ₀ and Q jointly control black hole vs. horizonless transitions; 2) circular orbit existence/stability depends on both parameters; 3) T-duality ensures finite central tidal forces with novel compression/stretching regimes (including simultaneous radial–angular compression); 4) ℓ₀ and Q suppress accumulated radial stretching in geodesic deviation, while angular deformation is weakly charge-dependent.

Conclusion: T-duality regularization fundamentally alters strong-field behavior—rendering tidal singularities finite and introducing qualitatively new dynamical features—while electric charge fine-tunes their strength and location; both effects vanish smoothly in the classical (ℓ₀→0, Q fixed) limit.

Abstract: In this work, we investigate the geodesic dynamics and tidal force effects in neutral and electrically charged regular spacetimes inspired by T-duality. We first analyze the geometric properties of these solutions, including their regular central structure and horizon configurations, and show how the interplay between the T-duality scale $\ell_0$ and the electric charge $Q$ determines the transition between black hole and horizonless geometries. We then study the motion of massive and massless particles through the corresponding effective potentials, examining the existence and stability of circular orbits and the behavior of their characteristic radii. We further investigate the tidal forces experienced by observers in radial free fall, focusing on the behavior of the radial and angular components, their extrema and sign changes, and the location of these characteristic points relative to the horizon structure of the geometry. We show that the T-duality regularization renders the tidal forces finite in the central region and gives rise to different stretching and compression regimes, including a region of simultaneous compression in both the radial and angular directions, while the electric charge modifies the strength and location of these tidal features. Finally, we analyze the evolution of the geodesic deviation vector for two sets of initial conditions. We find that both the T-duality scale and the electric charge tend to suppress the accumulated radial stretching, whereas the angular deformation is considerably less sensitive to the charge. These results show how the quantum regularization induced by T-duality and the presence of electric charge modify the orbital dynamics and tidal response of these geometries in the strong-field regime. Furthermore, we demonstrate that the corresponding classical limits are recovered appropriately.

</details>


### [75] [Quasinormal Ringing of a Schwarzschild Black Hole Surrounded by a King Dark Matter Halo: Higher-Order WKB Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37501)
*Semra Gurtas Dogan,Hassan Hassanabadi,Abdullah Guvendi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates how a dark matter halo—modeled via King profile—affects scalar and electromagnetic quasinormal modes of a Schwarzschild black hole, finding reduced oscillation frequencies and damping rates; halo mass inside the photon sphere dominates real-frequency shifts, while redshift and boundary discontinuities modulate damping; no instabilities are found.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how realistic dark matter halos—beyond idealized point-mass or vacuum models—influence black hole spectroscopy and quasinormal mode (QNM) signatures, especially in light of upcoming gravitational-wave and electromagnetic observations.

Method: Analyzes perturbations of a Schwarzschild BH embedded in a King-profile dark matter halo using an effective metric with lapse function $1 - 2[M + M_{\rm DM}(r)]/r$; computes QNMs via sixth-order WKB and Padé-improved WKB; avoids numerical instability by reformulating the lapse using $\operatorname{arsinh}$; validates against time-domain evolution; compares configurations with halo partially inside horizon vs. Einstein-cluster exterior placement; quantifies impact of density discontinuity at inner halo edge.

Result: Halo reduces both real (oscillation) and imaginary (damping) parts of fundamental QNM frequencies; real-part shift is dominantly set by halo mass within photon sphere ($r = 3M$); damping reduction scales with Lyapunov exponent over null-orbit angular frequency; for exterior halo (Einstein cluster), real part follows Schwarzschild value redshifted by halo’s gravitational potential; a sharp inner density jump increases damping rate variation up to ~9%; continuous profiles yield <1.5% deviation from redshift prediction for $r_{\rm in} = 3M$, and <0.5% for $r_{\rm in} \geq 4M$; no unstable modes detected.

Conclusion: Dark matter halos measurably alter black hole quasinormal spectra in a geometrically interpretable way—primarily through enclosed mass and redshift—making QNMs a potential probe of halo structure near the horizon; however, deviations remain small (<1–2%), demanding high-precision measurements for observational discrimination.

Abstract: We study scalar and electromagnetic perturbations of a Schwarzschild black hole (BH) surrounded by a dark matter halo described by the King density profile. The lapse function can be expressed as $1-2[M+M_{\rm DM}(r)]/r$, where $M_{\rm DM}(r)$ is the halo mass enclosed within radius $r$; thus, the halo affects the geometry through its enclosed-mass function. We compute the quasinormal frequencies using the sixth-order WKB method and its Padé-improved version. Rewriting the lapse function in terms of the inverse hyperbolic sine avoids the severe numerical cancellations that arise when evaluating its high-order derivatives. For the configurations examined, the fundamental frequencies agree with those extracted from time-domain evolution to within $0.2\%$.The halo lowers both the oscillation frequency and the damping rate. The change of the real part is almost entirely accounted for by the halo mass enclosed by the photon sphere, and the additional decrease of the damping rate follows the ratio of the Lyapunov exponent to the angular frequency of the circular null orbit. Since part of the halo lies inside the horizon in this metric, we also place the halo outside the BH by means of an Einstein cluster. The frequencies then decrease by a similar amount, but the real part follows the Schwarzschild value multiplied by the gravitational redshift factor of the halo. A jump in the density at the inner edge of the halo scatters the waves and changes the damping rate by up to about $9\%$. For a continuous density profile, deviations from the redshift relation remain below about $1.5\%$ for $r_{\rm in}=3M$ and below about $0.5\%$ for the cases with $r_{\rm in}\geq4M$. No unstable modes are found within the perturbation sectors and parameter configurations examined.

</details>


### [76] [One Method to Find Them All: Analytic Black Holes beyond Einstein](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37515)
*Dongjun Li,Jierui Hu,Nicolás Yunes*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a unified framework to construct stationary, axisymmetric deformations of the Kerr black hole geometry induced by higher-curvature gravity corrections, yielding closed-form analytic solutions for six such interactions without slow-rotation approximations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Rotating black holes are key probes of strong-field gravity, but deriving their exact geometries beyond vacuum general relativity—especially in higher-curvature theories—is technically difficult due to lack of symmetry and complexity of field equations.

Method: The authors develop a geometric framework based on tidal curvature and physical source decomposition for stationary, axisymmetric spacetimes; they solve the linearized field equations analytically at leading order in coupling constants, leveraging algebraic classification and chirality properties of curvature interactions.

Result: Closed-form, fully analytic black-hole metrics for six higher-curvature terms (e.g., quadratic and cubic curvature invariants) valid for generic subextremal spin; solutions satisfy field equations exactly to leading order, match prior mode-based results, and reproduce known horizon thermodynamics.

Conclusion: Chirality governs the analytic structure of deformations, suggesting a broader organizing principle for metric-based higher-curvature theories; the framework enables systematic construction of black-hole geometries for gravitational-wave tests, imaging, X-ray spectroscopy, and near-extremal physics.

Abstract: Rotating black holes offer powerful probes of gravity, but constructing their geometries beyond vacuum general relativity remains challenging. Here we develop a unified framework for constructing stationary, axisymmetric deformations of Kerr from their tidal curvature and physical sources. As a demonstration, we obtain closed-form solutions for six higher-curvature interactions at leading order in the couplings and for generic subextremal spin, without slow-rotation expansions, numerical fitting, or mode decomposition. The solutions pass stringent checks against the field equations, previous mode-by-mode constructions, and known analytic predictions for horizon quantities. We find that the chirality of the interactions organizes the analytic structure of these solutions, a pattern that likely extends to a large class of purely metric higher-curvature theories. Our framework opens a systematic route to black-hole geometries in beyond-Einstein theories and perturbative astrophysical environments. These geometries provide a foundation for strong-field tests with gravitational waves and other observational channels, such as black-hole images and X-ray spectroscopy, as well as theoretical studies of near-extremal black holes.

</details>


### [77] [Gravitational wave signatures of periodic orbits around a Schwarzschild-like black hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37525)
*Mirzabek Alloqulov,Sanjar Shaymatov,Bobomurat Ahmedov,Chengxun Yuan*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies how deviations from Schwarzschild geometry—parameterized by ξ and γ—affect orbital motion and gravitational wave emission of test particles around an improved Schwarzschild black hole; periodic orbits are identified via radial–azimuthal frequency commensurability, and waveforms are computed using the mass-quadrupole formalism, revealing modest but detectable imprints of the modified spacetime.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe dynamical signatures of a new improved Schwarzschild black hole model by analyzing how its geometric deviations (controlled by parameters ξ and γ) influence bound orbital motion and associated gravitational radiation.

Method: Identifies periodic orbits via commensurability of radial and azimuthal orbital frequencies; numerically integrates particle trajectories; computes gravitational wave polarizations h₊ and hₓ using the symmetric trace-free (STF) mass-quadrupole formalism.

Result: Parameters ξ and γ induce modest but systematic changes in orbital configurations (e.g., precession rates, resonance locations) and in gravitational waveform phase/amplitude structure relative to standard Schwarzschild predictions.

Conclusion: Gravitational waveforms from periodic orbits encode measurable imprints of the modified spacetime geometry, making them a viable probe for constraining ξ and γ—and thus testing the viability of this BH model—via future high-precision GW observations.

Abstract: We investigate how deviations from the Schwarzschild geometry affect the motion of massive test particles and the gravitational radiation associated with their periodic trajectories around a new improved Schwarzschild black hole. Periodic configurations are determined by examining the commensurability of the radial and azimuthal orbital frequencies. For representative trajectories, we numerically reconstruct the particle motion and calculate corresponding gravitational wave polarizations using the symmetric trace-free mass-quadrupole approach. By comparing these signals with their Schwarzschild counterparts, we assess how the modified spacetime geometry is encoded in the radiation. Our analysis shows that the parameters $\xi$ and $\gamma$ produce modest changes in the orbital configurations and their associated waveforms, providing a means of investigating the dynamical signatures of this BH model.

</details>


### [78] [Weighing dark matter particles with gravitational waves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37638)
*Rodrigo Vicente,Francisco Duque,Theophanes K. Karydas,Gianfranco Bertone*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes that ultralight dark matter particle mass can be measured via wave effects in extreme-mass-ratio inspirals (EMRIs) observed by LISA, leveraging dark matter density spikes and incorporating wave-dependent accretion and dynamical friction into relativistic waveform models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The mass of dark matter particles is typically inaccessible from purely gravitational, collisionless dynamics; however, wave-like behavior of ultralight dark matter (ULDM) in dense environments—such as spikes around massive black holes—offers a novel probe when coupled to EMRI gravitational-wave signals.

Method: The authors model ULDM as a wave field forming a collisionless density spike around a massive black hole, while retaining wave interference and coherence effects on the stellar-mass companion. They integrate wave-dependent accretion and dynamical friction into relativistic EMRI waveform models and perform Bayesian parameter inference on simulated LISA data.

Result: For $M \sim 10^5\,M_\odot$ massive black holes, ULDM particle masses in the range $10^{-14}–10^{-11}\,\mathrm{eV}$ can be constrained to within one order of magnitude using LISA-observable EMRIs.

Conclusion: Gravitational-wave observations of EMRIs with LISA provide a unique, direct astrophysical probe of ultralight dark matter particle mass—bypassing traditional limitations of collisionless approximations—by exploiting quantum wave effects in strong-field gravity.

Abstract: It is commonly assumed that the mass of dark matter particles cannot be determined from their gravitational effects when dark matter behaves as collisionless matter. Here we show that extreme-mass-ratio inspirals observable by millihertz gravitational-wave observatories such as the Laser Interferometer Space Antenna (LISA) could measure the mass of ultralight dark matter particles in sufficiently dense dark matter spikes. The separation of scales between the two black holes allows ultralight dark matter to form a collisionless density spike around the massive primary while retaining wave effects in its interaction with the stellar-mass companion. We incorporate wave-dependent accretion and dynamical friction into relativistic inspiral waveforms and jointly infer the dark matter particle mass, spike normalisation and binary parameters from simulated LISA signals. For massive black holes at the low-mass end of the LISA population ($M\sim 10^5 M_\odot$), our forecasts show that particle masses spanning $10^{-14}-10^{-11}\,\mathrm{eV}$ could be measured to within an order of magnitude.

</details>


### [79] [Foldy--Wouthuysen Transformation of the Generalized Dirac Equation in Symmetric Teleparallel Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.27889)
*Sabutay Ugur,Muzaffer Adak,Ali Bagci,Caglar Pala*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper derives a low-energy effective Hamiltonian for fermions in symmetric teleparallel gravity by taking the non-relativistic limit of a generalized Dirac equation with extended spinor connection; it reveals novel spin–gravity couplings beyond standard GR, validated via Foldy–Wouthuysen expansion and Earth-field scaling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how generalized spinor couplings to non-metricity in symmetric teleparallel gravity modify low-energy fermion dynamics beyond standard general relativistic predictions, potentially yielding new experimental signatures.

Method: Non-relativistic limit via Foldy–Wouthuysen transformation up to O(1/m²) of the generalized Dirac equation in weak-field, spherically symmetric symmetric teleparallel gravity (coincident gauge, isotropic Schwarzschild metric); retains terms linear in gravitational potential and its spatial derivatives.

Result: A block-diagonal Hamiltonian containing standard gravitational analogs (kinetic, spin–orbit, Darwin) plus new operators: direct spin–gravity, anisotropic spin–momentum–gravity, and tidal spin–momentum couplings—originating from the generalized spinor connection’s non-metricity dependence.

Conclusion: The generalized Dirac equation in symmetric teleparallel gravity predicts novel low-energy spin–momentum–curvature (non-metricity) interactions, offering a theoretical basis for constraining non-metricity parameters via precision atomic or neutron interferometry experiments.

Abstract: We investigate the non-relativistic limit of the generalized Dirac equation in a weak, static, and spherically symmetric background of symmetric teleparallel gravity. The underlying generalized spinor connection incorporates the complete Clifford-algebra basis and introduces additional couplings to the non-metricity sector beyond those of the conventional Dirac theory. Working in the coincident gauge and adopting the weak-field Schwarzschild geometry in isotropic coordinates, we derive the corresponding generalized Dirac Hamiltonian and perform successive Foldy--Wouthuysen transformations up to order $1/m^2$, retaining terms to first order in the gravitational potential and its spatial derivatives. The resulting block-diagonal Hamiltonian contains not only the expected gravitational counterparts of the kinetic, spin--orbit, and Darwin interactions, but also additional operator structures generated by the generalized spinor connection. In particular, direct spin--gravity, anisotropic spin--momentum--gravity, and tidal spin--momentum couplings arise naturally from the generalized metric-affine interaction. We further perform an order-of-magnitude analysis for an electron in the Earth's weak gravitational field to justify the adopted truncation of the inverse-mass expansion. These results demonstrate that the generalized Dirac equation in a symmetric teleparallel background gives rise to new low-energy interaction channels involving the fermion spin, momentum, and spatial derivatives of the gravitational field. The resulting effective Hamiltonian provides a framework for exploring phenomenological constraints on the additional couplings entering the generalized spinor connection.

</details>

### [80] [Spin Hamiltonian for Quantum Simulation of Minimally Truncated $3+1$D SU(2) Lattice Gauge Theory and Testing Eigenstate Thermalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36608)
*Ziyuan Li,Xiaojun Yao*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper constructs a qubit-based spatial-gauge-singlet Hamiltonian for minimal-truncation (j_max=1/2) pure SU(2) lattice gauge theory in 3+1D, enforces Gauss’s law, identifies minimal independent loop variables mapped to qubits, and verifies ETH via exact diagonalization on a 2×3×3 lattice — confirming thermalization at g=0.3.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the eigenstate thermalization hypothesis (ETH) in a realistic, quantum-simulatable formulation of 3+1D pure SU(2) lattice gauge theory, enabling future real-time quantum simulation of non-equilibrium dynamics.

Method: Constructed the spatial-gauge-singlet Hamiltonian under j_max=1/2 truncation; enforced Gauss's law exactly; reduced redundancy among gauge-invariant loop operators to identify a minimal bijective set mapped to qubits; expressed Hamiltonian as Pauli strings; performed exact diagonalization on a 2×3×3 lattice with fixed boundaries (requiring 20 qubits); analyzed level statistics, local energy density matrix elements, and entanglement entropies across eigenstates.

Result: Identified ergodic coupling range; at g=0.3, level statistics, off-diagonal matrix elements of local energy density, and eigenstate entanglement entropies all satisfy ETH predictions; confirmed thermalization in truncated 3+1D SU(2) LGT.

Conclusion: The minimal-truncation 3+1D SU(2) lattice gauge theory thermalizes according to ETH, establishing it as a valid quantum simulator benchmark and validating its use for studying non-equilibrium gauge dynamics.

Abstract: We construct the spatial-gauge-singlet Hamiltonian of pure SU(2) lattice gauge theory in $3+1$ dimensions ($3+1$D) on qubits under the minimal truncation $j_{\rm max}=1/2$, building on previous work on the hyperhoneycomb lattice, and test the eigenstate thermalization hypothesis (ETH). By enforcing Gauss's law and exploiting the redundancy among gauge-invariant loop operators, we find the minimal set of independent loop variables that can be bijectively mapped onto qubits, and write the Hamiltonian as a sum of Pauli strings. An $n\times n\times n$ lattice with fixed boundary conditions requires $2(n-1)^3+3(n-1)^2$ qubits. We exactly diagonalize the Hamiltonian on a $2\times 3\times 3$ lattice and identify the coupling range of quantum ergodicity from the level statistics. At the ergodic coupling $g=0.3$, we calculate the matrix elements of local energy density operators and the entanglement entropies of all eigenstates and find them consistent with the ETH. These results show that the $3+1$D truncated SU(2) lattice gauge theory thermalizes in accordance with the ETH and provide a benchmark for future real-time quantum simulations of non-equilibrium dynamics.

</details>


### [81] [Easier parameter scans with hmcdj](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37120)
*Alexis Verney-Provatas,Ed Bennett,Gaurav Ray*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: hmcdj is a C++-based, Grid-based tool for efficiently generating large-scale lattice gauge theory ensembles, featuring YAML-driven configuration, automatic MD step-size tuning, ILDG-compliant output, and HPC scheduler integration.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To simplify and scale the generation of large numbers of gauge ensembles for lattice field theory simulations, reducing boilerplate and improving job management on HPC systems.

Method: Built atop the Grid library; enables concise C++ specification of lattice gauge theories; uses YAML for runtime parameter control; implements adaptive molecular dynamics step-size tuning, ILDG-compliant I/O, and dynamic trajectory adjustment via HPC scheduler feedback.

Result: A production-ready, open-source (GPLv2) ensemble generation tool with robust automation for acceptance-rate control, standardized output, and scheduler-aware resource utilization.

Conclusion: hmcdj significantly lowers the barrier to high-throughput lattice ensemble generation while ensuring reproducibility, interoperability, and scalability on modern HPC infrastructure.

Abstract: We present hmcdj, a new tool developed to ease the process of generating large numbers of gauge ensembles for a given theory. Building on top of the Grid library, hmcdj allows quick specification of lattice gauge theories in C++ with minimal boilerplate, and precise specification of per-ensemble parameters to be controlled at run time from a YAML input file. Additionally, it supports many quality-of-life features for managing large numbers of running jobs, including automatic tuning of the molecular dynamics step size to control the Metropolis acceptance rate; output of ILDG-compliant data; and integration with HPC schedulers to dynamically adapt the target trajectory count of a job to match its time limits. hmcdj is openly available under the GPLv2 license, and welcomes new contributions.

</details>
