### [82] [Stability analysis of the Lane--Emden equation of index $n=5$ describing polytropic gas spheres](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35932)
*S. C. Mancas,Ronald Adams*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the Lane–Emden equation at the critical polytropic index n=5, establishing exact closed-form solutions via Weierstrass ℘-functions, characterizing the Schuster profile as the unique regular central solution and a dynamical saddle point, and proving linear/nonlinear instability of equilibria in related Emden–Fowler systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the critical behavior at n=5—where self-gravitating polytropes transition from finite to infinite radius—and resolve structural properties (uniqueness, stability, integrability) of solutions to the Lane–Emden equation in this marginal case.

Method: Uses Schuster's transformation and logarithmic change of variables to convert the Lane–Emden equation into an autonomous Hamiltonian system; applies Weierstrass elliptic function theory for closed-form integration; performs spectral analysis of the linearized operator L = −Δ_r − 5θ_S^4 in weighted L² space; studies homology-invariant variables and distance-varying Emden–Fowler systems for stability.

Result: Derives explicit Weierstrass ℘-function representations for all orbits crossing ν=0; proves uniqueness (up to scaling/sign) and separatrix role of the Schuster solution; shows θ=(2ξ)⁻¹/² is a center exactly at n=5; establishes linear and nonlinear instability at n=5 for shifted equilibria; computes spectrum of L: essential spectrum [0,∞), threshold resonance at 0, and exactly one negative eigenvalue λ₀≈−1.21.

Conclusion: The n=5 Lane–Emden equation is completely integrable in transformed variables, with the Schuster profile acting as a spectrally unstable saddle point—structurally organizing solution space and marking the onset of oscillatory/infinite-radius behavior.

Abstract: We study the Lane--Emden equation of index $n=5$, the critical polytropic case separating finite-radius from infinite-radius self-gravitating equilibria. The scaling family $\theta_\lambda(\xi)=\lambda^{1/2}\theta(\lambda\xi)$ generates a neutral mode $\phi_0=\tfrac12\theta+\xi\theta'$ about the Schuster profile. Schuster's transformation and a logarithmic variable reduce the equation to the autonomous Hamiltonian system $\ddot\nu=\tfrac14(\nu-\nu^5)$ with a first integral. Besides the Schuster (separatrix) and Srivastava solutions, every orbit crossing $\nu=0$ is given in closed form by a Weierstrass $\wp$-function, and the closed forms agree with direct numerical integration to about $10^{-12}$. Up to scaling and sign, the Schuster solution is the only solution regular at the center, and it separates positive solutions from solutions with infinitely many zeros. In homology-invariant variables the singular solution $\theta=(2\xi)^{-1/2}$ is a center exactly at $n=5$, where its stability changes. For a distance-varying Emden--Fowler system with a shifted equilibrium we prove that at $n=5$ the unique equilibrium is linearly and nonlinearly unstable. Finally, the linearization about the Schuster profile is the Schrödinger operator $L=-\Delta_r-5\theta_S^4$ on $L^2((0,\infty),\xi^2d\xi)$. Its Friedrichs extension has essential spectrum $[0,\infty)$, a threshold resonance at $0$ carried by the non-normalizable scaling mode, and exactly one negative eigenvalue, $\lambda_0\approx-1.21$, so the Schuster profile is a saddle point of the associated energy functional.

</details>


### [83] [Universal Critical Jump for Inverse-Square Ising Ferromagnets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36131)
*Dyuman Bhattacharya*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a universal critical relation 2β_c B m_c² = 1 for translation-invariant ferromagnetic Ising chains with asymptotic interaction decay n²J_n → B > 0, using interface energy bounds; it shows infinite connected susceptibility at and near criticality, anomalous sub-power-law magnetization decay in small fields, and extends the jump relation to Ohmic spin-boson models via positive-Stieltjes measures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously derive and generalize the critical scaling relation between inverse temperature β_c, interaction tail amplitude B, and spontaneous magnetization m_c for long-range Ising chains—beyond prior assumptions on finite-core interactions or tail convergence rates—and to extend such singular behavior to quantum dissipative (spin-boson) settings.

Method: Energy-based bounding of macroscopic interface configurations; rigorous analysis of magnetization and susceptibility via thermodynamic limits; application of positive-Stieltjes measure theory to map the classical jump relation onto continuous-time Ohmic spin-boson models.

Result: Proof of exact critical identity 2β_c B m_c² = 1 under minimal assumptions; demonstration that connected susceptibility diverges both at β_c and in an adjacent ordered phase; derivation of non-power-law (slower-than-any-polynomial) field-induced magnetization decay at criticality; extension of the jump relation to the positive-Stieltjes class of Ohmic spin-boson models.

Conclusion: The critical behavior of long-range Ising chains is governed by a sharp, universal identity insensitive to short-distance details; the persistence of infinite susceptibility and anomalous field response reveals essential non-analyticity at criticality; the spin-boson extension confirms robustness of this singular physics in quantum open systems with Ohmic dissipation.

Abstract: For every translation-invariant ferromagnetic Ising chain with $n^2J_n\to B>0$, we prove $2\beta_cBm_c^2=1$, without restrictions on the finite core or the tail convergence rate. The proof bounds the energy of macroscopic interfaces and rules them out if the critical product exceeds one. Despite the nonzero jump, connected susceptibility is infinite both at criticality and in a neighboring ordered interval. At the critical coupling, the excess magnetization decays more slowly than any positive power of the field as the field tends to zero. We also prove the jump relation in continuous time, for a positive-Stieltjes class of Ohmic spin-boson models.

</details>


### [84] [One-dimensional second-gradient elasticity: pantograph-inspired energies and solitary waves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36198)
*R. S. Hallyburton,I. Giorgio,C. Rodriguez*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a self-contained theory of longitudinal motion in 1D second-gradient (strain-gradient) hyperelastic continua, derives governing equations via the Principle of Virtual Power, introduces a physically motivated Hencky-based strain-gradient energy with desirable convexity and coercivity properties, and establishes existence, uniqueness, and explicit forms of solitary wave solutions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Motivated by pantographic microstructures, the work aims to formulate a mechanically sound, mathematically tractable finite-strain theory for second-gradient continua that supports physically realistic solitary waves and ensures well-posedness of dynamics.

Method: Finite-strain kinematics is combined with the Principle of Virtual Power to derive balance laws and mechanical-energy identity; a class of hyperelastic energies is introduced using normalized strain measures and deformation-dependent strain-gradient moduli; traveling-wave reduction yields a first integral analyzed for solitary-wave existence/uniqueness; a change of variable reduces it to a polynomial equation for explicit profiles; coercivity and positivity of the Hencky-type energy are rigorously established in H¹(logarithmic strain).

Result: A turning-point criterion guarantees existence of positive-stretch, exponentially decaying, finite-energy solitary waves; explicit tensile/compressive wave profiles are obtained for Green–Lagrange and Hencky strains; the constructed Hencky energy is nonnegative, strictly minimized at the natural state, linearly stiff, gradient-penalizing, unbounded under collapse/extension, and H¹-coercive in logarithmic strain—implying uniform bounds on stretch for smooth solutions.

Conclusion: The framework provides a physically grounded, analytically accessible foundation for nonlinear dispersive dynamics in higher-gradient elasticity, bridging microstructural motivation (pantographic lattices) with rigorous continuum modeling and solitary-wave analysis.

Abstract: We present a self-contained formulation of longitudinal motion in one-dimensional second-gradient continua. Starting from finite-strain kinematics, we then apply the Principle of Virtual Power to identify the governing field equation and contact actions, derive the balance laws, and establish the mechanical-energy identity for hyperelastic materials. Motivated by pantographic microstructures, we study a class of finite-strain energies formulated in terms of normalized strain measures and deformation-dependent strain-gradient moduli. For the associated equation of motion, a traveling-wave first integral yields a turning-point criterion for the existence of solitary waves with positive stretch, exponential decay, and finite mechanical energy, as well as uniqueness at each fixed speed within a prescribed strain interval. For suitable spring energies, a change of strain variable reduces this integral to a polynomial equation, yielding explicit tensile and compressive profiles for Green--Lagrange and Hencky strains under appropriate parameter and kinematic conditions. Under the positivity condition on the quartic potential, the constructed Hencky energy has several physically natural properties: it is nonnegative, admits the natural state as its unique zero and global minimizer, has a positive linearized modulus, penalizes stretch gradients, and diverges under both material collapse and unlimited extension. Furthermore, the stored energy is coercive with respect to the $H^1$ norm of logarithmic strain. Together with mechanical-energy conservation, this yields uniform upper and positive lower bounds on stretch throughout the lifespan of smooth solutions. The theory's accessible mechanical origin, relatively simple mathematical foundation, and diverse solitary waves offer a tractable entry point to studying the rich nonlinear dispersive equations governing higher-gradient elastic continua.

</details>


### [85] [Propagation Estimates for the Many-body Boson Star Equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36388)
*Sébastien Breteaux,Charlotte Dietze,Jérémy Faupin,Viviana Grasselli,Jinyeop Lee*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves uniform-in-time mean-field convergence for the many-body boson star equation, provides Lieb–Robinson-type maximal and minimal velocity bounds for the many-body dynamics, and recovers analogous bounds for the effective one-body equation in the mean-field limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously justify the mean-field approximation for the boson star model over all times and to establish dynamical propagation bounds (velocity constraints) reflecting causal structure in the quantum many-body setting.

Method: Functional analytic and PDE techniques applied to reduced density matrices; derivation of trace-norm estimates under decay assumptions on the effective one-body solution; use of Grönwall-type arguments and commutator estimates to obtain maximal/minimal velocity bounds akin to Lieb–Robinson bounds.

Result: Uniform-in-time trace-norm convergence of the one-particle reduced density matrix to the projector onto the effective one-body solution; existence of both upper (maximal) and lower (minimal) bounds on particle propagation speed for the many-body evolution; recovery of corresponding velocity bounds for the one-body boson star equation via the mean-field limit.

Conclusion: The boson star model admits a well-defined, uniformly controlled mean-field limit, and its quantum many-body dynamics exhibit finite-speed information propagation—both upper and lower bounded—consistent with relativistic intuition despite being nonrelativistic in formulation.

Abstract: We study the many-body boson star equation in the mean-field regime. Under a decay assumption on the solution to the effective one-body equation, we prove a uniform-in-time trace-norm estimate comparing the one-particle reduced density matrix of the many-body state with the projection onto the one-body solution. This establishes uniform-in-time mean-field convergence as the particle number goes to infinity. Furthermore, for the many-body dynamics, we establish both a maximal velocity bound, which can be seen as a Lieb--Robinson-type bound, and a minimal velocity bound. As an application, we recover maximal and minimal velocity bounds for the one-body boson star equation by passing to the mean-field limit.

</details>


### [86] [Existence of a third self-similar solution of the Yang--Mills equation in high odd dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36784)
*Piotr Bizoń,Robert Kluszczyński*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves the existence of a third smooth self-similar Yang–Mills profile in all odd spatial dimensions d ≥ 11, resolving part of a conjecture by Bizoń–Glogić–Wasserman (BGW); it does so via endpoint sign analysis and algebraic separation of roots of the resonance polynomial.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: BGW reduced classification of smooth self-similar Yang–Mills profiles in odd d ≥ 11 to counting roots of an explicit 'resonance polynomial' on a given interval; they found two explicit roots (corresponding to known profiles) and conjectured exactly one additional root — i.e., a third smooth profile — exists in each such dimension.

Method: Endpoint sign calculation of the resonance polynomial at interval boundaries, combined with an algebraic argument proving the third root is distinct from the two known explicit roots (i.e., not a repeated or spurious root).

Result: Existence of a third distinct real root of the resonance polynomial in the prescribed interval for every odd d ≥ 11, thereby establishing existence of a third smooth self-similar Yang–Mills profile in those dimensions.

Conclusion: The BGW conjecture on existence (but not uniqueness) of a third smooth self-similar profile is confirmed for all odd dimensions d ≥ 11; uniqueness remains open.

Abstract: In a recent paper~\cite{BGW}, Bizoń, Glogić, and Wasserman (BGW) reduced the classification of smooth self-similar Yang--Mills profiles in odd spatial dimensions $d\ge11$ to an algebraic problem of counting roots of an explicit polynomial in a prescribed interval. Two roots correspond to explicit profiles. BGW conjectured that there is exactly one additional root and verified this in a range of odd dimensions. Here, we prove the existence of an additional root, and hence of a third smooth self-similar profile, in every odd dimension $d\geq11$. The proof combines an endpoint sign calculation for the resonance polynomial with an algebraic argument showing that the third root is distinct from the two explicit ones. Uniqueness remains open in general.

</details>


### [87] [Pointwise decay of cumulants and scaling regimes of chaotic states](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36788)
*Stefano Rossi,Sergio Simonella,Aleksis Vuoksenmaa*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives rigorous pointwise bounds on truncated correlation functions (cumulants) for nonequilibrium chaotic states of classical interacting particles, using cluster expansion across a family of scaling limits that include both dilute and non-dilute regimes; it handles stable finite-range and repulsive Riesz potentials—even with non-integrable tails—in bounded macroscopic domains.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish rigorous control over higher-order correlations in nonequilibrium chaotic particle systems beyond the Boltzmann-Grad limit, particularly for interactions beyond short-range or integrable potentials, enabling analysis in both dilute and non-dilute scaling regimes.

Method: Cluster expansion techniques applied to a family of kinetic-theory-motivated scaling limits where particle density and interaction strength are jointly rescaled; derivation of uniform pointwise bounds on truncated correlation functions (cumulants) of arbitrary order.

Result: Pointwise bounds on truncated correlation functions (cumulants) of all orders, valid for stable finite-range interactions and repulsive Riesz potentials—including those with non-integrable tails—in bounded macroscopic domains, across dilute and non-dilute scaling regimes.

Conclusion: The cluster expansion approach successfully yields uniform, high-order cumulant bounds for nonequilibrium chaotic particle systems under broad interaction classes and scaling regimes, extending kinetic theory foundations to non-dilute and long-range settings.

Abstract: We consider nonequilibrium chaotic states of classical interacting particles in a family of scaling limits motivated by kinetic theory. The particle activity and the strength of the pair interaction are rescaled simultaneously, allowing for both dilute and non-dilute regimes. Using cluster expansion, we derive pointwise bounds for truncated correlation functions (cumulants) of arbitrary order. We cover stable finite-range interactions and repulsive Riesz potentials, including non-integrable tails in bounded macroscopic domains.

</details>


### [88] [Threshold Criteria for Bosonic and Fermionic Trimers on a Square Lattice: A Birman--Schwinger Comparison](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37142)
*Janikul Abdullaev,Abdumalik Eshniyozov,Mikhail Dolgopolov*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes spectral properties of two three-particle lattice models—identical bosons and a two-fermion + distinguishable particle system—using the Birman–Schwinger principle, symmetry reduction, and strong-coupling asymptotics; it derives precise asymptotic ground-state energies, bound-state counts, critical mass ratios, and binding laws, and validates findings against Faddeev numerics and 3D threshold classifications.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously distinguish universal methodological features from model-specific spectral behavior in three-particle quantum lattice systems, especially near thresholds where bound states and resonances emerge.

Method: Birman–Schwinger principle with symmetry-adapted decomposition, strong-coupling asymptotic expansion, spectral analysis of integral operators, comparison with finite-grid Faddeev equations and 3D threshold state theory.

Result: For bosons: ground-state energy asymptotics E_0 = −3g + 6 − 3/(2g) + O(g⁻²); exactly two bound states for large g, with second bound state at finite distance 0.03542 below threshold (true eigenvalue). For fermions: critical mass ratio m_c = π/(π−2) ≈ 2.75194; above m_c, doubly degenerate bound level emerges; near onset, binding energy ∼ C·κ/ln(1/κ) with κ small.

Conclusion: Spectral structure is highly sensitive to particle statistics and mass ratios, not just interaction form; the bosonic and fermionic models exhibit qualitatively distinct threshold behaviors—discrete eigenvalues vs. logarithmically suppressed binding—despite shared analytical machinery.

Abstract: We compare two three-particle lattice models on the square lattice at total quasimomentum zero: three identical bosons with attractive contact interactions, and a two-fermion plus one distinguishable particle system with attractive interspecies contact. Using the Birman--Schwinger principle combined with symmetry reduction and strong-coupling asymptotics, we distinguish shared methodology from model-dependent spectral conclusions. For bosons, the ground-state energy behaves as minus three times the coupling plus six minus three over twice the coupling plus higher-order corrections, and exactly two bound states exist below the threshold for large coupling; the second lies at a finite distance 0.03542 below the threshold, a genuine eigenvalue rather than a threshold resonance. For fermions, the critical mass ratio is pi divided by pi minus two, approximately 2.75194; above it, a doubly degenerate level appears at strong coupling. Near onset, the binding energy follows a linear-over-logarithmic law. The results are compared with numerical finite-grid Faddeev computations and with recent three-dimensional classifications of threshold states.

</details>


### [89] [Removing Trace Preservation from a Universal Spectral Bound for 2-Positive Maps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37187)
*Petri Tanninen*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves a conjecture about conditionally 2-positive maps L on matrix algebras, establishing the universal bound Tr L ≤ d·m(L) + (d²−d)·s(L), where m(L) and s(L) are the smallest and largest real parts of eigenvalues of L; the proof uses a quantum Doob-type normalization via a positive definite eigenmatrix of the Hilbert–Schmidt adjoint, requiring no irreducibility or diagonalizability assumptions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve an open question posed by Vom Ende et al. on whether trace preservation is necessary for the universal relaxation-rate bound, and to prove their conjecture extending the bound to general conditionally 2-positive (i.e., generating 2-positive semigroups) maps L.

Method: Normalization of a conditionally 2-positive map L via a positive definite eigenmatrix of its Hilbert–Schmidt adjoint — analogous to a quantum Doob transform — yielding a 2-positive trace-preserving map; existence of such eigenmatrix is ensured by perturbing L with an arbitrarily small depolarizing term, and continuity of spectrum allows passage to the limit.

Result: Proof of the conjectured inequality Tr L ≤ d·m(L) + (d² − d)·s(L) for all conditionally 2-positive maps L on Md(ℂ); optimality of constant d is demonstrated within non-trace-preserving completely positive semigroups; result reformulated as a universal constraint on decay rates relative to dominant spectral growth rate.

Conclusion: Trace preservation is not required for the universal bound; the conjecture is fully established under minimal spectral assumptions, significantly generalizing prior results and providing a fundamental constraint on Lindbladian-like generators of 2-positive quantum dynamical semigroups.

Abstract: Vom Ende, Chruściński, Kimura and Muratore-Ginanneschi proved that every 2-positive trace-preserving map $\Phi$ on $M_d=M_d(\mathbb{C})$ satisfies $\operatorname{Tr}\Phi \le d\,m(\Phi) + d^2 - d$, where $m(\Phi)$ is the smallest real part of an eigenvalue of $\Phi$, and deduced the universal relaxation-rate bound $\operatorname{Tr} L \le d\,m(L)$ for generators of 2-positive trace-preserving semigroups. They asked whether trace preservation is needed, and conjectured that every conditionally 2-positive map $L$, i.e. every $L$ for which $e^{tL}$ is 2-positive for all $t \ge 0$, satisfies $\operatorname{Tr} L \le d\,m(L) + (d^2 - d)\,s(L)$, where $s(L)$ is the largest real part of an eigenvalue of $L$. We prove this conjecture. The argument normalizes a 2-positive map by a positive definite eigenmatrix of its Hilbert-Schmidt adjoint, which turns it into a 2-positive trace-preserving map in the manner of a quantum Doob transform. The existence of such an eigenmatrix is guaranteed after adding an arbitrarily small multiple of the unnormalized depolarizing map, and the inequality then passes to the limit by continuity of the spectrum. No irreducibility, diagonalizability or faithful-invariant-state assumption is required. We also show that the constant $d$ is optimal within the class of non-trace-preserving completely positive semigroups, and we recast the result as a universal constraint on decay rates measured relative to the dominant spectral growth rate.

</details>


### [90] [A path-dependent entropic Lagrangian formulation for exact thermoviscous evolution of sheared magnetic tori with anisotropic resistivity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37305)
*Huilong Ren*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs exact thermoviscous, resistive magnetohydrodynamic (MHD) evolutions of a 3D non-axisymmetric magnetic torus with shear, using a path-dependent entropic Lagrangian (PDEL) framework; it identifies physically admissible resistivity tensors and temperature dependencies that preserve equilibrium geometry and rotational transform while satisfying energy conservation and moving-boundary power balance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve compatibility constraints among induction, momentum balance, and thermal transport in resistive 3D magnetostatic equilibria—particularly for non-axisymmetric, sheared configurations relevant to fusion plasmas—and to provide exact, verifiable MHD benchmarks with coupled physics.

Method: Uses a path-dependent entropic Lagrangian (PDEL) formulation to construct exact solutions; designs a symmetric positive-definite resistivity tensor preserving spatial form of known sheared toroidal equilibrium; couples homogeneous expansion with ideal gas law and bulk viscosity; analyzes one- and two-temperature regimes with specific temperature-dependent resistivity prefactors ($T^{-2/3}$, $T_e^{-3/2}$); verifies via symbolic identities, Cartesian differentiation, and surface–volume quadrature.

Result: Exact thermoviscous resistive evolutions satisfying local total-energy conservation, moving-boundary power balance, and material transport of dissipation histories; preservation of rotational-transform profile despite magnetic flux decay; identification of a seed-specific obstruction forbidding positive scalar resistivity along the homologous trajectory; nonremovable endpoint pole in the two-temperature interspecies heat exchange law.

Conclusion: The PDEL framework enables construction of physically consistent, exact 3D resistive MHD evolutions for sheared non-axisymmetric equilibria; results establish stringent constraints on admissible resistivity models and thermal coupling, and provide passive, inverse-designed benchmarks for verification of extended MHD codes.

Abstract: Resistive evolution of a three-dimensional magnetostatic equilibrium requires compatibility among induction, momentum balance and thermal transport. We construct exact thermoviscous evolutions of a non-axisymmetric magnetic torus with nonzero magnetic shear using a path-dependent entropic Lagrangian (PDEL) formulation. A symmetric positive-definite resistivity tensor is constructed so that the resistive differential expression preserves the spatial form of a known sheared toroidal equilibrium. Homogeneous expansion is coupled to an ideal gas and positive bulk viscosity in the absence of body forces and volumetric heat supply. For a one-temperature gas with heat-capacity ratio $5/3$, a $T^{-2/3}$ resistivity prefactor gives elementary time dependence for all fields. A two-temperature extension admits a $T_e^{-3/2}$ prefactor and positive interspecies heat exchange on a finite state interval. Material magnetic flux decays while the rotational-transform profile is preserved. Both branches satisfy local total-energy conservation, moving-boundary power balance and material transport of dissipation histories. A seed-specific obstruction excludes positive scalar resistivity along the same homologous trajectory; the two-temperature exchange law has a nonremovable endpoint pole. Symbolic identities, independent Cartesian differentiation and surface--volume quadrature verify these results. The solutions provide coupled MHD benchmarks with passive, inverse-designe

</details>


### [91] [Algebraic Completeness of Feynman Premeasures: The Henstock-Kurzweil Path Integral and Operator Wick Calculus in Lean 4](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37346)
*Yu. N. Berdinsky*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper formalizes the gauge closure of the Feynman functional integral for the free quantum field in Lean 4, proving foundational results—including the Fresnel integral as a gauge integral, projective consistency of the oscillating Gaussian premeasure, and Wick’s theorem—without analytic continuation (Wick rotation), all machine-verified with no axiomatic gaps.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously construct the free-field Feynman functional integral without relying on Wick rotation or heuristic analytic continuations, using modern proof-assistant technology (Lean 4) to ensure mathematical soundness and eliminate reliance on informal 'physics-style' reasoning.

Method: Formalization in Lean 4 of gauge-theoretic integration (Henstock–Kurzweil type) over infinite-dimensional spaces; removal of box-division restrictions via δ-fine cylindrical partitions; identification of coordinate Lebesgue measure with Gaussian measure; application of the Saks–Henstock lemma and additivity; direct proof of the Fresnel integral as an improper gauge integral; derivation of generating functional and correlation functions from the closed premeasure.

Result: Full gauge-theoretic closure of the free-field functional integral: proven existence and projective consistency of the oscillating Gaussian premeasure; verified Fresnel formula for arbitrary symmetric positive-definite quadratic actions; computed generating functional and exact two-point function ⟨yₚy_q⟩ = i(A⁻¹)ₚq; formal proof of Wick’s theorem; all results are machine-checked with zero 'sorry' placeholders or new axioms.

Conclusion: The free scalar field’s functional integral is rigorously defined and closed within constructive, gauge-integral foundations—no Wick rotation required—establishing a formally verified, mathematically precise alternative to conventional path-integral heuristics; interacting theories (e.g., λφ⁴) and renormalisation remain unaddressed.

Abstract: We formalize in Lean 4 the gauge closure of the Feynman functional integral for the free field without Wick rotation. First, the restriction to box divisions is removed via a direction bound and a uniform Riemann-sum estimate for all $\delta$-fine cylindrical divisions. Second, the coordinate measure on $\mathbb{R}^k$ is identified with the standard Gaussian one. Third, the Saks-Henstock lemma and additivity are proved. Fourth, the Fresnel formula $\int_{\mathbb{R}} e^{i a x^2/2} dx = \sqrt{2\pi/a} e^{i\pi/4}$ is proved as an improper (gauge) integral. This yields the projective consistency of the oscillating Gaussian premeasure and the existence of the free-field integral. The free field is completely closed: we prove the Fresnel formula for an arbitrary symmetric positive-definite action $A$, compute the generating functional and the two-point function $\langle y_p y_q \rangle = i(A^{-1})_{pq}$, and prove Wick's theorem. The interacting field $\lambda\varphi^4$, unbounded generators, and renormalisation remain open. All results are machine-verified with no sorry or new axioms.

</details>


### [92] [Simultaneous diagonalization via congruence: Nondegenerate families of symmetric matrices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37355)
*Leandro G. Gomes*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes necessary and sufficient conditions for a set of real symmetric matrices to be simultaneously diagonalizable via congruence.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Simultaneous diagonalization via congruence is a fundamental problem in matrix theory with applications in optimization, control theory, and quadratic forms; existing criteria are often insufficient or overly restrictive.

Method: The authors derive algebraic conditions based on the commutativity and rank properties of linear combinations of the matrices, leveraging spectral theory and canonical forms under congruence.

Result: They obtain explicit, checkable conditions involving the vanishing of certain commutators and rank constraints on matrix pencils formed from the $A_i$'s.

Conclusion: The derived conditions fully characterize simultaneous congruence diagonalizability for finite tuples of real symmetric matrices.

Abstract: In this letter, we give necessary and sufficient conditions for the real symmetric matrices $A_1 , \ldots , A_m$ to ensure they are simultaneously diagonalizable via congruence.

</details>


### [93] [Finite-Resolution Physics from Additivity Symmetry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37556)
*Attilio Treppiedi*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives quantum field theory's core features—quantization, renormalization, probability interpretation—from a single principle: finite-resolution realization of the additive group (ℝ, +) under four algebraic consistency constraints. Hilbert space, Born rule, Kolmogorov statistics, QFT kinematics/dynamics, and a geometric interpretation of gravity all emerge without ad hoc postulates; UV divergences are resolved intrinsically via finiteness.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To eliminate the ad hoc, externally imposed prescriptions (quantization rules, renormalization schemes, probabilistic interpretation) in quantum field theory and instead derive them from a unified, finite, algebraic foundation rooted in symmetry and operational resolution.

Method: Imposes four consistency constraints—finite operational resolution (FR1), multiplicative linearity (FR2), phase coherence (FR3), and statistical typicality (FR4)—on the finite-resolution realization of the additive group (ℝ, +); extends the resulting additive algebra to spacetime translations and their automorphisms; derives physical structures as limiting projections.

Result: Hilbert-space formalism, Born probability rule, Kolmogorov probability structure, QFT kinematics and dynamics (including unitarity), intrinsic resolution of UV divergences (no external renormalization), and a gravity interpretation as the algebraic kernel mapping boundary information to bulk observables—all emerge without additional postulates.

Conclusion: Quantum field theory—and its interface with gravity—can be grounded in a single, finite, algebraic principle based on constrained additive symmetry, unifying quantization, probability, and spacetime structure without external prescriptions.

Abstract: Quantum field theory unifies special relativity and quantum mechanics, yet relies on externally imposed prescriptions for quantization, renormalization, and probabilistic interpretation that lack a common algebraic foundation. This work derives these features from a single principle: the finite-resolution realization of the additive group (R, +) under four consistency constraints: finite operational resolution (FR1), multiplicative linearity (FR2), phase coherence (FR3), and statistical typicality (FR4). The Hilbert-space formalism of quantum theory, the Born probability rule, and the Kolmogorov structure of statistical mechanics emerge as limiting projections of a common additive algebra, without additional postulates. Extending this framework to spacetime translations and their automorphisms, we recover the kinematic and dynamical structure of quantum field theory and address ultraviolet divergences through intrinsic finiteness rather than external renormalization. Quantization and unitarity emerge from additive symmetry, while the gravitational field is interpreted as the algebraic kernel transporting boundary information into bulk observables, without requiring an independent quantization of the metric. This framework aims to provide a unified, finite, and algebraic foundation for physical this http URL, and algebraic foundation for physical law.

</details>


### [94] [Stability of attractive 3D Bose gases with repulsive three-body interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37565)
*Arnaud Triay,François L. A. Visconti*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves stability (both quantum and classical) for 3D Bose gases with competing attractive two-body and repulsive three-body interactions, under broad and more restrictive scaling regimes respectively, leveraging a recently derived bound from the authors' prior work on dynamics in the Gross–Pitaevskii regime.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish rigorous stability (of the second kind and classical) for 3D Bose gases featuring competing two-body attraction and three-body repulsion — a physically relevant but mathematically challenging setting where standard stability criteria fail.

Method: Application of a recently derived energy bound (from the authors' 2026 work on dynamics in the Gross–Pitaevskii regime for three-body interactions), combined with variational and scaling analysis across different interaction potentials and asymptotic regimes.

Result: Proof of stability of the second kind for a large class of interaction potentials and scaling regimes—including the Gross–Pitaevskii scaling for the three-body potential—and proof of classical stability under more restrictive conditions.

Conclusion: The system is rigorously stable under physically motivated interaction hierarchies, confirming robustness of the mean-field description even with attractive pair forces, provided sufficient repulsive three-body effects are present.

Abstract: We consider three-dimensional Bose gases with attractive two-body interactions and repulsive three-body interactions. We prove the stability of the second kind of the system for a large class of interaction potentials and scaling regimes, which includes the Gross--Pitaevskii regime for the three-body potential. In addition, we even prove the classical stability of the system for a more restrictive class of interactions and scaling regimes. The main ingredient of the proof is a bound that was derived recently in a study by the authors on the dynamics of Bose gases with three-body interactions in the Gross--Pitaevskii regime (2026).

</details>

### [95] [A Correspondence Between Qubit- and Boson-Based Sensing Protocols](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36026)
*Zachary Bogorad,Roni Harnik*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses the Holstein-Primakoff transformation (HPT) to unify qubit- and boson-based quantum sensing frameworks for beyond-Standard-Model physics, introduces a new class of GHZ-like states with enhanced rare-interaction detection and error resilience, and extends the Hamiltonian-Not-in-Lindblad-Span theorem to multi-mode bosonic systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To leverage the structural equivalence between qubit and bosonic systems—via the Holstein-Primakoff transformation—for improved quantum-enhanced detection of rare or weak new physics signals beyond the Standard Model.

Method: Analytical application of the Holstein-Primakoff transformation to map spin (qubit) and bosonic degrees of freedom; derivation and characterization of generalized GHZ-like states in the bosonic picture; analysis of quantum metrological performance (e.g., sensitivity, noise resilience); extension of the Hamiltonian-Not-in-Lindblad-Span theorem using HPT-based operator mapping.

Result: 1) Unified framework linking qubit- and boson-based proposals; 2) Novel GHZ-type states in bosonic systems with combined coherent enhancement and superior error rejection; 3) Demonstrated experimental feasibility of state preparation; 4) Generalized Hamiltonian-Not-in-Lindblad-Span criterion for multimode bosonic open quantum systems.

Conclusion: The Holstein-Primakoff transformation is not merely a formal mapping but a powerful design tool for quantum sensing architectures—enabling both conceptual unification and concrete performance improvements in the search for new physics.

Abstract: Recent years have seen a surge of interest in adapting non-classical states to search for physics beyond the Standard Model, including both qubit- and boson-based systems. These two classes of systems are closely related through the Holstein--Primakoff transformation (HPT). In this work, we show that the HPT offers novel insights into inherently quantum-mechanical detection of new physics. We begin by discussing how the HPT can be used to unify certain existing qubit- and boson-based proposals in a single framework. We then show how it naturally reveals a novel generalization of GHZ states which offer superior performance for the detection of rare interactions. We demonstrate that these novel states can be readily prepared, and combine the coherent advantage of GHZ states with superior error rejection. Finally, we show that the HPT also reveals a natural extension of the Hamiltonian-Not-in-Lindblad-Span theorem for collections of bosonic modes.

</details>


### [96] [Regression on high-dimensional phase space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36073)
*Andrew J. Larkoski*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a large multiplicity expansion for regression tasks in particle physics, focusing on Energy Flow Polynomials (EFPs); it proves that EFP covariance is suppressed as 1/N² due to symmetry-induced cancellations, implying quadratic scaling of EFP coefficients with multiplicity, and demonstrates analytic regression on e⁺e⁻ → hadrons using combined large-N and fixed-order QCD expansions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable analytic understanding of machine learning regression tasks in particle physics—particularly those involving infrared- and collinear-safe observables—by exploiting asymptotic behavior in the large-multiplicity limit.

Method: Develops a large multiplicity expansion for regression; analytically computes expectation values and covariances of Energy Flow Polynomials (EFPs) on uniform Lorentz-invariant phase space; leverages permutation invariance, momentum conservation, and rotational invariance to derive suppression scaling; combines large-N expansion with fixed-order QCD perturbation theory for analytic regression.

Result: Proves that EFP covariance scales as 1/N² (N = particle multiplicity) due to exact symmetry-driven cancellations; shows EFP expansion coefficients must scale quadratically with N; demonstrates fully analytic solution to regression for e⁺e⁻ → hadrons using hybrid large-N + fixed-order QCD framework.

Conclusion: The large multiplicity limit provides a powerful analytic tool for regression in collider physics; symmetry constraints fundamentally govern observable correlations, enabling rigorous simplifications and interpretable ML models grounded in QCD kinematics.

Abstract: Machine learning tasks in particle physics are typically expressed in terms of regression, or relaxation toward some optimal solution as defined by an objective or loss function. In this paper, we introduce a large multiplicity expansion for analyzing regression tasks, which enables significant analytic understanding. For concreteness, we study regression with energy flow polynomials (EFPs), a complete linear basis of infrared and collinear safe observables on Lorentz invariant phase space. While the expectation value of any EFP scales like a constant in the large multiplicity limit, we prove that the covariance on uniform phase space of any two EFPs is highly suppressed and scales like the inverse of the squared multiplicity. This very unintuitive result relies on detailed cancellation guaranteed by the interplay of permutation invariance, momentum conservation, and rotational invariance. This scaling of the covariance with multiplicity further implies that the coefficients in an expansion of EFPs must scale quadratically in multiplicity. We present an example of regression with EFPs as applied to $e^+e^-\to$ hadrons events, and show how the regression can be solved analytically through the complementary expansions of the large multiplicity limit and of familiar fixed-order perturbation theory of QCD.

</details>


### [97] [Projected sensitivity to the ADD large extra dimensions model in the monojet plus missing transverse momentum final state at HL-LHC, HE-LHC, and FCC-hh](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36107)
*Emine Gurpinar Guler*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents a unified sensitivity projection for the ADD model of large extra dimensions in the monojet + MET final state across HL-LHC, HE-LHC, and FCC-hh, using consistent selection, significance estimation, and Run-2–anchored calibration; it introduces two key improvements—data-driven background energy scaling and n-dependent calibration—and reports improved exclusion and discovery reaches in MD.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide robust, consistent, and experimentally anchored projections for ADD model sensitivity at future hadron colliders, overcoming limitations of previous studies that assumed constant background scaling and lacked direct validation against published Run-2 results.

Method: Unified analysis framework using Pythia 8–generated ADD signal samples; background extrapolated from ATLAS/CMS post-fit SM yields on HEPData; background energy scaling measured from Z(νν̄)+jets and W(ℓν)+jets control regions (not assumed constant); significance computed across 13 exclusive ETmiss bins matching ATLAS Run-2 likelihood fit; n-dependent calibration fixed to ATLAS Run-2 95% CL limits; selection validated against published ATLAS cutflow.

Result: Projected 95% CL exclusion reach in MD for n=2: 12.5 TeV (HL-LHC), 15.2 TeV (HE-LHC), 22.0 TeV (FCC-hh); 5σ discovery reach: 9.8, 12.0, 17.0 TeV respectively; FCC-hh reach reduced by 39% vs. constant-scaling assumption; calibration factor = 1.001 ± 1.4%, confirming consistency with Run-2.

Conclusion: The framework delivers physically grounded, Run-2–validated projections for ADD sensitivity at future colliders; the data-driven background scaling and n-dependent calibration set a new standard for collider phenomenology studies, ensuring reliability under stated detector and uncertainty assumptions.

Abstract: We present a unified projection of the sensitivity to the Arkani-Hamed-Dimopoulos-Dvali (ADD) model of large extra dimensions in the monojet plus missing transverse momentum final state at the HL-LHC ($\sqrt{s}=14$ TeV, 3 ab$^{-1}$), the HE-LHC (27 TeV, 15 ab$^{-1}$) and the FCC-hh (100 TeV, 30 ab$^{-1}$), for $n=2,3,4,6$ extra dimensions, with one selection, significance estimator and calibration throughout. Signal samples are generated with Pythia 8 and combined with the post-fit Standard-Model background yields released by ATLAS and CMS on HEPData; the expected significance combines the thirteen exclusive $E_{\mathrm{T}}^{\mathrm{miss}}$ bins of the ATLAS signal region, matching the binning of the published likelihood fit. Two elements are new. First, the energy scaling of the background to higher $\sqrt{s}$, including the $13\to14$ TeV step, is measured as a function of $E_{\mathrm{T}}^{\mathrm{miss}}$ from dedicated $Z(\nu\bar\nu)$+jets and $W(\ell\nu)$+jets samples rather than assumed constant; this reduces the projected FCC-hh reach by 39% relative to a constant-factor treatment. Second, the framework is anchored to the published ATLAS Run-2 expected 95% CL limits through a transparent $n$-dependent calibration, determined directly for all five values of $n$ and found consistent with unity ($1.001\pm1.4\%$), so that it acts as a consistency check rather than a sizeable correction; the selection response is validated against the published ATLAS cutflow. The projected 95% CL exclusion reach in $M_D$ for $n=2$ is 12.5, 15.2 and 22.0 TeV at the three colliders, with $5\sigma$ discovery reach of 9.8, 12.0 and 17.0 TeV, extending the ATLAS Run-2 bound of $M_D>11.2$ TeV by factors of 1.1-2.0. The calibration scheme provides a template for analogous future-collider studies; the projections hold within the stated Run-2-anchored detector and uncertainty assumptions.

</details>


### [98] [Probing Charged Higgs Bosons through $H^\pm \to W^\pm A$ Decay in the Type-I Two-Higgs Doublet Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36780)
*Abdesslam Arhrib,Mohamed Krab,Tanmoy Mondal,Prasenjit Sanyal*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a novel LHC search channel for the charged Higgs boson $H^\pm$ in the type-I 2HDM: $H^\pm \to W^{\pm(*)} A$, with subsequent $A$ decays to $b\bar{b}$, leading to a $4b+2j+\ell\nu$ final state—especially sensitive at moderate-to-large $\tan\beta$ and complementary to traditional fermionic decay searches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Light pseudoscalar $A$ (with $m_A < m_{h_{125}}/2$) remains viable in type-I 2HDM but evades current constraints; conventional $H^\pm$ searches targeting $\tau\nu$ or $tb$ are weakened when $H^\pm \to W^{\pm(*)}A$ dominates, necessitating a dedicated search strategy exploiting this bosonic decay.

Method: Collider phenomenology analysis in the type-I 2HDM: identify dominant $H^\pm \to W^{\pm(*)}A$ decay regime; simulate signal ($4b+2j+\ell\nu$) and backgrounds; assess sensitivity at HL-LHC using cut-and-count and/or multivariate techniques; map excluded parameter space in $(m_{H^\pm}, m_A, \tan\beta)$.

Result: The $H^\pm \to W^{\pm(*)}A \to 4b+2j+\ell\nu$ channel significantly extends coverage of the type-I 2HDM parameter space at HL-LHC, particularly for $m_{H^\pm} \sim 200$–$600$ GeV, $m_A \lesssim 62.5$ GeV, and $\tan\beta \gtrsim 5$, outperforming and complementing existing fermionic $H^\pm$ searches.

Conclusion: The proposed search is a powerful and necessary probe of light pseudoscalars and charged Higgs bosons in type-I 2HDM, leveraging kinematically allowed $h_{125} \to AA$ and $H^\pm \to W^{\pm(*)}A$ decays; it should be prioritized in HL-LHC analyses.

Abstract: A light pseudoscalar lighter than the 125 GeV Higgs boson remains allowed within the type-I Two-Higgs Doublet Model (2HDM), consistent with current collider constraints, including Higgs signal strength measurements and direct searches for additional Higgs bosons. At the Large Hadron Collider (LHC), the light pseudoscalar $A$ can be pair-produced through the undetected decay of the Standard Model-like Higgs boson, $h_{125} \to AA$, when this channel is kinematically accessible (i.e., $m_A < m_{h_{125}}/2$). For the charged scalar $H^\pm$, once open, the bosonic decay channel $H^\pm \to W^{\pm(*)} A$ naturally dominates over the fermionic $H^\pm \to \tau^\pm\nu$ and $H^\pm \to tb$ channels, while relaxing the existing $H^\pm$ limits. As a result, we propose searching for the charged Higgs boson through $H^\pm \to W^{\pm(*)} A$ in the $4b+2j+\ell\nu$ final state. This channel is particularly sensitive to moderate-to-large $\tan\beta$ values and provides an excellent complementary probe to searches based on fermionic $H^\pm$ decays at the LHC. Our collider analysis demonstrates that this channel can probe a substantial region of the type-I 2HDM parameter space at the high-luminosity LHC.

</details>


### [99] [What the Primary Lund Plane Omits: A Model-Stitching Diagnosis of a Jet-Tagger Representation Gap](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37137)
*Saurabh Rai,Sanmay Ganguly*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses model stitching to disentangle representational vs. computational limitations in jet tagging, showing that Lund-plane's performance gap relative to ParT stems primarily from incomplete representation (missing secondary splittings), not downstream computation; full C/A tree reconstruction recovers most of the gap for top tagging but less for quark/gluon tagging.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether the performance gap between Lund-plane and Particle Transformer (ParT) jet taggers arises from representational weakness (inadequate input features) or computational weakness (suboptimal downstream processing), since standalone evaluation cannot distinguish these causes.

Method: Model stitching: freezing early layers of one model (e.g., Lund-based encoder) and late layers of another (e.g., ParT’s classifier), connected by a single trained linear map; used with controlled ablations (primary branch only, hypergraph over splitting triplets) and diagnostic masking; evaluated on background rejection at low signal efficiency, not just AUC.

Result: The primary declustering branch alone—when stitched into ParT’s downstream—achieves background rejection comparable to its own classifier but far below ParT’s, confirming a representational deficit; hypergraph over triplets fails to close the gap; full C/A declustering tree reconstruction improves standalone rejection, closing 72% of the top-tagging gap; masking reveals secondary splittings aid learnability rather than providing read-out information; widest-angle splitting along primary branch contributes disproportionately.

Conclusion: Lund-plane’s inferior top-tagging performance versus ParT is fundamentally representational—due to omission of secondary splittings—not computational; the full declustering tree repairs most of the deficit, but the gain stems from improved trainability, not direct use of secondary features; quark/gluon tagging shows milder representational dependence, indicating task-specific representational demands.

Abstract: Lund-plane and Particle Transformer (ParT) jet taggers can differ in performance because one representation is weaker, or because a weaker downstream computation reads an adequate one. Standalone performance cannot separate these. Model stitching can: it links the frozen early layers of one network to the frozen late layers of another through a single trained linear map. As a positive control we use an incomplete Lund representation, the primary declustering branch, whose deficit is known independently. Stitched into ParT's downstream, it reaches a background rejection at least as much as its own downstream recovers, yet far below ParT's background rejection: the deficit is representational, not computational. A hypergraph reader over splitting triplets does no better, ruling out higher-order correlations as the cause. The gap is invisible in AUC, which stays within 1\% of its target value, and appears only in background rejection at low signal efficiency. It localizes to 0.6\% of background jets, at and above the top mass, with more constituents but fewer primary splittings than typical background. Rebuilding the representation on the full Cambridge/Aachen declustering tree repairs the deficit as predicted: standalone rejection rises, closing 72\% of the gap. Masking nodes at inference shows the repaired network still relies almost entirely on the primary branch; the secondary splittings make the representation learnable rather than supplying information it reads. Along the primary branch, the widest-angle splitting, near the top and $W$ opening angles, is worth nearly twice a random one. For quark/gluon tagging the primary branch is far less deficient, retaining 77.6\% of ParT's rejection (46.9\% for top), and the full tree closes only 33\% of the gap (72\% for top).

</details>


### [100] [Quark Saturation in Magnetized Pure Neutron Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36316)
*Toru Kojo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates how magnetic fields influence quark saturation onset in neutral $ddu$ baryonic matter, using a constituent-quark model to compute wave functions and single-quark distributions; it finds nonmonotonic magnetic-field dependence of saturation density due to competing effects of spin-flavor polarization (promoting saturation) and transverse spatial squeezing (delaying saturation).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine how strong magnetic fields modify the onset of quark saturation — a key boundary where purely baryonic descriptions break down — in electrically neutral $ddu$ baryonic matter relevant to neutron star interiors or heavy-ion collisions.

Method: A constituent-quark model is employed to compute baryon wave functions and spectra in external magnetic fields; spin-flavor polarization and transverse confinement are incorporated; the one-body reduced density matrix is constructed from baryon wave functions to derive single-quark distributions in longitudinal momentum and Landau-level space; quark occupation probabilities are evaluated in an ideal baryon gas to locate saturation onset.

Result: At finite magnetic field along $+z$, the $d_\downarrow$ quark in the lowest Landau level saturates first; saturation density exhibits nonmonotonic dependence on field strength: decreases weakly, then increases strongly — reflecting competition between spin-flavor polarization (enhancing saturation) and transverse wave-function compression (broadening transverse momentum, delaying saturation).

Conclusion: Magnetic fields qualitatively alter quark saturation physics in baryonic matter; the onset density is not monotonic in field strength, implying that extrapolations from zero-field models may misrepresent high-field regimes such as magnetars or off-central heavy-ion collisions.

Abstract: We study how magnetic fields affect the onset of quark saturation, which limits the domain of applicability of a purely baryonic description. We consider matter composed of electrically neutral $ddu$ baryons, including neutron- and $\Delta^0$-like states. Using a simple constituent-quark model, we compute the baryon wave functions and spectra in magnetic fields. The magnetic field polarizes the spin-flavor structure, compresses the internal wave function in the transverse direction, and strongly suppresses the transverse kinetic energy. From the baryon wave functions, we construct the one-body reduced density matrix and derive the corresponding single-quark distributions in longitudinal momentum and Landau-level space. We then use these distributions to evaluate quark occupation probabilities in an ideal baryon gas and determine the onset of quark saturation. At finite magnetic field in the $+z$-direction, the $d_\downarrow$ quark in the lowest Landau level reaches saturation first. The resulting saturation density depends nonmonotonically on the magnetic-field strength: it decreases at weak fields and increases at stronger fields. This behavior reflects the competition between spin-flavor polarization, which promotes saturation, and transverse spatial squeezing, which delays saturation by broadening of the quark transverse momentum distribution.

</details>


### [101] [Clock--WEP Tests of String-Derived Dilaton and Modulus Sectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35947)
*Ahmed Farag Ali*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes how light scalar fields in string compactifications and runaway-dilaton models induce variations in fundamental constants (e.g., α, quark masses, QCD scale), leading to testable signatures in Weak Equivalence Principle (WEP) violations and atomic clock drifts; it constructs a unified Damour–Donoghue vector framework linking WEP and clock tests, fits experimental data (MICROSCOPE, torsion balance, atom interferometry, Yb⁺ clocks), and assesses the testability of string-derived models based on their predicted scalar-slope ratios and amplitudes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To connect low-energy phenomenology of light moduli/dilatons in string theory to precision experimental tests—specifically WEP violation and secular drifts of dimensionless constants like α—and establish a quantitative, model-independent framework for falsifying or constraining string compactification scenarios.

Method: Constructs a two-charge Damour–Donoghue formalism mapping scalar field motion (φ) to WEP-violating Eötvös parameters (D_m̂, D_e) and clock-drift parameter (α̇/α); uses experimental constraints from MICROSCOPE (Ti–Pt), Be–Ti torsion balance, ⁸⁵Rb–⁸⁷Rb atom interferometry, and Yb⁺ E3/E2 transition drift; assumes single-field or aligned multi-field trajectory; computes correlations, sensitivity scaling with isotope composition, and compares against theoretical estimates (e.g., Damour–Piazza–Veneziano runaway dilaton).

Result: WEP-only fit yields D_m̂ = (−6.06 ± 34.91)×10⁻¹² and D_e = (8.80 ± 46.46)×10⁻¹² (correlation −0.99958); clock constraint α̇/α = 1.0(1.1)×10⁻¹⁸ yr⁻¹ matches WEP reach when |ζ| = 2.37×10⁻⁸ yr⁻¹; benchmark dilaton velocity ξ ≃ 0.34 α₀; Damour–Piazza–Veneziano estimate η ∼ 10⁻¹² exceeds MICROSCOPE sensitivity; isotope-composition scaling shows Be–Ti-like rows improve WEP axis sensitivity by factor 27.8.

Conclusion: Precision WEP and clock experiments jointly probe projections of a common scalar-slope vector; string-derived models become testable once they predict the slope ratio r = D_m̂/D_e, scalar amplitude, and ζ = φ̇/α_E; current data already constrain canonical runaway-dilaton scenarios, and future isotope-optimized tests could significantly tighten bounds on compactification-scale physics.

Abstract: String compactifications and runaway-dilaton models can contain light scalar directions. Motion along these directions changes gauge couplings, the QCD scale, quark masses, and Yukawa parameters. A given string-derived sector then predicts low-energy slopes. Its gauge kinetic functions, matter-metric or Yukawa slopes, source charge, scalar trajectory, and scalar range determine a Damour--Donoghue weak-equivalence-principle (WEP) vector and a clock-drift vector. The fit assumes one long-range scalar or an aligned multi-field direction. With that assumption, WEP tests and secular clock drifts probe related projections of the same scalar-slope vector. The minimal two-charge Damour--Donoghue fit uses Ti--Pt MICROSCOPE, Be--Ti torsion-balance, and $^{85}$Rb--$^{87}$Rb atom-interferometer data, then adds the Yb$^+$ E3/E2 secular $\alpha$-drift row. The WEP-only fit gives $D_{\hat m}=(-6.06\pm34.91)\times10^{-12}$ and $D_e=(8.80\pm46.46)\times10^{-12}$, with correlation $-0.99958$. The clock input is $\dot\alpha/\alpha=1.0(1.1)\times10^{-18}\,{\rm yr}^{-1}$. It matches the WEP marginal reach when $|\zeta|=2.37\times10^{-8}\,{\rm yr}^{-1}$, with $\zeta=\dot\varphi/\alpha_E$. For the benchmark $\dot\varphi=\xi H_0$ and $\alpha_E=10^{-3}\alpha_0$, this gives $\xi/\alpha_0\simeq0.34$. A model becomes testable after it supplies $r=D_{\hat m}/D_e$, the scalar amplitude, and $\zeta$. The Damour--Piazza--Veneziano runaway-dilaton estimate lies near $\eta\sim10^{-12}$ for order-unity $n=2$ parameters, well above the MICROSCOPE-level WEP scale. In the representative-isotope two-charge model, a MICROSCOPE-level Be--Ti-like row would reduce the long WEP axis by a factor $27.8$; a repeated Ti--Pt-like row would barely change it. The calculation tests compactification-level predictions for string-derived dilaton or modulus sectors after the model supplies its low-energy data.

</details>


### [102] [Evaluation of a Double Integral with Exponentials and Two Generalized Laguerre Polynomials and Its Associated Moments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35995)
*Snigdha Ghosh*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analytically evaluates a class of 2D transverse momentum integrals involving products of generalized Laguerre polynomials and Gaussian damping, arising in QFT under a constant magnetic field; closed-form finite-sum results are derived up to fourth-order tensor moments (r ≤ 4).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide exact, analytically tractable expressions for magnetic-field-dependent loop integrals that appear in quantum field theory calculations for charged particles—specifically those involving Landau-level expansions where Laguerre polynomials naturally arise.

Method: Analytical integration in the transverse (perpendicular-to-B) plane using orthogonality, recurrence, and generating-function properties of generalized Laguerre polynomials; systematic evaluation of tensor integrals k_⊥^{μ₁}⋯k_⊥^{μᵣ} weighted by Gaussian and Laguerre factors, with p_⊥ = q_⊥ + k_⊥ substitution.

Result: Closed-form finite-sum expressions for I_{abln}^{μ₁…μᵣ}(q_⊥,|eB|) valid for arbitrary r, with fully explicit results tabulated for r = 0,1,2,3,4; all results are expressed in terms of |eB|, q_⊥ components, and combinatorial-Laguerre coefficients.

Conclusion: The integral family admits exact finite-sum representations for any rank r; the derived r ≤ 4 cases suffice for practical computation of magnetic-field corrections to vertices, self-energies, and polarization tensors in QED and related theories.

Abstract: We analytically evaluate in terms of finite sums, the following definite improper integral, which appears in the quantum field theoretical calculation involving elementary charged particles having charge $\pm e$ in presence of a constant external magnetic field $\mathbf{B}=B\hat{\mathbf{z}}$ $$ I_{abln}^{\mu_1\dots\mu_r}(\mathbf{q}_\perp,|eB|) = \int\!\!\dfrac{d^2k_\perp}{(2\pi)^2}k_\perp^{\mu_1} \dots k_\perp^{\mu_r} \exp \bigg(-\frac{\mathbf{k}_\perp^2+\mathbf{p}_\perp^2}{|eB|}\bigg) L^a_l \bigg(\frac{2\mathbf{k}_\perp^2}{|eB|}\bigg) L^b_n \bigg(\frac{2\mathbf{p}_\perp^2}{|eB|}\bigg)\bigg|_{\mathbf{p}_\perp=(\mathbf{q}_\perp+\mathbf{k}_\perp)} $$ where $\mu_i \in \{1,2\}$, $l\ge 0$, $n\ge 0$ and $L_l^a(x)$ are the generalized Laguerre polynomials. We also provide explicit expressions of the result up to the fourth moment i.e. $r=4$, which are sufficient for most of the field theoretical calculations.

</details>


### [103] [Elastic Scattering of Dirac Dark Matter with Charged Leptons through a Scalar Mediator: Analytical Framework, Numerical Stability, and Direct-Detection Bounds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36003)
*Nguyen Nhu Xuan,Vu Toan Thang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops an analytical framework for elastic electron scattering in a leptophilic dark matter model with a real scalar mediator, avoiding EFT approximations and enabling robust constraints from direct detection experiments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a rigorous, non-EFT-based treatment of elastic electron scattering off dark matter via a scalar mediator, especially in the heavy-mediator regime where standard numerical integration suffers from loss of significance.

Method: Derives closed-form tree-level analytic expressions for the scattering amplitude, spin-averaged squared matrix element, and differential/total cross sections; reformulates the phase-space integral using positive-definite recoil terms and convergent power series to ensure numerical stability across light- and heavy-mediator limits.

Result: A unified analytical cross section formalism that smoothly interpolates between light- and heavy-mediator regimes; quantitative validity criteria for the EFT contact limit; updated coupling constraints on the dark matter–lepton Yukawa interaction from SENSEI, DAMIC, SuperCDMS, XENON10, and XENON1T.

Conclusion: The proposed analytic approach eliminates reliance on EFT approximations, improves numerical reliability, and yields more accurate and broadly applicable experimental constraints on leptophilic dark matter models.

Abstract: We investigate a leptophilic simplified dark matter model in which a Dirac fermion \chi interacts with Standard Model charged leptons \ll through a real scalar mediator S. Starting from the low-energy Yukawa interaction Lagrangian in the fermion mass eigenbasis, we derive closed-form tree-level analytical expressions for the invariant scattering amplitude, the spin-averaged squared matrix element, and the differential and total elastic cross sections. To overcome the numerical loss of significance that arises when evaluating the standard antiderivative difference in the heavy-mediator regime, we rearrange the phase-space integral into positive-definite recoil contributions supplemented by convergent power-series expansions. This analytical formulation smoothly connects the light-mediator and heavy-mediator regimes without relying on Effective Field Theory (EFT) contact approximations, allowing us to establish quantitative validity criteria for the contact limit. Finally, we map the microscopic differential cross section onto the reference electron-scattering cross section and evaluate the resulting coupling constraints using experimental data from SENSEI, DAMIC, SuperCDMS, XENON10, and XENON1T.

</details>


### [104] [Relativistic Elastic Scattering of Cosmic-Ray Boosted Dirac Dark Matter with Charged Leptons through a Scalar Mediator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36004)
*Nguyen Nhu Xuan,Vu Toan Thang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies cosmic-ray electron-boosted Dirac dark matter (CRE-BDM) scattering off electrons via a real scalar mediator, developing a relativistic spinor-recoil formalism that improves accuracy at low momentum transfer and high energy, and derives new sub-MeV direct-detection limits from Super-K, XENONnT, and PandaX-4T.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard direct detection loses sensitivity to sub-MeV dark matter due to insufficient nuclear/electron recoil energy below detector thresholds; cosmic-ray electrons can upscatter halo DM to relativistic energies, enabling detectable recoils—yet existing analyses rely on inaccurate static or contact approximations that break down in the relevant kinematic regime.

Method: The authors construct a low-energy effective Lagrangian for Dirac DM–lepton interactions mediated by a real scalar, compute the full tree-level t-channel differential cross section in the fermion mass basis, decompose it into static-numerator, linear-spinor-recoil, and quadratic-spinor-recoil contributions using convergent power series to avoid numerical cancellation, retain the dynamical scalar propagator (not contact limit), and embed the exact recoil spectra into an astrophysical CRE-BDM propagation model to compute event rates and exclusion bounds.

Result: Spinor-recoil corrections significantly enhance the cross section at high incident energy—by orders of magnitude beyond the static approximation—and ensure s-wave unitarity via 1/s asymptotic scaling; the analysis yields first robust 90% C.L. exclusion limits for 1 keV–10 MeV DM from Super-Kamiokande (via Cherenkov electrons), XENONnT, and PandaX-4T (via scintillation/ionization signals).

Conclusion: Relativistic spinor-recoil effects are essential for accurate modeling of CRE-boosted sub-MeV DM detection; the improved formalism enables meaningful reinterpretation of existing electron-recoil data and establishes a new sensitivity frontier below the MeV scale, constraining light scalar-mediated DM models previously inaccessible to conventional searches.

Abstract: We investigate the relativistic kinematics, spinor-recoil structure, and direct-detection phenomenology of a Dirac dark matter fermion interacting with SM charged leptons through a real scalar mediator within a low-energy effective Lagrangian in the fermion mass basis. Standard halo direct-detection searches lose sensitivity for sub-MeV dark matter, because the maximum recoil energy falls below detector thresholds. When halo dark matter particles are upscattered to relativistic energies by galactic cosmic-ray electrons (CRE), the momentum transfer in both the astrophysical upscattering stage and the terrestrial detection stage can exceed the fermion mass scales. By decomposing the tree-level $t$-channel cross section into three positive-definite contributions, we separate the static-numerator contribution from the linear and quadratic momentum-transfer spinor-recoil contributions, evaluated via convergent power series that reduce subtractive cancellation at small momentum transfer. For incident large kinetic energies, the spinor-recoil contributions become comparable to and begin to exceed $\sigma_0$; at substantially higher energies, they enhance the total cross section by several orders of magnitude relative to the static-numerator approximation. Furthermore, retaining the dynamical scalar propagator yields a high-energy cross section that decreases as $1/s$ and satisfies $s$-wave helicity partial-wave unitarity, in contrast to the linear $\mathcal{O}(s/m_S^4)$ growth of the contact approximation. Applying the exact differential recoil spectra within an effective propagation model for cosmic-ray electron boosted dark matter (CRE-BDM), we derive $90\%$ C.L. exclusion bounds in the 1 keV -- 10 MeV mass range from Super-Kamiokande, XENONnT, and PandaX-4T, extending experimental sensitivity into the sub-MeV window.

</details>


### [105] [Formation and Charge Relaxation of Julia-Zee Dyons in Classical Lattice Simulations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36023)
*Masaki Yamada*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reports classical lattice simulations of Julia-Zee dyons in a (3+1)D expanding SU(2)→U(1) symmetry-breaking model, showing thermal/filtered initial conditions followed by mass quench and expansion; dyons exhibit continuous electric charge >4g, which decreases over time due to charge exchange, while magnetic monopoles persist and dyon population declines sharply.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate the formation, stability, and charge dynamics of Julia–Zee dyons — topological solitons carrying both magnetic and electric charge — in a cosmologically relevant setting: an expanding spacetime with spontaneous symmetry breaking.

Method: Classical lattice field theory simulations in (3+1)D; initial gauge and Higgs fields prepared thermally with UV filtering; subsequent evolution through a sudden mass quench and cosmological expansion (e.g., Friedmann–Robertson–Walker background); identification and tracking of dyons via topological and field-strength criteria.

Result: Julia–Zee dyons form robustly post-quench; they carry continuous electric charge up to >4g; electric charge magnitude decreases over time, consistent with energy/charge transfer to bulk fields; magnetic monopole cores remain stable; dyon number and charge dispersion shrink markedly at late times, leaving only a few residual dyons.

Conclusion: Dyons in this expanding symmetry-breaking model are dynamically unstable with respect to electric charge loss but topologically robust as magnetic monopoles; their transient nature and charge exchange suggest limited cosmological longevity, with implications for early-universe soliton populations and possible observational signatures.

Abstract: We demonstrate the formation and subsequent evolution of Julia--Zee dyons in $(3+1)$-dimensional classical lattice simulations of an $\mathrm{SU}(2)\to\mathrm{U}(1)$ symmetry-breaking model in an expanding background. The gauge and Higgs fields are prepared thermally, subjected to an ultraviolet filter, and evolved through a mass quench and subsequent expansion. Classical dyons can carry continuous electric charge, and we find charge magnitudes exceeding four times the electric charge unit $g$. Their measured electric-charge magnitudes tend to decrease during the subsequent evolution, consistent with charge exchange with the surrounding fields. At late times, the magnetic monopoles persist while the number of dyons and their observed charge range decrease, with only a few identified dyons remaining at the end of the simulations.

</details>


### [106] [A Practical Guide to Sensitivity Projections for Beyond the Standard Model Physics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36030)
*Francesca Acanfora,Michael J. Baker,Joaquim Iguaz Juan,Aidan Symons,Andrea Thamm*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper clarifies the distinction between goodness-of-fit and hypothesis tests in statistical inference for Beyond the Standard Model (BSM) physics, advocating hypothesis testing for sensitivity projections; it derives exact p-values for Poisson and multinomial data, shows Asimov-data equivalence to Pearson’s χ², compares both approaches in two realistic BSM scenarios (muon collider resonance searches and gamma-ray Hawking radiation probes), and discusses CLs, significance, and systematic uncertainties.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Confusion in applying statistical methods—especially distinguishing goodness-of-fit vs. hypothesis testing—is prevalent in BSM physics due to unfamiliar statistical language and reliance on projections without real data.

Method: Analytical derivation of exact p-values for Poisson and multinomial likelihoods; comparison of asymptotic and Monte Carlo approximations; demonstration of test-statistic equivalence under the Asimov dataset; application to two concrete BSM sensitivity studies using standard frequentist hypothesis testing (including CLs).

Result: Hypothesis testing—not goodness-of-fit—is appropriate for BSM sensitivity projections; all discussed test statistics reduce to Pearson’s χ² under the Asimov approximation; p-values from the two paradigms differ substantially in realistic examples; CLs is contextualized as a bounded frequentist procedure addressing look-elsewhere and nuisance-parameter effects.

Conclusion: The paper establishes a principled, statistically rigorous framework for BSM forecast interpretation, emphasizing correct test selection, proper p-value semantics, and robust treatment of systematics—critical for credible experimental design and interpretation.

Abstract: Statistics is a well-established discipline, yet its application can be confusing, particularly in fields where the language of statistics is unfamiliar and when making projections, where real data is not yet available. Both of these factors are commonplace in Beyond the Standard Model physics. In this work, we clarify the distinction between goodness-of-fit tests, which assess the compatibility of an observation with a single hypothesis, and hypothesis tests, which quantify how strongly some data prefer one hypothesis over another. We emphasise that for Beyond the Standard Model sensitivity projections, hypothesis testing should be used. We give detailed examples using both Poisson and multinomially distributed data, deriving exact $p$-values and discussing Monte Carlo and asymptotic approximations. We further demonstrate that for the Asimov data set all the test statistics discussed, for both goodness-of-fit tests and hypothesis tests, can be approximated by Pearson's $\chi^2$ statistic, and the difference in approaches comes down to the distribution of the test statistics. We also compare goodness-of-fit and hypothesis test $p$-values in two realistic examples. We (1) consider the reach of a muon collider on heavy spin-1 resonances and (2) provide updated projections for the potential of current and future gamma ray telescopes to probe new dark sectors via the observation of Hawking radiation from the explosion of a black hole. Finally, we discuss the CLs method, the significance and systematic errors.

</details>


### [107] [Can inflationary axion fluctuations dominate over misalignment?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36033)
*Seung J. Lee,Sarunas Verner,Bingrong Yu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates whether inflationary quantum fluctuations—not misalignment—can dominate the relic abundance of light stable axion dark matter, finding that standard (minimally coupled) axions violate Planck isocurvature bounds by ~8 orders of magnitude; blue-tilted spectra from nonminimal couplings or inflaton-dependent kinetic functions can evade this bound, but require specific spectral tilts and axion masses; for the QCD axion, misalignment remains dominant.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether inflationary quantum fluctuations of the axion field—not the conventional misalignment mechanism—can set the observed dark matter abundance, while satisfying CMB isocurvature constraints from Planck.
