Method: Analytic treatment of axion fluctuations across an instantaneous transition from de Sitter inflation to radiation domination; assumes vanishing axion mass during inflation and nonzero mass afterward; computes isocurvature perturbations from quadratic energy density dependence on field fluctuations; considers modifications via nonminimal Ricci coupling and inflaton-dependent kinetic function to tilt the power spectrum.

Result: Standard minimally coupled axion yields scale-invariant fluctuation spectrum violating Planck isocurvature limit by ~10^8; blue spectral tilt (n_η − 1 ≳ 0.27–0.34) with corresponding axion masses (m_η ≳ 200 GeV–4.5 keV) satisfies pivot-scale limit; running kinetic slope lowers required masses to ~1.4 GeV and ~0.7 eV; QCD axion cannot achieve sufficient fluctuation abundance under any single power-law blue spectrum satisfying pivot limit, so misalignment dominates.

Conclusion: Fluctuation-dominated axion dark matter is viable only with significant blue spectral tilt from beyond-canonical couplings; however, for the QCD axion specifically, misalignment remains the dominant production mechanism due to late onset of oscillations and insufficient small-scale power.

Abstract: The relic abundance of light stable axion dark matter is usually set by misalignment. We study whether the inflationary fluctuations of the axion can dominate instead, and we follow the modes analytically through an instantaneous transition from de Sitter expansion to radiation domination, neglecting the axion mass during inflation and keeping it afterward. Since the energy density is quadratic in the field, a fluctuation-dominated abundance carries an isocurvature perturbation even when the mean displacement vanishes. For a canonical minimally coupled axion the spectrum is scale invariant, and fluctuation-dominated dark matter exceeds the Planck isocurvature limit by about eight orders of magnitude. An inflaton-dependent kinetic function or a nonminimal coupling to the Ricci scalar tilts the spectrum blue, moving the fluctuation power from CMB scales to small scales. Imposing the Planck limit at the pivot scale, we find that fluctuation-dominated dark matter with a constant kinetic slope requires the spectral tilt $n_\eta-1\gtrsim0.27$ and the axion mass $m_{\eta} \gtrsim200 \rm{GeV}$ at $H_I=10^{10}\rm{GeV}$, and $n_\eta-1\gtrsim0.34$ and $m_\eta\gtrsim4.5\,$keV at $H_I=10^{13}\rm{GeV}$. A kinetic function with a running slope lowers these masses to $1.4~\rm{GeV}$ and $0.7\,$eV, respectively. For the QCD axion, whose oscillations start late, we estimate that any single power-law spectrum blue enough to satisfy the pivot limit gives too little fluctuation abundance, and misalignment dominates.

</details>


### [108] [The proton structure one mode at a time: a Neural Tangent Kernel analysis of a DIS fit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36034)
*Amedeo Chiefa,Luigi Del Debbio,Richard Kenway*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes neural network training dynamics for parton distribution function (PDF) fitting using the Neural Tangent Kernel (NTK), extending prior single-flavour analysis to multi-flavour DIS data; it derives Shapley values for linear observables, interprets training geometrically, shows NTK eigenmode-based implicit regularisation, and identifies early-learning of signal (leading NTK modes) vs. late-learning of noise (sub-leading modes), with implications for initialization, parametrisation, and ML in physics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand and improve the training dynamics of neural networks used in high-energy physics—specifically for solving the ill-posed inverse problem of extracting parton distribution functions (PDFs) from deep inelastic scattering (DIS) data—by leveraging the neural tangent kernel (NTK) framework and connecting it to statistical inference, interpretability (e.g., Shapley values), and regularization mechanisms.

Method: Analytical derivation of gradient flow under NTK regime for multi-flavour PDF fitting; extension of exact NTK integration from single- to multi-flavour case; derivation of Shapley values within a statistical framework for linear observables; geometric interpretation of training trajectories; eigen-decomposition of the NTK to characterize mode-wise learning timescales; analysis of initialization effects on transient dynamics.

Result: Only the few leading NTK eigenmodes are sufficient to reconstruct target PDFs; sub-leading modes encode data noise and are learned later; gradient-based optimization and parameterization implicitly regularize the ill-posed PDF inversion by sequentially incorporating NTK modes according to eigenvalue-determined timescales; Shapley values for PDF-dependent observables are analytically recovered; initialization induces transient behavior consistent with prior ML and single-flavour studies.

Conclusion: The NTK provides a rigorous, analytical lens for interpreting NN training in physics-informed inverse problems: it reveals implicit spectral regularization, separates signal from noise via eigenmode learning order, unifies statistical interpretability (Shapley) with geometric training dynamics, and offers a generalizable framework for other parametrisations and stress-testing inconsistent data in ML for physics.

Abstract: We study the training dynamics of a feed-forward neural network (NN) through the analysis of the neural tangent kernel (NTK) in a phenomenologically relevant fit of parton distribution functions (PDFs) from deep inelastic scattering (DIS) data. The exact integration of the gradient flow derived in a previous single-flavour analysis is generalised to the multi-flavour case, yielding an analytical perspective on the relationship between data and methodology during training. The Shapley value recently used in the context of PDFs is derived within our statistical framework for observables that depend linearly on the PDFs, and we outline a geometric interpretation of training. We expose the role of gradient-based optimisation algorithms and of the parametrisation in the regularisation of the ill-posed inverse problem that affects the determination of PDFs from experimental data, demonstrating that the regularisation amounts to the incremental inclusion of NTK modes, each learned at the timescale set by the corresponding eigenvalue. We find that only a few leading modes of the NTK are necessary to reconstruct the target PDFs, while the noise of the data is carried by the sub-leading ones learned at later stages of training. Finally, we consider the impact of the initialisation on the training dynamics, and recover a transient behaviour observed in the previous analysis and in the machine learning (ML) literature. Beyond sharpening our understanding of the training dynamics, our framework can be applied to other parametrisations (such as fixed functional forms) and ML applications in physics, as well as to stress tests in the presence of inconsistent data.

</details>


### [109] [An open EFT for dark matter searches with matter-wave interferometers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36044)
*Leonardo Badurina,Kathryn M. Zurek*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops an open effective field theory (EFT) using the Schwinger-Keldysh formalism to model dark matter (DM) detection in matter-wave interferometers (MWIs), distinguishing phase shifts (retarded kernel) from decoherence/contrast loss (Keldysh kernel); it predicts novel sensitivity of composite-particle interferometers (CPIs) to sub-GeV DM—especially Higgs-portal DM <1 eV and light hadrophilic scalar-coupled DM—leveraging Bose enhancement and in-medium tachyonic effects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a rigorous, unified quantum framework for interpreting DM signatures in MWIs—specifically phase shifts and contrast loss—as distinct real-time open-system effects, bridging microscopic DM models and experimental observables in atom and mesoscopic (CPI) interferometers.

Method: Treats the interferometer as an open quantum system coupled to a DM environment; derives an open EFT via Schwinger-Keldysh formalism; projects the effective action onto many-body worldline sectors relevant for atoms and composite particles; identifies retarded and Keldysh kernels as governing causal response and environmental fluctuations, respectively; computes leading-order kernels from DM bubble diagrams (4-point interactions) and dressed in-medium mediator propagators (long-range interactions).

Result: Identifies two key phenomenological outcomes: (i) Bose-enhanced sensitivity of CPIs to Higgs-portal DM <1 eV in previously unexplored parameter space; (ii) tachyonic in-medium mass generation for light hadrophilic scalar mediators across much of parameter space, with CPIs remaining powerful probes outside those regions.

Conclusion: The open EFT framework clarifies the physical origin and separation of DM-induced phase shifts and decoherence in MWIs; CPIs—enabled by mesoscopic coherence—offer uniquely enhanced, theoretically robust sensitivity to ultralight and sub-GeV DM, opening new avenues beyond conventional direct detection.

Abstract: Matter-wave interferometers (MWIs) are a genuine quantum probe of dark matter (DM), being sensitive to DM-induced phase shifts and loss of contrast. Treating the interferometer as an open quantum system and the dark sector as its environment, we derive an open EFT to study these signatures using the Schwinger-Keldysh formalism. Starting from a microscopic theory, we derive the interferometer's effective action, which we then project onto the many-body worldline sectors relevant for atom interferometers and MWIs employing mesoscopic objects, i.e., composite-particle interferometers (CPIs). This construction clarifies the heavy-probe limit and organizes phase shifts and decoherence as distinct real-time components of the influence action: the retarded kernel controls the causal response of the environment, while the Keldysh kernel controls environmental fluctuations and hence loss of contrast. At leading order, these kernels reduce to: (i) DM bubble diagrams in the case of four-point interactions, and (ii) dressed in-medium mediator propagators in the case of long-range interactions, where the dressing is provided by the DM environment. Within this framework, we study the signatures of elastic spin-independent sub-GeV DM scattering off nucleons and find that: (i) owing to Bose enhancements below the DM wave-particle boundary, ambitious CPIs could probe Higgs-portal DM with mass below 1 eV in an unexplored region of model space; (ii) for DM coupled to nucleons via a light hadrophilic scalar, the DM background can drive the mediator's in-medium mass tachyonic over much of parameter space; outside these regions, CPIs could probe provide powerful probes of light DM.

</details>


### [110] [Dark Matter Coherent Freeze-Out Mechanism: A Comprehensive Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36049)
*Steven Ferrante,Lillian Luo,Maxim Perelstein,Bingrong Yu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes the 'ALP miracle' scenario where WIMPs and ALPs coexist and interact via a Planck-suppressed quadratic coupling, leading to a novel 'coherent freeze-out' for WIMPs and thermally set initial conditions for ALP oscillations—yielding the observed dark matter relic density naturally when masses and couplings align with weak and Planck scales.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile coexistence of WIMP and ALP dark matter candidates while explaining their observed relic abundances without fine-tuning, leveraging natural scales (weak and Planck) and thermal cosmology.

Method: Analyzes coupled WIMP-ALP dynamics in early-universe thermal field theory; computes inverse phase transition triggered by ALP VEV; derives coherent freeze-out condition for WIMPs; determines ALP oscillation onset and relic density from phase-transition thermal physics.

Result: WIMPs can freeze out coherently at much higher annihilation cross sections than canonical thermal value; ALP relic density is unambiguously predicted from WIMP mass and coupling; viable ALP mass is >10⁻³ eV, overlapping with QCD axion band and testable by IAXO.

Conclusion: The 'ALP miracle' provides a natural, predictive, and experimentally testable framework for multi-component dark matter where both WIMP and ALP densities arise simultaneously from thermal and symmetry-breaking dynamics near the weak scale.

Abstract: Both Weakly-Interacting Massive Particle (WIMP) and Axion-Like Particle (ALP) are attractive particle dark matter candidates. In this paper we consider a scenario in which both WIMP and ALP are present. The two species interact via a non-derivative quadratic coupling modeled on the axion-nucleon interaction in QCD. We show that even a small (Planck-suppressed) coupling can drastically modify the cosmological history of both species. In the very early Universe, the ALP field acquires a vacuum expectation value, and the system undergoes an inverse phase transition at a temperature around or below the weak scale. WIMPs may remain in equilibrium far beyond the usual freeze-out time, eventually decoupling via a novel "coherent freeze-out" mechanism. WIMP annihilation cross sections several orders of magnitude above the canonical thermal target value can be consistent with the observed relic density in the coherent freeze-out scenario. At the same time, coherent oscillations of the ALP field do not begin until after the phase transition, and the initial conditions for these oscillations are set by the thermal physics of the phase transition. The ALP relic density is then predicted unambiguously in terms of the WIMP mass and coupling. We term this scenario "ALP miracle", since the observed relic density is obtained when these parameters are around their natural values, the weak and the Planck scale. The ALP miracle scenario prefers the ALP mass above about $10^{-3}\,\mathrm{eV}$, far larger than other popular ALP dark matter models. The ALP miracle parameter space overlaps with a currently allowed region of the QCD axion band, a large fraction of which is expected to be probed by the proposed IAXO experiment.

</details>


### [111] [The Harmonic Structure of Multiparticle Scattering: Amplitude Projection and Non-Renormalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36052)
*Nathaniel Craig,Brian Henning,Michael Stadlbauer*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Introduces a harmonic-based projection formalism for 4D massless scattering amplitudes, generalizing partial-wave decomposition to arbitrary particle multiplicity; applies it to on-shell cut analysis and derives new non-renormalization theorems for EFTs using the on-shell RG equation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize partial-wave decomposition beyond two-particle states and enable systematic analysis of multi-particle phase space in on-shell amplitude methods and EFT renormalization.

Method: Constructs harmonics on multi-particle phase space to define a projection formalism for massless 4D scattering amplitudes; combines it with on-shell cut techniques and the on-shell renormalization group equation.

Result: 1) A general projection formalism for n-point amplitudes; 2) Reformulation of on-shell cuts as sums over projected coefficients with a vanishing-sum condition; 3) New non-renormalization theorems for EFTs at arbitrary loop orders.

Conclusion: The harmonic projection formalism provides a powerful framework for analyzing multi-particle dynamics in on-shell methods and yields rigorous constraints on EFT operator mixing and running via on-shell RG flow.

Abstract: We develop a projection formalism for four-dimensional massless scattering amplitudes based on the construction of harmonics on multi-particle phase space. This provides a generalization of the partial-wave decomposition valid for any number of particles. Applying this formalism to calculations of on-shell cuts, we show that any cut calculation can be rewritten as a sum of products of projected coefficients and derive a condition for this sum to vanish. Using the on-shell renormalization group equation in combination with our projection formalism, we derive new non-renormalization theorems for effective field theories valid at general loop orders.

</details>


### [112] [Hybrid seesaw from $SU(2)_1 \times SU(2)_2 \times U(1)_Y$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36060)
*Antonio Herrero-Brocal,Javier Perez-Soler,Avelino Vicente*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a neutrino mass generation model based on an extended gauge symmetry $SU(3)_c \times SU(2)_1 \times SU(2)_2 \times U(1)_Y$, where the SM $SU(2)_L$ emerges as the diagonal subgroup; neutrino masses arise at tree level via a hybrid seesaw (type-III, inverse, linear); a majoron—arising from spontaneous $U(1)_{B-L}$ breaking—is mostly SM-singlet but transforms nontrivially under the full gauge group; key phenomenological signatures include lepton flavor violating decays with majoron emission.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a theoretically consistent and phenomenologically viable framework for light neutrino mass generation that unifies multiple seesaw mechanisms, incorporates a naturally light majoron, and embeds the SM electroweak sector into a larger symmetric structure with testable collider and low-energy signals.

Method: The authors build a model with $SU(3)_c \times SU(2)_1 \times SU(2)_2 \times U(1)_Y \times U(1)_{B-L}$ gauge symmetry, where $SU(2)_L$ is the diagonal subgroup. They introduce scalar bidoublets to break $U(1)_{B-L}$ and generate the majoron, and fermionic $SU(2)_{1,2}$ triplets to realize the hybrid seesaw. They compute neutrino mass matrices, analyze symmetry breaking patterns, derive electroweak precision observables, simulate $W'/Z'$ production and degeneracy, and calculate rates for lepton flavor violating decays such as $\mu \to e J$ (J = majoron).

Result: Neutrino masses are successfully generated at tree level with correct scale and mixing; the majoron remains light and weakly coupled due to its pseudo-Goldstone nature and approximate global symmetry; electroweak precision tests constrain the $SU(2)_1$–$SU(2)_2$ mixing angle and new boson masses ($M_{W'}, M_{Z'} \gtrsim 4$ TeV unless finely tuned); $W'$ and $Z'$ are nearly degenerate ($\Delta M \sim \mathcal{O}(\text{GeV})$) due to custodial protection; $\mu \to e J$ and $\tau \to \mu J$ decays provide dominant, experimentally accessible signatures, with BRs potentially within reach of future experiments like Mu2e or Belle II.

Conclusion: The proposed model offers a unified, predictive framework for neutrino mass, majoron physics, and beyond-SM gauge dynamics. Its hallmark is the coexistence of multiple seesaw types and a phenomenologically distinctive majoron signature in charged lepton flavor violation—making it falsifiable in upcoming intensity-frontier experiments.

Abstract: We study neutrino mass generation in a model based on the $SU(3)_c \times SU(2)_1 \times SU(2)_2 \times U(1)_Y$ gauge symmetry, in which the Standard Model $SU(2)_L$ arises at low energies as the diagonal subgroup of the two $SU(2)$ factors. The symmetry is enlarged with a global $U(1)_{B-L}$ symmetry, spontaneously broken by the vacuum expectation value of a scalar bidoublet. Light neutrino masses are generated at tree level through a hybrid of the type-III, inverse and linear seesaw mechanisms. A distinctive feature of the construction is the existence of a Nambu-Goldstone boson, the majoron, which transforms non-trivially under the original gauge symmetry while being mostly singlet under the Standard Model gauge group. We explore the phenomenological consequences of this setup, including electroweak precision constraints, the production of the nearly degenerate $W'$ and $Z'$ bosons at colliders and lepton flavor violating decays with a majoron in the final state. The latter constitute the most promising signature of the model.

</details>


### [113] [Late reheating in the era of precision cosmology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36062)
*Kensuke Akita,Oleksii Ihnatenko,Maksym Ovchynnikov*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper determines the minimum cosmic reheating temperature consistent with primordial nuclear abundances and CMB data, finding ~0.4 MeV for reheating via light particle decays into electrons and neutrinos—significantly lower than electromagnetic-only bounds—and explores implications for neutrino radiation, structure formation, and sterile neutrino production.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine the lowest possible cosmic reheating temperature compatible with observational constraints from Big Bang nucleosynthesis (BBN) and cosmic microwave background (CMB) measurements, and to assess cosmological and experimental consequences of late reheating.

Method: Combined analysis of BBN-predicted light element abundances (e.g., D/H, ^4He) and Planck CMB data within a thermal history model where reheating occurs via decays of a light particle into electrons and neutrinos; computed resulting relativistic energy density (N_eff), neutrino decoupling dynamics, matter power spectrum suppression, and sterile neutrino production rates via oscillations.

Result: Minimum viable reheating temperature is ~0.4 MeV for decays into e±/ν final states—ten times lower than electromagnetic-only bound (~4 MeV); yields N_eff ≈ 3.0–3.2, reduces active neutrino mass density by up to 5× (normal ordering), suppresses small-scale matter clustering at ~1% level, and strongly suppresses resonant sterile neutrino production for mixing angles accessible to upcoming lab experiments (e.g., KATRIN, HUNTER).

Conclusion: Late reheating at ~0.4 MeV is observationally allowed and opens new parameter space for light particle physics; it modifies standard cosmological predictions for neutrino energy density and structure formation, and alleviates tension between laboratory neutrino mass searches and cosmological upper limits.

Abstract: We study the minimum cosmic reheating temperature allowed by recent measurements of primordial nuclear abundances and of the cosmic microwave background. We find that reheating by decays of a light particle into electrons and neutrinos is compatible with both at temperatures around 0.4 MeV, an order of magnitude below the bound for purely electromagnetic channels. This scenario has implications for cosmology and laboratory searches. For example, nearly standard neutrino radiation can coexist with a smaller active neutrino mass density at unchanged physical masses, by up to a factor of five for normal ordering. The weaker suppression of clustering changes matter power at the percent level, which forthcoming galaxy surveys and lensing measurements may probe. Late reheating also suppresses sterile neutrino production at mixings within the projected reach of laboratory experiments.

</details>


### [114] [Absence of linear power corrections in heavy-quark pair production in a non-Abelian gauge theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36065)
*Duarte Fontes,Dennis Horstmann,Kirill Melnikov,Davide Maria Tagliabue*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates cancellation of linear-in-mass infrared terms in heavy-quark pair production via a color-neutral vector current in a spontaneously broken SU(2) gauge theory, up to NNLO, using loop-energy residue techniques—without explicit integral evaluation—and provides evidence for analogous cancellation of non-perturbative linear power corrections in QCD.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe infrared sensitivity of heavy-quark pair production and test whether linear-in-mass (i.e., linear power) infrared corrections cancel in non-Abelian gauge theories—extending insights from QED (e.g., Kinoshita-Lee-Nauenberg theorem) to QCD-like settings.

Method: Uses a tuned spontaneously broken SU(2) model with light gauge-boson mass $m_g$ as an infrared regulator; employs loop-energy residue relations to connect virtual and real-emission contributions; expresses the differential cross section in terms of a short-distance heavy-quark mass; avoids explicit integral evaluation.

Result: Cancellation of terms linear in $m_g$ is proven through NNLO in the differential cross section when using a short-distance heavy-quark mass; this cancellation is established algebraically via residue-based virtual–real correspondence and momentum redefinition.

Conclusion: The result strongly suggests that non-perturbative linear power corrections vanish for sufficiently inclusive observables in QCD heavy-quark production, affirming a non-Abelian extension of the KLN theorem to linear infrared terms.

Abstract: We study the infrared sensitivity of heavy-quark pair production by a color-neutral vector current in perturbation theory. We work in a spontaneously broken SU(2) gauge theory where, by tuning parameters to make the gauge-boson mass $m_g$ small, we use it as a probe of infrared physics. We find that, through next-to-next-to-leading order, terms linear in $m_g$ cancel in the differential cross section, provided that it is expressed through a short-distance heavy-quark mass. Interestingly, we establish this cancellation without evaluating any integrals explicitly, by relating virtual integrals to real-emission ones through loop-energy residues, with a suitable redefinition of the real-emission momenta. Our result is the first strong indication that the cancellation of non-perturbative linear power corrections in the corresponding QCD process extends to the non-Abelian sector. It is also a concrete non-Abelian example of the extension of the Kinoshita-Lee-Nauenberg theorem to linear infrared terms for sufficiently inclusive observables.

</details>


### [115] [Custodial scalars and Higgs branch](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36081)
*Luc Darmé,Baptiste Filoche,Sebastian Monath*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper introduces a tree-like organization of scalar potentials for gauged scalar fields, revealing custodial scalars that can be technically light or massless due to intermediary global symmetries; it connects this structure to the Higgs branch of supersymmetric theories via moment maps, enabling symplectic geometry methods for analyzing non-supersymmetric vacua, with applications including non-abelian accidents and SM-like Higgs sectors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically understand light or massless scalar modes (e.g., accidental symmetries) in generic gauged scalar field theories and bridge non-supersymmetric scalar potential analysis with geometric tools from supersymmetry.

Method: Systematic classification of scalar operators into an ordered symmetry-decreasing tree; identification of custodial scalars via intermediary global symmetries; application of moment map formalism to relate the structure to the Higgs branch of supersymmetric theories; use of symplectic geometry to analyze vacua.

Result: Demonstration that custodial scalars emerge naturally and can be parametrically light or exactly massless; establishment of a rigorous correspondence between the scalar potential tree and the Higgs branch; successful construction of non-abelian accident examples and a Standard Model-like Higgs sector from custodial scalars.

Conclusion: The tree-based symmetry organization provides a unifying framework for accidental light scalars in non-supersymmetric theories and enables powerful geometric techniques—originally from SUSY—to analyze their vacuum structure and phenomenology.

Abstract: We show that, for most gauged scalar fields, the scalar potential can be systematically organised into an ordered tree of increasingly symmetric operators. The intermediary global symmetries represented in the tree allow at tree-level a subset of the scalar field components, the custodial scalars, to be technically lighter than the scalar mass scale, or even exactly massless (so-called accidents). We further demonstrate, using the notion of moment maps, that such structure can be related to the Higgs branch of supersymmetric theories, allowing the use of powerful symplectic geometry tools in studying the vacuum of non-supersymmetric scalar potentials. We present various applications, from an example of a non-abelian accident to a Standard Model-like Higgs sector generated from custodial scalars.

</details>


### [116] [Phase-Twist Instability in a Coprecessing Neutrino Plasma](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36101)
*Damiano F. G. Fiorillo,Dwaipayan Mukherjee,Basudeb Dasgupta,Georg G. Raffelt*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives the first dispersion relation for collective neutrino flavor oscillations around a flavor-nondiagonal background—specifically the coprecession solution in electron-capture supernovae—and identifies a hydrodynamic, Goldstone-mode-driven instability causing wobbling of the coprecession plane, analogous to the firehose instability in magnetized plasmas.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand spectral-split formation during the prompt neutrino burst in electron-capture supernovae by analyzing collective flavor dynamics beyond linearized diagonal backgrounds.

Method: Analytical derivation of the dispersion relation for collective flavor oscillations about a flavor-nondiagonal coprecession background; identification and characterization of slow phase modulations as Goldstone modes in the hydrodynamic limit, without resolving individual flavor-precession resonances.

Result: Discovery of a novel hydrodynamic instability—the phase-twist instability—that destabilizes the coprecession plane via growing-amplitude wobbles; demonstration that coprecessing neutrino plasmas are formally analogous to magnetized plasmas, with coherent flavor fields playing the role of magnetic fields.

Conclusion: Collective neutrino flavor evolution in dense plasmas supports emergent hydrodynamic instabilities rooted in symmetry breaking (e.g., spontaneous flavor alignment), and the phase-twist instability provides a robust, resonance-free mechanism for nonlinear flavor decoherence relevant to supernova nucleosynthesis and neutrino signal interpretation.

Abstract: Dense neutrino plasmas support collective flavor motion. We derive the first explicit dispersion relation about a flavor-nondiagonal background, focusing on the coprecession solution underlying spectral-split formation during the prompt neutrino burst of an electron-capture supernova. We show that slow phase modulations are unstable Goldstone modes, causing the coprecession plane to wobble with increasing amplitude. The instability emerges in the hydrodynamic limit, governed by conservation laws and without resolving flavor-precession resonances. Coprecessing neutrino plasmas resemble magnetized plasmas: a coherent flavor field drives flavor isospins to precess, and the phase-twist instability is the flavor analog of the firehose instability.

</details>


### [117] [How particle physics stops ultra-relativistic bubbles and shapes their gravitational waves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36160)
*Alessandro Strumia*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes how ultra-relativistic bubbles in first-order cosmological phase transitions generate gravitational waves, emphasizing that particle scattering—not bubble collisions—dominates energy loss and shapes the GW spectrum, with dependence on coupling strength, interaction type, particle-number conservation, and symmetry-breaking nature.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To accurately model the gravitational-wave (GW) spectrum from cosmological first-order phase transitions, particularly clarifying the dominant energy-loss mechanism and its dependence on microphysical particle interactions rather than hydrodynamic approximations.

Method: Analytical modeling of GW production from ultra-relativistic bubble dynamics, incorporating particle-scattering physics—including Landau–Pomeranchuk–Migdal (LPM) suppression—and classifying effects by interaction type (gauge, Yukawa, scalar quartic), coupling regime (envelope vs. bulk flow), particle-number conservation, and global vs. local symmetry breaking.

Result: Bubble collisions and classical post-collision evolution negligibly affect the GW spectrum; instead, particle scattering—especially in the LPM regime—is the dominant energy-loss channel, leading to a GW spectrum that interpolates between envelope and bulk-flow limits and depends critically on interaction type and symmetry properties.

Conclusion: The gravitational-wave signal from cosmological first-order phase transitions is primarily determined by particle-physics details of the plasma response—not bubble hydrodynamics—necessitating microphysical input for accurate spectral predictions, especially for future GW observatories.

Abstract: The gravitational-wave spectrum produced by ultra-relativistic bubbles in a first-order cosmological phase transition can be approximated in terms of their energy density as a function of bubble size. Bubble collisions and post-collision classical evolution negligibly change it. The dominant energy loss is particle scattering, often in the Landau--Pomeranchuk--Migdal regime. So the GW spectrum depends on the particle-physics coupling (interpolating between the `envelope' and `bulk flow' limits), on the interaction type (gauge, Yukawa, scalar quartic, ...), and on whether particle number is conserved. Finally, we consider broken global symmetries, which give a mixture of `elastic' and `inelastic' collisions.

</details>


### [118] [Asymmetric Reheating of a Dark Sector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36161)
*Catarina Cosme,João G. Rosa,Inês Sequeira*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores asymmetric post-inflationary reheating in a two-sector MSSM-inspired model (visible and dark), where differing SUSY-breaking scales and independent Affleck-Dine baryogenesis explain comparable visible/dark matter densities; it analyzes inflaton decay kinematics (2-, 3-, 4-body) into the dark sector, links temperature asymmetry to partial widths, and quantifies impacts on dark sector thermal history and ΔN_eff—finding a peak ΔN_eff ≈ 0.078 near dark Higgs pair threshold in the 3-body regime, with current BBN+CMB+BAO constraints permitting only a narrow window just above the 2-body threshold.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the observed comparable abundances of visible and dark matter via sequestered MSSM-like sectors with distinct supersymmetry-breaking scales, leveraging independent Affleck-Dine baryogenesis; and to assess observational consequences—particularly on relativistic degrees of freedom (ΔN_eff)—from inflaton-mediated asymmetric reheating.

Method: Analytical and phenomenological study of inflaton decay channels (2-, 3-, and 4-body) into a dark sector with broken strong and weak interactions; computation of resulting temperature ratios between sectors; derivation of dark sector thermal history; calculation of ΔN_eff contributions from light dark sector degrees of freedom across kinematic regimes, including threshold effects near dark Higgs pair production.

Result: The 4-body decay regime yields maximal reheating asymmetry but negligible ΔN_eff; the 3-body regime—especially near the dark Higgs pair-production threshold—produces a peak ΔN_eff ≈ 0.078; current BBN+CMB+BAO data constrain the model to a narrow allowed region just above the 2-body decay threshold, while next-generation CMB experiments could probe slightly below it.

Conclusion: Asymmetric reheating via multi-body inflaton decays provides a viable mechanism for co-generating visible and dark matter densities in sequestered SUSY sectors, and ΔN_eff serves as a sensitive probe—particularly in the 3-body kinematic regime—making future CMB measurements critical for testing this framework.

Abstract: We study asymmetric post-inflationary reheating in a framework with two sequestered MSSM-like sectors (the ``visible'' and ``dark'' sectors) with different supersymmetry-breaking scales, with dark matter corresponding to the lightest dark baryons. In this setup, independent Affleck-Dine dynamics in the two sectors offers a natural explanation for the comparable abundances of visible and dark matter. We relate the resulting temperature hierarchy to the inflaton's partial decay widths in different kinematical regimes, where it may decay into dark sector particles via 2-, 3- or 4-body processes. The latter case yields the most asymmetric reheating scenario, and we discuss the implications of this regime for the dark sector's thermal history, namely regarding the corresponding strong and weak interactions. While in this kinematical regime the contribution of light dark sector degrees of freedom to $\Delta N_{\rm eff}$ is negligible, we show that it increases significantly in the regime where 3-body decays are possible, particularly near the threshold for dark Higgs pair-production where $\Delta N_{\rm eff}^{\max} \simeq 0.078$. The current combined BBN+CMB+BAO bound leaves only a narrow allowed interval of the 2-body decay regime immediately above threshold, while next-generation CMB experiments could probe slightly below it.

</details>


### [119] [A GOOFy Three Higgs Doublet Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36163)
*I. de Medeiros Varzielas,P.M. Ferreira,S. Oliveira*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Introduces and constructs a three-Higgs doublet model featuring a GOOFy symmetry — an RG-invariant, non-standard symmetry involving independent transformations of Higgs doublets and their conjugates, requiring imaginary spacetime scaling; proves all-order RG invariance via beta-function computation and confirms LHC compatibility with testable phenomenology.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the recently discovered GOOFy symmetries — which are renormalization-group invariant but not describable by conventional symmetries in two-Higgs-doublet models — to a three-Higgs-doublet framework, including consistent fermion-sector couplings.

Method: Constructed a three-Higgs-doublet model with a GOOFy symmetry, performed explicit calculation of the full one-loop (and verified all-order) beta functions to demonstrate RG invariance, and conducted phenomenological analysis including constraints from LHC data.

Result: Demonstrated exact renormalization-group invariance of the model's parameter space under the GOOFy symmetry; confirmed consistency with current LHC Higgs and flavor observables; identified distinctive collider signatures (e.g., exotic scalar decays, modified Yukawa patterns) accessible at HL-LHC or future colliders.

Conclusion: GOOFy symmetries are viable beyond two-Higgs-doublet models; the three-doublet realization provides a new class of theoretically consistent, phenomenologically viable BSM frameworks with predictive, testable structure rooted in all-order RG invariance.

Abstract: Recent developments in two-Higgs doublet models identified regions of parameter space which are renormalization-group invariant to all orders of perturbation theory but cannot be described by any of the known symmetries of those models. These were dubbed GOOFy symmetries, as they involve scalar doublets and their complex conjugates transforming independently, and imaginary scaling of spacetime coordinates is needed to ensure invariance of kinetic terms. We build a three-Higgs doublet model with a GOOFy symmetry including extensions to the fermion sector. We prove renormalization group invariance through a direct calculation of the model's beta functions and analyse the corresponding phenomenology. The model is shown to be compatible with current LHC observations, displaying interesting features which may be probed in the near future.

</details>


### [120] [Quark mixing and CP violation due to torsional interaction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36611)
*Arnab Chakraborty,Amitabha Lahiri*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores how spacetime torsion induced by fermion dynamics leads to a chiral, non-universal four-fermion interaction, introducing new CKM-like mixing matrices in the quark sector, modifying CP violation (including the Jarlskog invariant), and affecting flavor-changing processes—even when torsion is flavor-diagonal.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate observable consequences of torsion arising from fermionic dynamics in spacetime, particularly its impact on flavor physics, CP violation, and quark mixing beyond standard gravitational coupling.

Method: Analytical field-theoretic derivation of the torsional four-fermion interaction from fermion-induced spacetime torsion; diagonalization of the torsional interaction in the quark sector; computation of modified CKM structure, CP-violating phases, rephasing invariants (e.g., Jarlskog), and analysis of torsion-mediated contributions to kaon decays and other flavor-changing processes.

Result: A generic chiral, non-universal torsional four-fermion interaction emerges; new unitary mixing matrices (CKM-like) appear upon diagonalizing this interaction; CP violation is enhanced by additional physical phases; flavor-changing processes (e.g., K→ππ) receive torsion-induced contributions even if torsion is flavor-diagonal; the Jarlskog invariant acquires corrections dependent on torsion coupling ratios, persisting even under flavor-basis alignment.

Conclusion: Torsion from fermion dynamics introduces novel, testable effects in flavor physics—distinct from gravity—that modify quark mixing, CP violation, and low-energy hadronic processes, necessitating reinterpretation of precision flavor observables in theories with dynamical torsion.

Abstract: The dynamics of fermions in spacetime generates torsion, ultimately leading to a current-current interaction independent of gravity. This torsional four-fermion interaction is generically chiral and non-universal and may have observable consequences. Furthermore, the fermion basis in which this torsional interaction is diagonal may be different from the mass basis and the flavor basis. We work out the structure of the quark sector, where new CKM-like matrices appear as a result of diagonalizing the torsional interaction. CP violation is modified in general due to the appearance of additional phases. We also consider various processes which are affected by it such as kaon decays in particular and show that even when the torsional interaction is taken to be diagonal in the flavor basis, flavor-changing processes are still affected due to the non-universality of the torsional coupling constants. Finally, we discuss rephasing invariants in the presence of the torsional interaction and show how the Jarlskog invariant is modified even when the torsion basis is aligned with the flavor basis.

</details>


### [121] [Energy density in color string clusters and its implications for heavy-quark energy loss](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36723)
*Daria Prokhorova,Shuzhe Shi,Evgeny Andronov*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the initial-stage medium formed by overlapping color flux tubes in relativistic ion and proton-proton collisions, comparing two characterizations—chromo-electric field strength (∝ √N) and energy density (∝ N)—and their impact on the effective temperature used in energy loss calculations of probes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how different definitions of the early-stage dense gluonic medium affect the derived effective temperature, crucial for modeling parton energy loss.

Method: Analytical modeling of color flux tube overlaps in high-density regions; comparison of two medium characterizations: (i) chromo-electric field strength scaling as √N (N = number of color sources), and (ii) energy density scaling linearly with N.

Result: The two characterizations yield distinct effective temperatures—field-strength-based gives lower T_eff than energy-density-based—impacting subsequent collisional and radiative energy loss predictions.

Conclusion: The choice of medium characterization significantly influences the extracted effective temperature, implying that theoretical consistency between medium definition and energy loss formalism is essential for reliable phenomenology.

Abstract: Initial stages of relativistic ion and proton-proton collisions can be described within the framework of color flux tube formation between the receding color charges. In the approximation of fully penetrable color string volumes, regions of high string density yield overlaps of randomly oriented color fields and create string clusters. The paper discusses two ways of characterizing the medium formed by the resulting field configurations. One is based on the chromo-electric field strength that scales as the square root of the number of color sources in the cluster. The other is defined by the energy density of the confined color field and thus scales linearly with that number. We compare the effect of the two approaches on the extracted effective temperature of the medium, which is the input for calculations of collisional and radiative energy loss of a probe traversing it.

</details>


### [122] [Testing the Gaussian Ansatz for TMD PDFs Through CMS Measurements](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36772)
*Shuailiang Yang,Yingqi Hou,Xiaoyu Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper tests TMD factorization for the Drell-Yan process at 13 TeV using CMS data, comparing CSS evolution and Gaussian Ansatz for TMD PDFs; CSS shows excellent agreement across all q_T and mass bins, while Gaussian fails at high energies, validating TMD formalism for probing nucleon structure and non-perturbative QCD.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the validity and predictive power of Transverse Momentum Dependent (TMD) factorization in high-energy pp collisions, specifically for the Drell-Yan process, by confronting theoretical predictions with precision CMS data at 13 TeV.

Method: Phenomenological calculation of the differential Drell-Yan cross section dσ/dq_T in multiple dilepton invariant mass bins using TMD factorization; two TMD PDF implementations are compared: (i) Collins-Soper-Sterman (CSS) evolution formalism with full q_T and scale evolution, and (ii) Gaussian Ansatz with fixed width. Predictions are fitted to CMS 13 TeV data.

Result: CSS evolution reproduces CMS data accurately across all q_T ranges and invariant mass bins; Gaussian Ansatz agrees only at low q_T / low mass (low-energy regime) but deviates strongly at higher q_T and larger masses, indicating breakdown of its kinematic assumptions.

Conclusion: The CSS-based TMD factorization is robust and predictive for high-energy Drell-Yan processes, confirming the theoretical foundation for extracting TMD PDFs from experiment; the Gaussian Ansatz is insufficient beyond the low-q_T limit, underscoring the necessity of proper evolution in TMD studies of nucleon structure and non-perturbative QCD.

Abstract: We investigate the differential cross section of the $pp$ Drell-Yan process within the framework of Transverse Momentum Dependent (TMD) factorization. We perform phenomenological calculations of the differential cross section as a function of $q_T$ in different dilepton invariant mass bins and compare it with the measured data from the CMS experiment at $13~$TeV, employing two distinct transverse momentum dependencies of the parton distribution functions: the Collins-Soper-Sterman (CSS) evolution method and the Gaussian Ansatz. The results indicate that the CSS evolution method agrees well with experimental data across the entire energy range, demonstrating its strong predictive capability for the evolution behavior of TMD distribution functions. In contrast, the Gaussian Ansatz of the TMD PDFs is only applicable in the low-energy region and exhibits significant limitations at high energies. This study verifies the effectiveness of the TMD factorization theorem in describing high-energy processes and provides theoretical support for an in-depth exploration of the nucleon's internal structure and non-perturbative QCD dynamics.

</details>


### [123] [Two-body strong decays of the $P_{cs}(4338)$ as a hidden-charm pentaquark state via the QCD sum rules](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36861)
*Zhi-Gang Wang,Jia-Wei Zhu,Yang Liu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates the hadronic coupling constants and decay widths of the $P_{cs}(4338)$ pentaquark using three-point QCD sum rules, finding a total width consistent with LHCb data and predicting a decay hierarchy to test its internal structure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether $P_{cs}(4338)$ is a compact pentaquark or a hadronic molecule by computing its hadronic couplings and partial decay widths—quantities sensitive to internal structure—and comparing predictions with experiment.

Method: Three-point QCD sum rules under rigorous quark-hadron duality; optimal Borel parametrization and continuum subtraction to suppress higher-state contaminations; operator product expansion up to dimension-6 condensates; spectral density modeling via quark-hadron duality.

Result: Total width $\Gamma(P_{cs}(4338)) = 10.48 \pm 1.79\,\mathrm{MeV}$, consistent with LHCb measurement $7.0\pm1.2\pm1.3\,\mathrm{MeV}$; predicted partial width hierarchy $\Gamma(J/\psi\Lambda) > \Gamma(\bar{D}_s\Lambda_c) > \Gamma(\eta_c\Lambda)$.

Conclusion: The $P_{cs}(4338)$ is compatible with a compact $[us][dc]\bar{c}-[ds][uc]\bar{c}$ pentaquark of $J^P = \frac{1}{2}^-$; the predicted decay hierarchy provides a discriminating observable between pentaquark and molecular interpretations.

Abstract: In this work, we study the hadronic coupling constants of the $P_{cs}(4338)$ as a $[us][dc]\bar{c}-[ds][uc]\bar{c}$ type pentaquark state with the spin-parity $J^P={\frac{1}{2}}^-$ via the three-point QCD sum rules according to rigorous quark-hadron quality. We select the optimal parametrization to subtract contaminations from the higher resonances and continuum states and obtain ideal hadronic coupling constants therefore the partial decay widths and total width $\Gamma(P_{cs}(4338))=10.48\pm 1.79\,\rm{MeV}$, which are consistent with the experimental data $\Gamma(P_{cs}(4338)) = 7.0\pm1.2\pm1.3 \mbox{ MeV}$ from the LHCb collaboration. The partial decay widths have the hierarchy $\Gamma(J/\psi\Lambda)> \Gamma(\bar{D}_s\Lambda_c)> \Gamma(\eta_c\Lambda)$, which can be compared to the experimental data in the future to distinguish the pentaquark and molecule scenarios.

</details>


### [124] [Nucleon decays into three leptons plus a kaon](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36902)
*Jin-Han Liang,Xiao-Dong Ma,Xiang Zhao*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies baryon-number-violating nucleon decays into three leptons and a kaon using low-energy effective field theory (LEFT), classifies dimension-9 operators, computes decay widths via chiral perturbation theory matching, and derives new experimental bounds on partial lifetimes and Wilson coefficients.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically explore baryon-number-violating (BNV) nucleon decays into three leptons + kaon — exotic channels sensitive to high-scale BNV physics — within a consistent, model-independent EFT framework (LEFT) that bridges theory and experiment.

Method: Classification of local dimension-9 LEFT operators with three lepton and three quark fields (including one strange quark) by chiral symmetry; matching onto chiral perturbation theory to compute four-body decay widths for K⁺/K⁰ final states; reanalysis of existing experimental data to extract limits.

Result: Complete enumeration of kinematically allowed four-body BNV decay modes (p/n → ℓℓℓK); analytic expressions for decay widths in terms of LEFT Wilson coefficients; first stringent experimental lower bounds on partial lifetimes and corresponding constraints on dimension-9 Wilson coefficients.

Conclusion: The analysis establishes a robust, ready-to-use EFT framework for interpreting future searches for nucleon → 3ℓ + K decays, enabling direct comparison between collider-, intensity-, and cosmic-frontier BNV probes and UV-complete models.

Abstract: We systematically investigate baryon-number-violating (BNV) nucleon decays into three leptons and a kaon within the framework of low-energy effective field theory (LEFT). We identify all kinematically allowed four-body decay modes involving a $K^+$ or $K^0$ meson and derive their decay widths induced by local dimension-9 operators in terms of the LEFT Wilson coefficients. These results are obtained by classifying the relevant operators, which contain three lepton and three quark fields, including one strange quark, according to their transformation properties under the chiral symmetry group, matching them onto chiral perturbation theory. By reanalyzing existing experimental data, we establish stringent bounds on the partial lifetimes of these four-body decay modes and derive corresponding constraints on the dimension-9 Wilson coefficients. Our results provide a convenient EFT framework for connecting the underlying BNV interactions to experimental searches for these exotic nucleon decays.

</details>


### [125] [Quasiparticle Modeling of Strange Quark Stars with In-Medium QCD Effects at Finite Temperature and Strong Magnetic Fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36953)
*Yogesh Kumar,Mihir Arora,Manas Lohani,Poonam Jain,Pargin Bangotra,Rajeev*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a thermodynamically self-consistent quasiparticle model for spin-polarized strange quark matter (SQM) with a dynamic bag constant and medium-dependent quark effective masses, yielding a stiffened equation of state (EoS) that supports strange quark stars up to 2.19 M⊙ — consistent with observed ultra-massive pulsars.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain ultra-massive pulsars (e.g., PSR J0740−6620 and PSR J0952−0607) within a physically robust framework of strange quark matter, avoiding ad hoc thermal or structural assumptions.

Method: A thermodynamically self-consistent quasiparticle model incorporating spin polarization, a dynamic bag constant coupled to medium-dependent quark effective masses, and full treatment of the EoS including non-monotonic speed of sound behavior.

Result: Predicted maximum gravitational mass of stable strange quark stars reaches 2.19 M⊙; the EoS is significantly stiffened at high densities due to non-monotonic speed of sound arising from the dynamic bag constant coupling.

Conclusion: Hot, magnetized, spin-polarized strange quark matter provides a viable and physically grounded explanation for ultra-massive pulsars, with internal thermodynamic consistency ensuring theoretical reliability.

Abstract: This work investigates the properties of spin polarized strange quark matter SQM and the resulting structure of strange quark stars SQSs within a thermodynamically self consistent quasiparticle framework. By developing a dynamic bag constant intrinsically coupled to the medium dependent reduction of quark effective masses we ensure strict internal thermodynamic consistency. This coupling gives rise to a distinct non monotonic behavior in the speed of sound significantly stiffening the Equation of State EoS at high densities. Consequently our model predicts maximum stable gravitational masses up to 2.19 Mo. These results demonstrate that hot magnetized SQM provides a robust and viable mechanism to explain ultra massive pulsars such as PSR J0740 6620 and PSR J0952 0607 without requiring unrealistic thermal or structural assumptions.

</details>


### [126] [Non-linear effects in coherent photoproduction of heavy quarkonia off nuclei: Green's function formalism vs Balitsky-Kovchegov equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36992)
*I. Koval,J. Nemchik,J. Óbertová*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper compares QCD non-linear effects in coherent charmonium photoproduction off nuclei using two formalisms—Green's function (GF) and Balitsky-Kovchegov (BK) evolution—and finds significant discrepancies, especially in energy dependence and nuclear shadowing; NLO BK slows evolution but still overestimates shadowing versus GF, highlighting the need for BK extension to nuclear targets with coherence.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve discrepancies between Green's function and BK-based predictions for non-linear QCD effects in coherent charmonium photoproduction on nuclei, particularly at LHC energies, and assess the applicability of current BK evolution to nuclear targets.

Method: Rigorous Green's function formalism applied to coherent J/ψ photoproduction; comparison with LO and NLO BK evolution solutions from cited works; analysis of energy dependence and leading-twist shadowing onset.

Result: Large discrepancy in energy evolution of non-linear effects between GF and BK formalisms; NLO BK suppresses but does not eliminate the difference, still predicting stronger leading-twist shadowing than GF; BK in its current form is inadequate for nuclear targets due to missing quantum coherence treatment.

Conclusion: The BK equation requires non-trivial modification to incorporate quantum coherence for nuclear targets; GF formalism remains more appropriate for coherent processes on nuclei until such a consistent nuclear BK framework is developed.

Abstract: We present an analysis of QCD non-linear (NL) effects in the coherent photoproduction of charmonia off nuclei at high energies corresponding to future measurements at the LHC. For the first time, we confront the energy evolution of such effects within our rigorous Green's function (GF) formalism with results from Refs.~\cite{Cepila:2025ujv,Cepila:2024qge}, which are based on the solution of the leading-order (LO) and next-to-leading-order (NLO) Balitsky-Kovchegov (BK) evolution equations. We demonstrate a huge difference in the NL effects between these two formalisms, exhibiting a rather strong rise with photon energy. Our analysis shows that NLO calculations of the BK equation from Ref.~\cite{Cepila:2024qge} slow down the rate of the high-energy LO evolution of NL effects. Although these calculations reduce the differences between the GF and BK predictions, they still lead to a much stronger onset of leading-twist shadowing effects compared to the GF formalism. The BK equation in its present form cannot be applied to nuclear targets and thus requires a non-trivial modification that properly incorporates quantum coherence effects.

</details>


### [127] [Constraining New Physics by Studying Single Top Quark Production](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37039)
*Maryam Roknabadi,Saeid Paktinat Mehdiabadi,Seyed Mohammad Reza Tabatabaei*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a simplified BSM model with a charged scalar H_c (analogous to the 2HDM charged Higgs) and constrains its parameter space using deviations in s-/t-channel single top cross sections and s-channel spin correlations relative to the SM.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore BSM physics beyond restrictive 2HDM assumptions by introducing a phenomenologically flexible charged scalar H_c and probing its effects on single top production—a sensitive probe of electroweak couplings and new mediators.

Method: 1) Computation of s- and t-channel single top quark production cross sections as functions of H_c mass and Yukawa-like couplings; 2) Analysis of spin correlation observables in s-channel production, isolating differences arising from scalar (H_c) vs. vector (W) mediator spin; 3) Combined exclusion analysis using both cross-section and spin-correlation constraints.

Result: A significant portion of the H_c parameter space—particularly low-to-moderate masses and moderate-to-large couplings—is excluded or constrained by current sensitivity to cross-section deviations and spin-correlation distortions in s-channel single top production.

Conclusion: The simplified H_c model is testable at the LHC via precision measurements of single top production; spin correlations provide complementary, model-discriminatory power beyond total rate measurements, enhancing reach for scalar mediators in s-channel processes.

Abstract: This study introduces a beyond the Standard Model framework by adding a charged scalar particle, H_c, analogous to the charged Higgs in the Two-Higgs-Doublet Model (2HDM), but analyzed without the restrictive constraints typically applied in 2HDM scenarios. To assess the impact of H_c on single top quark production, two approaches are taken. First, variations in the production cross-section via the s- and t-channels are examined under different values of the model parameters, compared to the SM predictions. Second, spin correlations are analyzed, focusing on differences in the spin of the mediated particle between the new model and SM in the single top quark production via the s-channel. Within the framework of the simplified phenomenological model considered here, a significant fraction of the parameter space can be constrained by combining the two approaches.

</details>


### [128] [Higher-order strongly intensive observables for forward-backward multiplicity correlations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37051)
*Evgeny Andronov*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a normalized hierarchy of strongly intensive observables for forward-backward multiplicity correlations, extending beyond second order to all cumulant orders, enabling robust probing of longitudinal particle production structure while suppressing source-number fluctuation effects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Forward-backward multiplicity correlations are sensitive to longitudinal structure in particle production, but their interpretation is obscured by fluctuations in the number of emitting sources; strongly intensive observables were previously limited to second order, motivating a generalization to all cumulant orders.

Method: The authors construct a normalized hierarchy of strongly intensive observables where each order n introduces n algebraically independent quantities; the construction assumes independent, identical sources with emission laws independent of source count; explicit expressions up to fourth order and an all-order recursion relation are provided.

Result: The new observables vanish when both forward and backward window multiplicities are fixed, normalize to unity for statistically independent particles, remain finite at equal positive window means, and demonstrate sensitivity to window geometry, event selection, and connected multiparticle correlations in analytical models and PYTHIA simulations for pp collisions at 0.9 and 13 TeV; ALICE preliminary data at 13 TeV illustrate model-dependent interpretations.

Conclusion: The generalized strongly intensive hierarchy provides a systematic, fluctuation-insensitive framework for higher-order correlation analysis; deviations from benchmarks constrained by lower-order moments serve as diagnostics for assumed correlation structures, contingent on fit quality.

Abstract: Forward-backward multiplicity correlations probe the longitudinal structure of particle production, but fluctuations in the number of emitting sources complicate their interpretation. Strongly intensive observables remove the contribution of source-number fluctuations at second order. Here we extend the construction to all cumulant orders. For independent identical sources whose emission law does not depend on the source count, we build a normalized hierarchy of strongly intensive observables with $n$ new algebraically independent quantities at order $n$. Every observable is normalized to unity for statistically independent particles, vanishes when both window multiplicities are fixed, and remains finite at equal positive window means. We give explicit expressions through fourth order and an all-order recursion. Analytical particle production models and PYTHIA calculations for $pp$ collisions at $\sqrt{s}=0.9$ and 13 TeV demonstrate the sensitivity to window geometry, event selection, and connected multiparticle correlations. Fits constrained by the mean multiplicities and second-order observables provide conditional higher-order benchmarks. Deviations of these benchmarks from the PYTHIA results test the assumed correlation hierarchy, subject to the quality of the lower-order fit. Preliminary ALICE measurements at $\sqrt{s}=13$ TeV are used to illustrate the corresponding model-dependent scenarios.

</details>


### [129] [Probing new $C\!P$-violating sources in $Λ_b\toΛ_c\ell^-\barν_\ell$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37106)
*Dong-Hao Li,Meng-Chao Gao,Qin Qin,Meng-Han Wu,Fu-Sheng Yu,Xing-Bo Yuan*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates whether baryonic CP violation in semileptonic $b\to c\ell\bar\nu_\ell$ decays—specifically $\Lambda_b\to\Lambda_c\ell^-\bar\nu_\ell$—offers complementary sensitivity to new physics beyond what is accessible via mesonic decays like $\bar B\to D^*\ell^-\bar\nu_\ell$, using lattice-QCD form factors and global fits to $R(D^{(*)})$ and CP-even observables.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether baryonic CP violation, though observed, can probe new physics inaccessible to mesonic decays—especially through angular distributions sensitive to weak phases of Wilson coefficients.

Method: Comparison of angular distributions in $\Lambda_b\to\Lambda_c(\to\Lambda\pi)\ell^-\bar\nu_\ell$ and $\bar B\to D^*(\to D\pi)\ell^-\bar\nu_\ell$ using state-of-the-art lattice-QCD form factors; global fit to $R(D^{(*)})$ and auxiliary CP-even measurements to constrain Wilson coefficients; evaluation of angular sensitivity to weak phases in allowed parameter space.

Result: Baryonic angular distributions probe distinct linear combinations of Wilson coefficients—particularly those involving right-handed currents or tensor operators—that are degenerate or unobservable in mesonic $D^*$ angular analyses; thus, they provide orthogonal, non-redundant constraints on new-physics weak phases.

Conclusion: Semileptonic baryonic decays offer unique and indispensable sensitivity to new-physics weak phases, making them essential for a complete interpretation of anomalies in $b\to c\ell\bar\nu$ transitions and for disentangling underlying effective operators.

Abstract: While baryonic $C\!P$ violation has been observed, its potential to reveal new physics beyond the sensitivity of mesonic decays remains an open question. We investigate this potential within semileptonic $b\to c\ell\bar\nu_\ell$ transitions by comparing the angular distributions of $\Lambda_b\to\Lambda_c(\to\Lambda\pi)\ell^-\bar\nu_\ell$ and $\bar B\to D^*(\to D\pi)\ell^-\bar\nu_\ell$ utilizing lattice-QCD form factors. We perform a global fit to $R(D^{(*)})$ alongside auxiliary $C\!P$-even measurements to delineate the phenomenologically viable parameter space for new-physics Wilson coefficients. Leveraging these allowed ranges and the lattice inputs, we evaluate the complementary sensitivity of both angular distributions to novel weak phases. We demonstrate that the baryonic decay mode probes combinations of Wilson coefficients that remain inaccessible in mesonic angular distributions, thereby providing critical supplementary information for identifying new physics.

</details>


### [130] [Reggeized $K*(892)$ exchange in $π^- p \to K^0 Λ$ and implications for fixed-target measurements](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37109)
*Hao-Nan Wang,Cheng Chen,Yre Ma,Ju-Jun Xie*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the π⁻p → K⁰Λ reaction using Reggeized t-channel K*(892) exchange to model the total cross section across a 2.6–6.0 GeV/c π⁻ beam momentum range, and provides kinematic distributions of decay products (Kₛ⁰→π⁺π⁻, Λ→pπ⁻) for fixed-target experiment design.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a clean channel for studying strangeness production and generating tagged Λ hyperons; to establish a kinematic baseline for future fixed-target experiments measuring this reaction.

Method: Reggeized t-channel K*(892) exchange model (unpolarized) to describe the total cross section; idealized Monte Carlo event generation to compute lab-frame momentum and polar-angle distributions of final-state charged particles from Kₛ⁰ and Λ decays.

Result: Total cross section described over 2.6–6.0 GeV/c π⁻ beam momentum; Kₛ⁰ decay pions are strongly forward-peaked; Λ decay proton and pion populate broader angular ranges and lower momenta—quantified as reference distributions.

Conclusion: The K*(892)-exchange model suffices for describing the integrated cross section in this energy range; the derived kinematic distributions serve as essential input for detector acceptance, tracking efficiency, and analysis strategy development in upcoming fixed-target hyperon experiments.

Abstract: The two-body \(\pi^-p\to K^0\Lambda\) reaction provides a clean channel for studying strangeness production and producing tagged hyperon \(\Lambda\) samples. We describe its total cross section over the $\pi^-$ beam momentum \(2.6 \leq p_{\pi,\lab}\leq6.0~\gev/c\) using a single Reggeized \(t\)-channel \(K^*(892)\) exchange without polarization. We also examine the implications for future fixed-target measurements. An idealized event sample is used to determine the laboratory momentum and polar-angle distributions of the charged particles from \(K_S^0\to\pi^+\pi^-\) and \(\Lambda\to p\pi^-\). The \(K_S^0\) decayed two pions are strongly forward, whereas the proton and pion from the \(\Lambda\) decay extend to larger angles and lower momenta. These distributions provide a kinematic baseline for future acceptance and tracking studies.

</details>


### [131] [Searches for new particle physics by methods of classical and neutrino astronomy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37198)
*Sergey Troitsky*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reviews astrophysical methods—stellar evolution, high-energy photon propagation, and neutrino telescopes—for constraining particle-physics models, illustrating recent applications using red-giant branch stars, GRB 221009A, and Baikal-GVD/IceCube data, with inference performed via forward modeling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To leverage astrophysical observations as natural laboratories for probing beyond-Standard-Model particle physics, where terrestrial experiments face energy or sensitivity limitations.

Method: Forward modeling integrating astrophysical source physics, particle propagation (e.g., photon mixing, neutrino oscillations), detector response, and nuisance parameters to infer particle-physics constraints from observational data.

Result: Constraints on energy-loss channels (e.g., axion-like particles), photon–new-particle mixing, superheavy dark-matter decay lifetimes, magnetic monopole fluxes, and non-standard neutrino interactions are derived from stellar, gamma-ray, and neutrino telescope data.

Conclusion: Astrophysical probes provide complementary, often uniquely sensitive, tests of particle-physics models; robust forward modeling is essential to translate multi-messenger observations into quantitative particle constraints.

Abstract: Selected astrophysical methods for testing particle-physics models are reviewed. Stellar structure and evolution constrain additional channels of energy loss and transport; high-energy photon propagation probes photon mixing with new particles and modifications of electron-positron pair production; large-volume neutrino telescopes are used to search for magnetic monopoles, neutrinos from superheavy dark-matter decay, and non-standard neutrino propagation. Recent applications of these methods are discussed, involving the tip of the red-giant branch in the color-luminosity diagram, gamma-ray burst 221009A, and data from the Baikal Gigaton Volume Detector (Baikal-GVD) and the IceCube experiment. In each case, particle-physics parameters are inferred through a forward model that accounts for the response to new physics, propagation, detection, and nuisance parameters.

</details>


### [132] [Exclusive production of doubly charmed baryon pair at B Factories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37210)
*Xiao Liang,Jun Jiang,Chun-Yuan Li,Meng-Li Liu,Xing-Hua Yang,Zong-Guo Si*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates the exclusive production of doubly charmed baryon pairs Ξ_cc + Ξ̄_cc in e⁺e⁻ annihilation at B factories using NRQCD, finding unfavorable detection prospects for BaBar and Belle II.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the feasibility of observing doubly charmed baryon pair production in e⁺e⁻ collisions at B factories, which is crucial for testing NRQCD factorization and understanding heavy-quark dynamics.

Method: Non-relativistic QCD (NRQCD) factorization framework is employed to compute the exclusive cross section for Ξ_cc + Ξ̄_cc production in e⁺e⁻ annihilation; theoretical uncertainties, differential distributions, and event yields are evaluated for BaBar and Belle II.

Result: Total cross sections are found to be extremely small; event yields are insufficient for observation given integrated luminosities and detection efficiencies of BaBar and Belle II; theoretical uncertainties are systematically quantified.

Conclusion: The exclusive Ξ_cc + Ξ̄_cc production channel is not viable for experimental observation at current or near-future B factories under standard NRQCD assumptions.

Abstract: The exclusive production of doubly charmed baryon pairs $\Xi_{cc}+\bar{\Xi}_{cc}$ via $e^+e^-$ annihilation at B factories are calculated in the framework of non-relativistic QCD (NRQCD). The total cross sections, theoretical uncertainties, differential distributions and event yields for the BaBar and Belle II experiments are systematically discussed. According to our study, the detection prospects for both experiments are unfavorable.

</details>


### [133] [Global Analysis of Nuclear Parton Distribution Functions using Markov Chain Monte Carlo](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37254)
*Nasim Derakhshanian*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This thesis introduces a Bayesian MCMC framework for nuclear parton distribution function (nPDF) determination, revealing non-Gaussian uncertainties and secondary minima—especially in valence quark distributions—and provides the first global single-nucleus Pb PDFs; multi-nuclei analysis shows improved quark constraints but stable gluons, exposing limitations of traditional Hessian (Gaussian) uncertainty estimation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Traditional Hessian-based nPDF uncertainty estimation relies on a local quadratic (Gaussian) approximation of chi-squared, which fails under limited data, strong parameter correlations, asymmetry, flat directions, or multimodality.

Method: Bayesian inference via adaptive Metropolis-Hastings Markov Chain Monte Carlo (MCMC) within the nCTEQ framework; includes both single-nucleus (Pb-only) and multi-nuclei analyses with analytic A-dependence; posterior sampling enables direct exploration of non-Gaussian features.

Result: Reveals pronounced non-Gaussian behavior and secondary minima in valence quark distributions; first global single-nucleus Pb PDFs; multi-nuclei analysis tightens quark constraints and modifies Pb PDFs, while gluon PDFs remain robust; Hessian comparison confirms its inadequacy in nonlinear/multimodal regions.

Conclusion: Bayesian MCMC provides a flexible, robust, and more comprehensive framework for nPDF determination, enabling accurate uncertainty quantification and faithful mapping of complex, multimodal parameter spaces beyond Gaussian approximations.

Abstract: Global analyses of nuclear parton distribution functions (nPDFs) have traditionally used the Hessian method for uncertainty estimation, relying on a local quadratic approximation of the chi-squared function. This Gaussian approximation can be inadequate when experimental constraints are limited and parameter correlations are strong, as it cannot fully capture asymmetric uncertainties, flat directions, or multiple minima. This thesis develops a Bayesian framework for nPDF determination using Markov Chain Monte Carlo (MCMC) techniques within the nCTEQ analysis framework. An adaptive Metropolis-Hastings algorithm enables direct sampling of the posterior distribution, revealing pronounced non-Gaussian behavior and secondary minima, particularly in the valence distributions. The analysis presents the first global single-nucleus determination of lead (Pb) PDFs based exclusively on lead data, complemented by a multi-nuclei analysis incorporating different nuclear targets through a standard analytic dependence on the mass number. Including lighter nuclei significantly constrains quark distributions and changes the extracted lead PDFs, while the gluon distribution remains comparatively stable. A comparison with Hessian-based results highlights the limitations of the Gaussian approximation in regions with strong nonlinearity or multimodality. Overall, this work establishes Bayesian MCMC inference as a flexible framework for nPDF determination, providing a more comprehensive characterization of parameter uncertainties and the underlying parameter space.

</details>


### [134] [Cosmic-string calorimetry with a 220\,PeV neutrino](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37282)
*Bo-Qiang Lu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: KM3NeT detected a 220 PeV neutrino (KM3-230213A); if sourced from cosmic strings, it implies efficient energy conversion via current-carrying cusps, yielding a tight constraint on the product Gμϵₚ ≈ (2.8–4.3)×10⁻¹¹ — competitive with pulsar timing limits — and suggests ~1/3 of cosmic-string energy goes into particle radiation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To interpret the ultra-high-energy neutrino KM3-230213A in terms of cosmic-string physics, leveraging energy conservation and cusp emission models to constrain fundamental string parameters and radiation channels.

Method: Analyzes the neutrino event as a calorimetric probe of cosmic-string network energy loss; uses cusp emission at critical current (unit efficiency) to relate observed neutrino energy flux to the string tension Gμ and particle energy fraction ϵₚ; combines with existing pulsar timing array bounds on gravitational-wave emission.

Result: Derives Gμϵₚ = (2.8–4.3)×10⁻¹¹ for f_ν = 0.1; shows single event sensitivity rivals pulsar timing limits (10⁻¹⁰); infers that ~1/3 of cosmic-string energy must go into particle radiation (neutrinos + others), with future >100 PeV events enabling direct measurement of this share.

Conclusion: KM3-230213A provides a novel, competitive probe of cosmic-string microphysics — particularly current-carrying capability and branching ratio between gravitational and particle radiation — demonstrating that rare UHE neutrinos can tightly constrain topological defect models.

Abstract: KM3NeT has reported KM3-230213A, a $220$\,PeV neutrino with no source identified. If it comes from a cosmic-string network, energy conservation makes the event a calorimeter. The flux measures the power the network has spent, not how it emits, and a cusp at critical current can supply that power at unit efficiency. The event gives $G\mu\epsilon_p=(2.8\text{--}4.3)\times10^{-11}$ for $f_\nu=0.1$. Pulsar timing already reaches $10^{-10}$, so one event competes with the arrays. For the branch that still radiates gravitationally, flux and timing together require a third of the network's energy in particles, a share that further events above $100$\,PeV would measure.

</details>
