### [135] [Nonradial oscillation modes of axion condensed neutron stars in the Cowling approximation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37308)
*Antonio Gómez-Bañón,Pantelis Pnigouras,José A. Pons*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies nonradial (l=2) oscillations of neutron stars containing a light QCD axion condensate, revealing modified fluid mode frequencies, axion-mediated damping, and novel axion-dominated quasinormal modes and bound states with avoided crossings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how finite-density corrections to the axion potential in light QCD axion models affect neutron star dynamics, particularly nonradial oscillations and associated gravitational-wave signatures.

Method: Relativistic Cowling approximation with fully coupled, dynamical fluid and axion perturbations; analysis of quasinormal mode spectrum including f-, p-modes and axion-dominated modes; treatment of axion emission damping and bound-state interactions.

Result: Axion condensate raises f- and p-mode frequencies by up to ~20%, but dynamical coupling nearly cancels the shift for the f-mode; p-modes above axion mass are damped via axion emission on timescales as short as ~10 ms; f-mode stays below threshold; new strongly damped axion-dominated modes (~kHz imaginary part) and gravitationally bound axion states appear; avoided crossings occur between bound p-modes and axion levels below propagation threshold.

Conclusion: Axion condensates in neutron stars induce rich spectral features—frequency shifts, mode-dependent damping, and hybridized fluid–axion quasinormal modes—offering potential observational probes of light axions through asteroseismology and gravitational-wave astronomy.

Abstract: In light QCD axion models, finite-density corrections to the axion potential can source a nonvanishing axion field inside neutron stars. Extending our previous analysis of radial oscillations, we study the nonradial ($l=2$) oscillation spectrum of such axion condensed neutron stars in the relativistic Cowling approximation, keeping the fluid and axion perturbations fully coupled and dynamical. The condensate modifies the fluid spectrum in two ways: the altered equilibrium structure raises the $f$- and $p$-mode frequencies by up to $\sim20\%$, while the dynamical coupling to the axion field partially compensates this shift, almost entirely for the $f$-mode. Fluid modes with frequencies above the axion mass acquire a new damping channel through axion emission. The $f$-mode remains below this threshold for all configurations supporting a condensate, whereas the $p$-modes are damped on timescales that can be as short as $\sim10$ ms, shorter than their gravitational-wave damping times, except for certain axion masses where the outgoing axion amplitude nearly vanishes. The spectrum also contains a family of strongly damped axion-dominated quasinormal modes, with imaginary parts of order kHz, and a sequence of gravitationally bound axion states analogous to gravitational atom levels. Below the propagation threshold, the bound $p$-modes undergo avoided crossings with these levels, exchanging their fluid- and axion-dominated character.

</details>


### [136] [Color-Reconnection Effects on the nMPI-Nch Conditional Multiplicity Fluctuations in PYTHIA 8 pp Collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37511)
*Subir Sarkar,Kalyan Dey*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates conditional charged-multiplicity fluctuations at fixed multiparton interaction (MPI) count in PYTHIA proton-proton simulations across 2.76–13 TeV, finding consistent patterns—smaller absolute width but larger coefficient of variation for CR-ON vs. CR-OFF—across multiple tunes (CP5, Monash, A14), though causality of colour reconnection remains unverified due to uncontrolled effective lifetime/decay settings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To isolate and assess the impact of colour reconnection (CR) on charged-multiplicity fluctuations conditioned on MPI count, disentangling it from other model dependencies in high-energy pp collisions.

Method: Analysis of retained PYTHIA samples (historical CR-baseline, CMS CP5, Monash, A14) at √s = 2.76, 7, and 13 TeV; charged particles selected with |η| < 0.8 and 0.15 < pT < 20 GeV/c; comparison of conditional mean and standard deviation (and coefficient of variation) of multiplicity per MPI bin; cross-tune validation and comparison with ALICE ⟨pT⟩ data and simulated activity estimators.

Result: In all tested tunes, CR-ON yields smaller absolute width (std dev) but larger relative width (coefficient of variation) than CR-OFF at fixed MPI count; this ordering is robust across energies and tunes, yet no causal attribution to CR alone is established due to unverified effective lifetime/decay parameter correspondence in baseline samples.

Conclusion: The observed conditional fluctuation pattern—reduced absolute width but enhanced relative dispersion under CR—is reproducible across modern PYTHIA tunes and energies, suggesting a structural feature of CR modeling; however, without strict control of decay/lifetime parameters, CR cannot be uniquely identified as the sole driver.

Abstract: We examine conditional charged-multiplicity fluctuations at fixed multiparton interaction (MPI) count in retained PYTHIA proton-proton samples at centre-of-mass energies of 2.76, 7, and 13 TeV. Charged particles satisfy |eta| < 0.8 and 0.15 < pT < 20 GeV/c. In the historical colour-reconnection (CR)-labelled baseline samples, the CR-ON conditional mean is smaller by a larger fraction than the standard deviation: the absolute width is smaller, while the coefficient of variation is larger in every retained matched MPI bin. Effective lifetime/decay settings and their per-run correspondence are unverified for this baseline, so these comparisons do not establish that CR alone causes the observed differences or their energy dependence. A separately documented 13 TeV comparison using the Compact Muon Solenoid (CMS) CP5 tune, with equal non-CR physics settings and particle data across the ON/OFF pair, exhibits the same absolute-versus-relative width ordering. Retained independent Monash and A14 samples also show this pattern, subject to historical production-record limitations. A comparison with mean-transverse-momentum data from A Large Ion Collider Experiment (ALICE) and simulated activity estimators provides final-state context within the stated particle-definition and provenance limits.

</details>


### [137] [Electron-to-Muon Forward Annihilation at High Energy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37531)
*Maximilian Delto,Alexander A. Penin,Lorenzo Tancredi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives the all-order asymptotic behavior of the electron-to-muon annihilation amplitude in forward QED at high energy using effective field theory and renormalization group methods, up to next-to-leading logarithmic accuracy, and computes one- and two-loop fixed-order corrections including power-suppressed terms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a complete technical exposition—including omitted derivations and details—of the high-energy forward annihilation amplitude in two-flavor QED, complementing the concise presentation in arXiv:2510.04914.

Method: Effective field theory (EFT) framework combined with renormalization group evolution; dimensional regularization and minimal subtraction; explicit computation of one- and two-loop Feynman diagrams in the high-energy (s ≫ m_e, m_μ) forward limit, retaining subleading power corrections.

Result: All-order resummation of large logarithms in the forward e⁻μ⁺ → e⁻μ⁺ amplitude at next-to-leading logarithmic (NLL) accuracy; analytic expressions for O(α) and O(α²) fixed-order amplitudes including ℳ ∼ 1/s × [log²(s/m²), log(s/m²), constant, m²/s] terms.

Conclusion: The high-energy behavior is systematically controlled by EFT matching and RG evolution; fixed-order results validate the resummation and quantify power corrections, confirming consistency between NLL resummation and perturbative expansion up to two loops.

Abstract: We study the high-energy behavior of electron-to-muon annihilation in forward kinematics in QED with two massive lepton species. Using effective field theory renormalization group techniques we derive the all-order asymptotic behavior of the annihilation amplitude through the next-to-leading logarithmic approximation. Furthermore, we consider one- and two-loop fixed-order corrections to the forward-annihilation amplitude in the high-energy limit, which we compute up to power corrections. The goal of this paper is to elaborate on various technical details that we omitted in the original presentation of our results in arXiv:2510.04914.

</details>


### [138] [On-shell Mass Renormalisation and RG-improved Effective Potential in Vector-like Lepton Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37541)
*Gregor Daberstiel,Kilian Möhling,Dominik Stöckinger,Hyejung Stöckinger-Kim*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops an on-shell mass renormalisation scheme for six vector-like lepton (VLL) extensions of the Standard Model, enabling efficient computation of observables and low-scale MS-bar parameters while studying vacuum stability via 2-loop RG equations and the RG-improved effective potential.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To absorb large radiative corrections to SM lepton masses induced by SM–VLL mixing (up to 5×5 matrices) and enable reliable low-energy predictions and vacuum stability analysis in VLL-extended models.

Method: On-shell mass renormalisation; derivation and solution of redundancy-free 2-loop renormalisation-group equations; computation of RG-improved effective potential; analysis of vacuum instability scales.

Result: Identification of rich phase structure across VLL models; vacuum instability scales as low as 10^5 GeV; parameter regions where one-loop corrections to input parameters stabilise the electroweak vacuum.

Conclusion: The proposed on-shell scheme is essential for robust phenomenological analysis of VLL models, revealing significant sensitivity of vacuum stability to loop-level corrections and mixing-induced threshold effects.

Abstract: We discuss the on-shell mass renormalisation in a class of six vector-like lepton (VLL) extensions of the Standard Model with seesaw-like contributions to the SM lepton masses. The schemes absorb large radiative corrections to the SM lepton masses arising from mixing between the SM and VLLs with up to $5\times5$ mixing matrices and allows for efficient computation of observables and the low-scale $\overline{\text{MS}}$ values of model parameters. We determine and solve the redundancy-free 2-loop renormalisation-group (RG) equations, compute the RG-improved effective potential and study the vacuum instability scales of each of the VLL models. We find rich phase behaviour and parameter regions that show instability scales as low as $10^5$ GeV as well as regions where the one-loop corrections to the input parameters can lead to a stable vacuum.

</details>


### [139] [Using Higgs Boson Production in Association with Three Jets via Gluon Fusion at the LHC to Investigate the CP Properties of the Higgs Boson](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37627)
*Arunima Bhattacharya,Francisco Campanario,Michael Kubocz,Terrance Figy,Tunde Kushimo,Ivan Rosario*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the full mass-dependent cross section for neutral Higgs boson (Φ) production in association with three jets (pp → Φjjj) at O(αₛ⁵), including coherent top- and bottom-quark loop contributions, all partonic channels, and CP-mixed couplings; it benchmarks against effective theories, identifies kinematic regions where finite-mass effects matter (e.g., high-pₜ, dijet mass), reveals non-monotonic tanβ dependence in 2HDM, and introduces a novel CP-sensitive azimuthal correlation Φ₂ with a sin2Φ₂ term arising only from CP interference.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To go beyond the heavy-top effective theory and capture finite-mass effects—especially bottom-quark contributions and CP interference—in Higgs+3-jet production, which is crucial for precision Higgs physics and CP property determination at the LHC.

Method: Full five-flavor QCD calculation at O(αₛ⁵) for pp → Φjjj, retaining exact top- and bottom-quark mass dependence in gluon-fusion loops; includes all partonic subprocesses (gg, gq, qq̄); treats scalar and pseudoscalar couplings coherently to preserve CP interference; computes differential distributions (pₜ, dijet mass, z*, Δy, Φ₂); compares with heavy-top effective theory and form-factor-improved variants.

Result: Bottom-quark loops contribute ~50% of pseudoscalar Φ+3j cross section at tanβ=1; cross section becomes non-monotonic in tanβ in Type-II 2HDM due to bottom dominance at large tanβ; effective theories fail in high-pₜ and high-dijet-mass tails but agree well for z* and Δy; Φ₂ distribution exhibits CP-sensitive distortion: sin2Φ₂ term appears only for CP-mixed states via interference, isolable via a counting asymmetry.

Conclusion: Finite-mass bottom-quark effects and CP interference are essential for accurate theoretical predictions of Φ+3j production; the azimuthal observable Φ₂ provides a clean, experimentally accessible probe of Higgs CP properties beyond simple rate measurements, especially at the LHC.

Abstract: A neutral Higgs boson $\Phi$ with general scalar and pseudoscalar couplings and accompanying jets can be efficiently produced via gluon fusion through heavy-quark loops in high-energy hadronic collisions. We present integrated cross sections and kinematic distributions for the production of a generic Higgs boson $\Phi$ in association with three jets, $pp \to \Phi jjj$, at order $\alpha_{s}^{5}$. We include the full mass dependence of the top- and bottom-quark loop contributions, combined coherently so that their interference is retained. Earlier finite-mass calculations of $\Phi+3$ jets with bottom-quark loops and $CP$-odd or $CP$-mixed couplings were restricted to $gg \to ggg\Phi$; we include the complete set of partonic subprocesses, and find that the quark-containing channels supply close to one half of the pseudoscalar three-jet cross section at $\tan\beta=1$. In a Type-II two-Higgs-doublet model the bottom-quark loop comes to dominate the rate as $\tan\beta$ is increased, so that the cross section is non-monotonic in $\tan\beta$. Comparing against the heavy-top effective theory and its form-factor-improved version, we show that the effective description fails in the hard tails of the transverse-momentum and dijet-mass distributions, while the third-jet centrality $z^\star$ and the rapidity separation of the two leading jets are largely insensitive to the heavy-quark treatment. We further study the multi-jet azimuthal correlation $\Phi_{2}$, built from the jet momenta on the two sides of the Higgs boson in rapidity, and find that the bottom-quark contribution modifies its shape even for a state of definite $CP$. For a $CP$-mixed state the interference of the two couplings adds a $\sin2\Phi_2$ term, which a simple counting asymmetry isolates and which no $CP$ eigenstate can produce. Numerical results are presented for the LHC at $\sqrt{s}=13.6~$TeV and $m_\Phi=125.2~$GeV.

</details>
